# Contactcenter-Management-App (CMA)

The Contactcenter-Management-App (CMA) is Pulse4all's own application for running its contact center on top of a **CRM plus a telephony tool** (today HubSpot and Aircall). Those tools hold the customer and the call, but they don't manage the people doing the work or make them fast: who is clocked in, who covers which skill, who gets the next lead, what the agent should do next, how productive the team is. The CMA is that missing layer for Pulse4all: the workforce, productivity and management layer on top of CRM plus phone. Agents log in to start their day and work from one directive screen that serves them the next task; managers plan and steer their team; leads are routed to the right available agent within seconds.

**For whom:** Pulse4all's own business lines. Built first for Pulse4all Subscriptions (AED subscriptions, HubSpot + Aircall), then configured for Pulse4all Invest, and for Pulse4all US if and when that business line starts. We develop the CMA as standalone technology: its own code, database and screens, connected to the CRM and telephony through adapters rather than built inside HubSpot (see Why standalone). Agents and managers use it at **workspace.pulse4all.app**.

**Setup in one line:** front = the Workspace; backend = PostgreSQL on Cloud SQL, the CRM, Aircall, Make and n8n, all on Cloud Run in the EU.

**Where this goes:** one point where everything about the operation comes together in the Workspace: the agent's next task, the team's day, and reporting for every management layer built from all sources (CRM, telephony, Shopify, NetSuite, email, SMS, chat, the Customer App, the Asset Management Database) joined to the staff data only the CMA has. The version in which all sources are connected is the convergence version; its number is set when the roadmap beyond step 10 is cut (see Roadmap, Convergence).

**Team:** Martin, Joshua, Finn
**Key stakeholders:** Arno (call center manager), Kira, Peter, Bas (dashboards and reporting), Yordi (compliance and data protection)

---

## How we work

- **Open to the team.** This README is the shared record of scope, decisions and status, kept in the repository next to the code (`Pulse4all-com/cma`). Dev and prod in Google Cloud are visible to everyone in the build group `cma@pulse4all.com`, and every change in prod is traceable in the audit log and in `records/`.
- **Decisions during the V1 build.** To get a first working version in place without long debates up front, Martin sets the direction and takes the build decisions during V1. Each one is written in the Decision log with its reason, so anyone can see what was decided and why, and anyone in the team can question or reopen it. Decisions that touch someone's own area are confirmed with them: KPI targets, result codes and cohorts with Kira, the role ladder and rosters with Arno, Invest with Peter, data protection and labour rules with Yordi, the final name with Mark (see Open decisions).
- **Google-native audits.** The Gem "CMA Google Cloud Auditor" in Pulse4all's Gemini audits the Google Cloud and Google Workspace setup (IAM, Cloud SQL, Cloud Run, IAP, Cloud Build, secrets, logging, BigQuery, cost) as a second opinion. It advises and never changes anything; its knowledge is this README, re-uploaded after every merge that changes it. Its answers go into the Google Doc "CMA - Gemini audit findings" (shared within pulse4all.com). Each finding is checked against this README and becomes a pull request, a console step, or a Decision log entry with the reason it was rejected. Live audits in the Console (Gemini Cloud Assist) run from an account with viewer roles only, because Cloud Assist acts with the asking user's permissions.

---

## What we are building, in plain words

**In one sentence:** "We're building our own operating system for the Pulse4all contact center: it hands every agent their next call, keeps the clock, and shows management who's productive and which customers stay." Shorter still: air traffic control for our call center. Technically it is a workforce, productivity and management layer on top of CRM plus telephony, built as standalone technology. Contactcenter-Management-App is a working title; the final name is chosen with Mark.

**Why we believe it works** (assessment of 6 October 2026, to be revisited with the business case below):

- **Technically doable.** Nothing in it is exotic: Postgres with row-level security, Cloud Run, Next.js, webhooks, a work queue on `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, an outbox. The AgentUI trial proved the hardest algorithmic piece and closed its concurrency gaps; the demo proved the screens. The difficulty is elsewhere: the two-way sync with HubSpot (rate limits, idempotency, conflicts), upstream data quality (the Data first list is longer than the code), the long tail of sources in the convergence version, and the cost of keeping everything configurable while building for Subscriptions first. With three people and AI-assisted development the control layer is weeks, the desk is months, convergence a year or more. The risk is scope, not feasibility.
- **Value and ROI.** Positive internally if two things hold, both measured from day one (see Business case): the desk gets adopted and lifts talk time per paid hour, and speed to lead plus kept callbacks turn into conversions that leak today. The management layer alone has softer returns: correct hours per employer, fewer disputes, compliance, steering for Kira and Peter. Cloud cost is modest; the team's time is the real cost.
- **AI agents will take a growing share of the calls; the need stands.** Hand-entered dispositions and scripted screens will fade as AI fills forms and infers outcomes from transcripts. What stays is the layer underneath: the system of record for work (who did what, when, for which customer, with what outcome), the queue that serves tasks, the reporting that joins outcomes to time and cost, and the compliance trail that AFM, FCA and GDPR demand from humans and machines alike. A work queue does not care whether the worker is a person or an AI agent; `app_user` gets a worker kind when the first AI agent works a queue, and attribution and reporting carry over unchanged. Build API-first (Open principle) so AI tools consume the CMA rather than replace it.
- **Why build rather than buy or stretch HubSpot.** HubSpot and Aircall focus on the CRM and the telephony, not on running the team, and the join of channel events with clock, roster and employer is outside both (see Why standalone). Workforce-management tools that integrate with Aircall do exist; a comparison with them is in Open decisions, so the choice to build stays checked. What makes the CMA valuable to Pulse4all is the operational knowledge and data it captures, not the code itself.

---

## Guiding principles

1. **Configure, don't hardcode.** Nothing specific to a business line, to HubSpot or to Aircall is hardcoded. Markets, teams, skills, channels, statuses, campaigns, result codes, rules and thresholds are tenant configuration; the CRM and the telephony tool are adapters per tenant; system names appear only as data values. We build for Subscriptions first and add another adapter *type* only when a business line moves to another CRM or telephony tool, not before. Being CRM-agnostic is not a future option but a V1 fact: Pulse4all itself runs a separate HubSpot portal and Aircall account per business line (Subs, Invest, later US), so every adapter is configured per tenant with its own credentials, ids and mappings from the first increment.
2. **Open.** The data layer is accessible to other applications through an API, so others can read from it, write to it and build on top of it.
3. **Right the first time.** Multi-tenancy (data strictly separated per business line), roles and permissions, and a change history are part of the foundation from the first table, not added later.
4. **Step by step, straight to production.** Build one working piece in dev, verify it, promote it to production, then start the next piece in dev. Production runs from the first increment; we never build the whole platform first. Always take the clean choice over the quick shortcut.
5. **Use the right tool for each job.** Don't force one system to do everything.
6. **Configurable by default (development rule, 6 October 2026).** Every feature is built so a Pulse4all business line with its own CRM portal and telephony account can start from configuration alone, without code changes. Business-line specifics are tenant configuration and seed data, never code; anything vendor-specific sits behind an adapter interface.
7. **Postgres is the operational core.** Customer facts are owned by the CRM; the work itself (tasks, time, rosters, messages, outcomes, productivity) lives in Postgres on Cloud SQL. We never squeeze the operation into the CRM, and the CRM is never a dependency for an agent to keep working.

---

## Why standalone and not inside the CRM

HubSpot stays our CRM and the source of truth for contacts, leads, deals, tickets and owners. The CMA is deliberately built **outside** HubSpot, on its own foundation. We looked at building it inside HubSpot and decided against it for these reasons:

1. **This is not CRM work.** A time clock that starts on login, work statuses, rosters with skill coverage, automatic logout on the scheduled end time, realtime messages to agents and gamification are workforce and call center functions. HubSpot is not designed for them. Building them inside HubSpot means forcing them into custom objects and workarounds that stay fragile and limited.
2. **Live, all-day use needs a fast database.** Every agent keeps the CMA open all day, and managers watch live views. Running that directly on HubSpot hits API rate limits and slows down. Postgres is built for many concurrent reads and writes and handles hundreds of thousands of rows without effort.
3. **We combine more than HubSpot.** Aircall calls and recordings, the shared Gmail mailbox and later Shopify, the customer portal and NetSuite all feed productivity and steering. A neutral data layer brings these together; HubSpot would only see its own part.
4. **We own the data and the logic.** Time registration affects pay and labour rules and needs a complete, reliable history under our control. Our own Postgres plus BigQuery gives us that, plus open APIs for other tools to build on.
5. **Reusable across business lines.** Built once and configured per tenant, the same CMA runs for Subscriptions, for Invest and later for Pulse4all US, without rebuilding it for each.
6. **Cost and flexibility.** The HubSpot features needed to stretch this far (custom objects at scale, advanced developer features) sit in the most expensive tiers, and we would still be limited by what HubSpot allows. A custom build costs effort up front but removes that ceiling.
7. **A directive work queue needs its own engine.** Agent productivity dropped when Pulse4all moved from Steam Connect to HubSpot with the Aircall plugin: agents choose their next task from lists instead of being served it. HubSpot's task queues are owner-based, have no leasing and cannot share a pool between agents safely. The CMA's queue engine serves the next task, leases it, retries it, closes it at the ceiling and never forgets it; HubSpot receives the outcome (decided 6 October 2026).
8. **Several CRM instances, one operation.** Pulse4all Subs and Pulse4all Invest each run their own HubSpot portal and their own Aircall account, and Pulse4all US will most likely do the same. No portal can see another portal. The one place where the whole operation comes together, people, hours, work and outcomes across business lines, has to sit outside all of them. That is the CMA: one application, one database for Pulse4all, one adapter instance per tenant, aggregation on top (decided 6 October 2026).

HubSpot remains fully part of the flow: contacts, deals and tickets live there, outcomes and field changes are written back there, and a HubSpot app card can serve as a shortcut to the CMA. Agents either do their customer work in HubSpot and Aircall, or, once the agent desk exists, in the CMA with the record read from HubSpot. If a business line ever moves to another CRM, the same holds: that is a new adapter, not a new application.

---

## Business lines, databases and tenants

Pulse4all's business lines are tenants in one CMA database per environment (dev and prod).

| Level | What it is | Separation | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| Database | the CMA's data for Pulse4all, one per environment | its own Cloud SQL instance, logins, secrets and readers | `cma` on `cma-prod-pg` |
| Tenant | one business line | `tenant_id` and row-level security inside the database | Pulse4all Subscriptions, Pulse4all Invest, later Pulse4all US |

- Pulse4all Subscriptions and Pulse4all Invest are two tenants in **one** database; Pulse4all US is the expected third. Each tenant has its **own CRM and telephony instance** (its own HubSpot portal and Aircall account), connected through its own adapter instance with its own credentials, external ids and property and pipeline mappings. A person who works for two business lines is two users with two sets of external ids.
- **Aggregation across tenants** happens in `cma_read` and BigQuery (readers see all business lines by default) and, for management views in the Workspace, through reporting views across business lines; the operational tables and the agent desk stay strictly per tenant. Matching the same customer across portals (a Subs customer who is also an investor) is a purpose-limitation question for Yordi before it is built.
- **Regions.** The EU-only policy on the folder means a US tenant's data would live in europe-west4 as well; whether Pulse4all US needs a US region (and therefore its own database and project) is decided when US is set up.
- If a business line ever gets its own database (a US region, for example), the same migrations run there unchanged; until then it is one database per environment and a checklist.

---

## Architecture

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Workspace (Cloud Run, EU)                                         │
│  Agent desk: next task · record · softphone · one-press outcome    │
│  Management: Live · Report · Data (roster, team, configuration)    │
└──────────────┬────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────┘
               │                                │
               ▼                                ▼
┌──────────────────────────────┐   ┌────────────────────────────────┐
│  Postgres (Cloud SQL, EU)    │   │  Realtime push service         │
│  one database for Pulse4all  │   │  (push to agents, chat)        │
│  cma: operational tables     │   └────────────────────────────────┘
│   time · roster · skills ·   │   ┌────────────────────────────────┐
│   queue · attempts · messages│   │  Scheduler / worker            │
│   · mirror of CRM and calls  │   │  (lease expiry, lead expiry,   │
│  cma_read: reporting views   │   │   auto-close, period close)    │
└───────┬───────────┬──────────┘   └────────────────────────────────┘
        │           │              ┌────────────────────────────────┐
        │           └──► cma_read ─┤  BigQuery (analytics)          │
        │                          │  NocoDB (read-only)            │
        ▼                          └────────────────────────────────┘
   Ingest API (Cloud Run): canonical entities in
   Outbox worker (Cloud Run): actions out, one row per action
        ▲                 │
        │                 ▼
   Adapters per tenant: CRM · telephony  (Make and n8n scenarios today)
        ▲                 │
        │                 ▼
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  SOURCES AND TARGETS: CRM (HubSpot) · telephony (Aircall)          │
│  · Gmail (team@pulse4all.com) · SMS · chat · Customer App          │
│  later: Shopify · NetSuite · Asset Management Database             │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Layers**

- **Sources and targets.** Per tenant a CRM and a telephony tool, plus every other channel and data source that feeds the operation and its reporting. For Pulse4all: HubSpot (CRM: contacts, leads, deals, tickets, owners) and Aircall (calls, recordings, phone numbers) are the starting systems; the shared Gmail mailbox, SMS, chat and the **Customer App** (self-service: orders, address and contract changes, service requests, in-app messages) are channels, each a row in `channel`; Shopify and Juo (orders, subscriptions), NetSuite (invoices, payments) and the **Asset Management Database** (devices, installations, service status, battery and pad expiry, replacements) are data sources that feed cohort attributes and generate work for agents. The CRM is also a target: outcomes, field changes and owner assignments are written back to it. Sources are connected one by one along the roadmap; the model is built for all of them from the start.
- **Adapters and the canonical model.** Postgres stores canonical entities (lead, deal, call, ticket, owner, contact reference) with a fixed shape plus `source_system`, `source_id`, `synced_at` and the original payload in `raw`. An adapter maps one source system into that shape and executes the actions the CMA issues from its outbox. An adapter runs as one *instance per tenant*, holding that tenant's credentials, portal or account id, webhook secrets and field, pipeline and owner mappings: Pulse4all's three HubSpot portals are three instances of the one HubSpot adapter, its three Aircall accounts three instances of the Aircall adapter. A second CRM is a second adapter type with the same interface. The CRM adapter interface is small and fixed: resolve a phone number to a record, read a record, write a field diff, write an engagement with a result code, assign an owner, receive new tasks. The telephony adapter: embed the softphone, start a call, receive call events and recordings, read and set the agent's availability. Every source also maps into one more canonical entity, the **activity event** (source system, source id, channel, actor resolved through `app_user_external_id`, `occurred_at`, outcome reference, customer and cohort references): calls, dispositions, emails sent, chats and SMS handled, tickets closed, app interactions and lead assignments are all rows of this shape. It is the universal join between what happened in a channel and who was on the clock when it happened (see Features 5). HubSpot and Aircall are the first implementations; today the adapters are Make scenarios, n8n on Cloud Run EU is the second automation runtime; a second CRM is a second mapping, not a schema change. The ingest API contract is written against the canonical entities, never against a vendor payload.
- **Ingest API (Cloud Run).** The only write path from outside Google Cloud. Since 10 October 2026 HubSpot's webhooks arrive directly at the ingest service on its adapter's route, and jobs sweep, poll forms, reconcile and backfill. Aircall's route is built (brief B9, 13 October 2026): `POST /aircall/<key>` with the webhook's token in the body compared in constant time with the connection's signing secret, one event per request recorded as `ac:<event>:<call id>:<timestamp>`, the call read back with `GET /v1/calls/{id}` into the telephony tables of 0007a (the other party's number as the keyed hash only), lines and tags at most hourly, reconcile with a nightly webhook check and backfill (`--object telephony_call`), the account pinned by the company name, the adapter's own pace (`aircall_max_per_minute`, default 60), dev replay only where `INGEST_ALLOW_REPLAY=1`, and the scripts `aircall-webhook.mjs` and `aircall-users.mjs` (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Aircall; prod steps in `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` phase 8). Shopify's route is built (brief B10, 13 October 2026): `POST /shopify/<key>` with `X-Shopify-Hmac-Sha256` over the raw body checked against the app's client secret in constant time, the shop pinned by `X-Shopify-Shop-Domain`, one event per delivery recorded as `sh:<X-Shopify-Webhook-Id>`, the customer or order read back through the Admin GraphQL API (version `2026-10` by default, the connection setting `api_version`) with a token of the client credentials grant cached per shop, within the route's own 1.5-second budget (`INGEST_SHOPIFY_READBACK_BUDGET_MS`; Shopify waits five seconds and deletes a subscription after eight failed deliveries), into the commerce tables of 0007b with the prior order count and the keyed phone hash of 0008g; every customer matched to its CRM contact (the CMA's own link, checked by one batch read, or held refs, else the email in memory, else the contacts' Shopify id properties) and the match kept in the CMA (addition 0008h, B10.1: nothing is written back to HubSpot, Decision log, 13 October 2026); a cost bucket per shop paces the reads, so a renewal run's burst is answered at once and read by the sweep; reconcile with the nightly subscription check and backfill (`--object commerce_customer|commerce_order`), and `shopify-subscribe.mjs` (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Shopify; prod steps in `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` phase 9). Make, n8n and later other tools call it over HTTPS with a key per tenant for other sources; the key identifies the tenant. It writes to Postgres through the Cloud SQL connector under its own service account. The service is `cma-ingest` in `ingest/` (brief B7, 10 October 2026): `GET /health` and `POST /<adapter>/<connection key>`, where the key gives the connection (`cma.ingest_connection`) and a connection answers only on its own adapter's route; the adapter authenticates the request before anything else touches the database, the events are recorded and committed (ids only), the objects are read back from the source within a 3-second budget and upserted, then 200, and what the read-back could not finish is left failed for the sweeper. The HubSpot adapter is the first (v3 signature, account pinning, CRM batch reads of the configured properties only, the keyed hash of call numbers); Aircall (B9) and Shopify (B10) plug into the same core. The jobs (brief B8, 11 October 2026) are Cloud Run jobs on the same image (`node jobs/<name>.mjs`, running as `cma-ingest@<project>`), started by Cloud Scheduler in Europe/Amsterdam through their run endpoints as `cma-ingest-scheduler@<project>`, which holds `run.invoker` on the scheduled jobs only: `ingest-sweep` every minute (due events claimed and read back until none is due, so an event that failed waits for its backoff and is parked as `needs_review` after `ingest.max_attempts`; records still without a contact in their first hour read again at most every ten minutes; `cma.link_calls(now() − 2 h)` per tenant; the outbox guard since 13 October 2026: outbox actions of active connections still waiting ten minutes after they fell due are counted and logged as a warning, and Reports → Data quality shows the count, because `ingest-outbox` has no schedule), `ingest-poll-forms` every minute (the HubSpot form catalog hourly, every counted form's submissions newest first until its cursor or `intake.start_date`, the contact found by a CRM search on the form definition's email field with the email in memory only, page host and path with the UTM parameters, kept fields only), `ingest-reconcile` at 02:30 (everything modified in the last 26 hours per object type, and Shopify's subscriptions checked and recreated; `--lookback-minutes` shortens it, so the polling fallback NIGHT_NOTES D3 is a schedule change), `ingest-outbox` without a schedule (B10's worker for the CRM contact write-backs of 0007c; generic and unused since 0008h, so `deploy.sh` schedules nothing and pauses an earlier schedule) and `ingest-backfill` without a schedule (`--connection`, `--object deal|ticket|crm_call|form_submission|contact|telephony_call|commerce_customer|commerce_order`, `--from`, default `intake.start_date`; paced under half of HubSpot's documented limits, Aircall's at the connection's `aircall_max_per_minute`, Shopify's by the shop's cost bucket, and resumable from `sync_cursor`). Reconcile, backfill and the second read turn what they find into synthetic events (`sync:<type>:<id>:<modified time>`) on the webhook's own read-back and upserts, so a job writes nothing a webhook would not, a source's 429 leaves the events to the sweep, and an unchanged object is read once; every run is a `sync_run` row (the sweep's own audit is the event log; its link step has a `link_calls` row). Deployed per project by `docs/ingest/deploy.sh`, the jobs and schedules with `--jobs` (setup and checks in `docs/ingest/README.md`). In prod the service is reached as `ingest.pulse4all.app` on the web load balancer (12 October 2026): backend `cma-ingest-backend` without IAP on a serverless NEG, ingress `internal-and-cloud-load-balancing` so the `run.app` address refuses everyone, Cloud Armor policy `cma-ingest-armor` with rate-limit rules per client IP (below), certificate `cma-ingest-cert` next to `cma-web-cert` on `cma-web-proxy`, a host rule on `cma-web-urlmap`; built by `docs/ingest/front.sh`, with `deploy.sh prod --front ingest.pulse4all.app` putting the public host first in `INGEST_PUBLIC_URLS`, and the prod sequence in `docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md`. The service's caps (13 October 2026): min 1 and max 5 instances in prod, min 0 and max 2 in dev, concurrency 20, a database pool of 5 per instance and 3 per job, and startup CPU boost; Cloud Armor throttles each client IP at 2,400 requests per minute on `/shopify/` (rule 900, evaluated first) and 600 per minute on every other request (rule 1000), answers 429 beyond, and those 429s, which never reach the Cloud Run request log, raise the alert policy `cma-ingest 429` (`docs/ingest/alert.sh`). Dev keeps its `run.app` address. No ingest route takes personal data in its path or query string: the path holds the adapter and the connection key (an identifier, not a secret), secrets travel in headers or signatures, data in the body, because Cloud Run's request log keeps the URL (`CLAUDE.md`; Decision log, 12 October 2026). The database is never opened to external IP addresses. Decided 3 October 2026, over a direct Make connection, because Make's outbound IPs are shared with all Make customers in a zone; the same rule applies to n8n (6 October 2026).
- **Outbox worker.** Every action towards another system (write a field diff or an engagement to the CRM, assign an owner, send a product email, push a message) is a row written in the same transaction as the fact that caused it, then delivered by a worker with claim, in-flight, sent and failed states, retries and a `needs_review` parking state that needs a named person. Nothing in an agent's render path waits on an integration; the agent only ever sees a failed dial. Ported from the AgentUI trial's design. It lands with migration 0007c, the first action being the CRM contact write-back (Decision log, 10 October 2026; written the same day, see Data model: outbox and CRM contact write-back). The worker came with the Shopify adapter (brief B10, 13 October 2026): the Cloud Run job `ingest-outbox`, every minute, claims 50 actions per CRM connection, reads the contacts' current values, applies `always`, `if_empty` and `slot` and writes the difference in one batch update, and refuses in code any property the connection's write-back fields do not name (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Outbox). Since B10.1 (13 October 2026, addition 0008h) it carries nothing: the CMA writes nothing back to HubSpot for Shopify (the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify contact fields; one writer per property), so the job has no schedule and stays, generic, for the next action that needs it (0009's owner write-back is planned on it); `verify/outbox-flow.mjs` proves it on its own.
- **Postgres on Google Cloud SQL (europe-west4).** The stable, fast foundation, one database for Pulse4all's business lines, in two schemas. `cma` holds the operational tables: tenants, people, roles, teams, skills, channels, statuses, time entries, rosters, messages, the work queue (campaigns, tasks, leases, attempts, callbacks, result codes), assignments, the mirror of CRM and call data, each with its change history. `cma_read` holds read-only reporting views and is the only thing readers ever see. Chosen over Airtable for volume (500k+ rows), stability and concurrent access from multiple points.
- **Mirror of customer data (6 October 2026).** Postgres holds a mirror of the customer fields the Workspace needs to serve a task: the CRM object ids, phone numbers in E.164 with an index, email, owner and the fields a campaign's field blocks declare. Nothing is mirrored because it might be useful later. The mirror is filled by the sync, read on open, and updated from the CRM's answer to a write, never from the agent's keystroke; a record deleted or anonymised in the CRM leaves the mirror through the sync. Which objects and fields are mirrored is tenant configuration behind the adapter. The mirror arrives with Sync (Roadmap step 3) and serves the screen pop and call matching before the agent desk exists.
- **Readers.** BigQuery (analytics, federated connection in europe-west4 or the EU multi-region, one connection per database) and NocoDB (read-only table browser for the team) read `cma_read` through their own login users, never the base tables, so tables can change without breaking either and every exposed column is a deliberate choice. NocoDB is self-hosted on Cloud Run (service `cma-nocodb`, europe-west4) per project: it reads `cma_read` through the IAM database user of its own service account (`cma-nocodb@<project>.iam`, member of `cma_readonly`) via a Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar, keeps its metadata in its own database `nocodb` on the same instance, and sits behind IAP on the service for `cma@pulse4all.com`; NocoDB reads `cma_read` live through Connect External Data, with edit and schema change switched off; NocoDB Cloud is not used (Decision log, 9 October 2026; setup in `docs/nocodb/README.md`).
- **Realtime push service.** A separate, specialised service for pushing messages to agents in the open CMA, later also chat. Postgres stays the record of every message; the service only delivers (see Messaging). Kept outside Cloud SQL on purpose; choice of service still open. A 10-second poll on the open screen is the fallback for the first increment.
- **Scheduler / worker.** Time-based work: lease expiry, lead expiry after the accept window, automatic closing of forgotten workdays, period closing, later reforecasts. Cloud Scheduler with a Cloud Run job, or pg_cron on Cloud SQL; choice open.
- **Workspace (the CMA application).** The application agents and managers use all day, at workspace.pulse4all.app: one Next.js app with two surfaces on one foundation, the agent desk and the management side (Live, Report, Data). It talks only to Postgres (and the push service), never directly to every source; customer records are read from the mirror and refreshed live from the CRM through the adapter. It runs as a Cloud Run service in europe-west4 per project, with its own service account as `cma_app` member, connecting through the Cloud SQL Node.js connector with automatic IAM database authentication (no password anywhere). The screens reach Postgres in-process through the data layer; the same layer is exposed as a small JSON API under `/api/v1/me` (see API). Visitors reach it only through a global external Application Load Balancer with Identity-Aware Proxy (IAP) in front (see Web app and domain).

**Customer data.** Customer facts are owned by the CRM. Postgres holds the mirror described above and the synced canonical entities, so it falls under the same GDPR care Pulse4all already applies to HubSpot and Aircall (EU hosting, access control, retention that follows the CRM). The management screens show no customer data: alerts carry case status and a link. The agent desk shows the record it serves, read from the mirror and the CRM, and the audit log records who opened which record and when (who-saw-what), from the first desk increment. Staff data (names, work emails, time and performance history, the audit log) is personal data too and gets a retention rule. Real staff data reaches prod with the first agent hours (Roadmap step 2), before any synced customer data; Yordi gets the heads-up on the new data store before that, and a second heads-up before the mirror lands (Roadmap step 3). Since 5 October 2026 prod holds the team's own working time (the first real workdays, through the screens); agents' hours follow only after the conditions in Roadmap step 2. The dev environment holds test data only; real data, staff or synced, lands only in prod.

### Environments and infrastructure

Everything lives in the pulse4all.com Google Cloud organization, in the folder **Contactcenter-Management-App** (folder ID 641665155313; renamed from Callcenter-Management-App, decided 3 October and executed 4 October 2026; projects and the EU location policy unchanged). An organization policy on that folder only allows EU locations. The folder also enforces `iam.automaticIamGrantsForDefaultServiceAccounts` and `iam.managed.preventPrivilegedBasicRolesForDefaultServiceAccounts` (since 11 October 2026); organisation policies on the folder are changed by the break-glass account by convention (`martin@pulse4all.com` also holds `roles/orgpolicy.policyAdmin` at organisation level, which stays: Decision log, 11 October 2026), and the `gcloud org-policies` commands need `--billing-project=p4a-cma-dev`. Both projects are billed to the Pulse4all billing account, each with its own budget alert. Access to the folder (11 October 2026, audit finding 07): the break-glass account `martin.bartels@pulse4all.com`, with two-step verification, holds `roles/owner` and `roles/resourcemanager.folderAdmin` on the folder; `martin@pulse4all.com` holds Folder Admin and Folder Editor on the folder and `roles/owner` on both projects. Roles at organisation level also reach the folder (`martin@` and `mark@` with `roles/owner`, `joshua@` with `roles/resourcemanager.organizationAdmin`) and are not changed from the CMA work (Decision log). Data Access audit logging (`DATA_READ`) is on for Secret Manager in `p4a-cma-prod` and `p4a-cma-dev` since 13 October 2026 (set by Martin in the console; sixth Gemini audit FIND-02), so every read of a secret's value is logged with the identity that read it.

**Dev database server `cma-dev-pg`:** PostgreSQL 18, Cloud SQL Enterprise edition, `db-g1-small` (shared core, no SLA, dev only), single zone in europe-west4. Daily backups at 01:00 UTC with point-in-time recovery, deletion protection, SSL required, IAM authentication on, maintenance week 1, Sunday 02:00 UTC. Database: `cma`. Budget alert €75 per month.

**Prod database server `cma-prod-pg`:** same edition, version, region and protection settings as dev, `db-custom-1-3840` (one dedicated vCPU, 3.75 GB) with high availability (regional: a standby in a second zone of europe-west4 with automatic failover) since 7 October 2026, `db-g1-small` before that (decision 4 October 2026), maintenance week 2 so dev receives updates a week earlier. The instance enforces a password policy for built-in logins (upper and lower case, digit and symbol). The dedicated core and high availability were switched on 7 October 2026 while prod held only the team's own time: the tier change restarts the instance for a few minutes, cheap with two people clocked in and a planned window with thirteen agents mid-shift (roughly $100 a month for the pair, against roughly $35 before; the exact figure from the pricing calculator). Database: `cma`. Budget alert €150 per month, which the new tier brings within reach; raise it when the first monthly bill shows the level.

**Web app and domain (prod), live since 4 October 2026:** Cloud Run service `cma-web` in europe-west4 with its own service account `cma-web@p4a-cma-prod.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, ingress limited to internal and Cloud Load Balancing, so the `run.app` address refuses everyone. In front of it a global external Application Load Balancer (`cma-web-backend`, `cma-web-urlmap`, `cma-web-proxy`, forwarding rule `cma-web-https`, HTTPS only on port 443) on the reserved address `cma-web-ip` (34.54.22.146), with a Google-managed certificate `cma-web-cert` for workspace.pulse4all.app. IAP on the backend admits only `cma@pulse4all.com` during the build; verified both ways on 4 October 2026 (a pulse4all.com account outside the group is refused). DNS for pulse4all.app is at IONOS: one A record `workspace` → 34.54.22.146; the other records (mail, root domain) are untouched. Cost about €18 per month for the forwarding rule; IAP has no charge. Chosen over Cloud Run domain mapping, which Google marks as preview and not recommended for production.

Since 5 October 2026 `cma-web` runs the CMA's own web application (image `europe-west4-docker.pkg.dev/p4a-cma-prod/cma/web:<short sha>`) instead of Google's placeholder: My day, My hours, No access yet and Log out, with real IAP token verification. Since the evening of 5 October it runs on the database: `CMA_AUTH_MODE=iap`, `CMA_DATA_MODE=api`, `IAP_AUDIENCE=/projects/467777891162/global/backendServices/4482730491680652158`, `CMA_DB_INSTANCE=p4a-cma-prod:europe-west4:cma-prod-pg`, `CMA_DB_USER=cma-web@p4a-cma-prod.iam` (an IAM database user, member of `cma_app` with inheritance and without `SET ROLE`), `CMA_DB_NAME=cma`, `CMA_DB_POOL_MAX=5`, at most 4 instances (4 × 5 connections against the instance's 50). Both mode variables fail closed (unset means iap and api). These values are set by the build, not on the service (see Build and deploy). The page shows the deployed short SHA bottom left. Since 12 October 2026 it runs with `min-instances=1`, against cold starts at sign-in (Decision log).

**Web app in dev, since 5 October 2026:** a private Cloud Run service `cma-web` in `p4a-cma-dev` (no unauthenticated access, so the `run.app` address answers 403), service account `cma-web@p4a-cma-dev.iam.gserviceaccount.com` with Cloud SQL Client and Cloud SQL Instance User, IAM database user `cma-web@p4a-cma-dev.iam` as `cma_app` member, `CMA_AUTH_MODE=mock`, `CMA_DATA_MODE=api`, at most 2 instances. It runs the image Cloud Build made for prod, by tag (build once, deploy many): dev's Cloud Run service agent has Artifact Registry reader on the `cma` repository in `p4a-cma-prod`, nothing else. A new image goes to dev with `gcloud run services update cma-web --project=p4a-cma-dev --region=europe-west4 --image=…/web:<short sha>`, which keeps the environment variables. Opened with `gcloud run services proxy cma-web --project=p4a-cma-dev --region=europe-west4 --port=8080`; the mock subject is chosen per request (see Authentication). Dev has no build of its own (the Cloud Build API is not enabled in `p4a-cma-dev`): an image exists only after a merge to `main`, whose build also deploys it to prod, so a web change reaches prod before dev's API verifier runs on it (see Release flow for the order this implies).

**Code:** GitHub repository `Pulse4all-com/cma` (public until the launch, then private: Decision log, 8 October 2026) (organisation owned by Pulse4all; Martin's account is Pulse4all-DEV) with `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (the working rules for Claude Code sessions), `db/` (the migration, seed and verify scripts `00` to `59`, in the repository since 5 October 2026; `08` retired 6 October 2026, the add-a-person template retired 8 October 2026 in favour of `cma.add_person()` and the People screen), `web/` (the application), `cloudbuild.yaml` and `records/` (verify output per migration and environment, for example `records/0002-dev-2026-10-05`; the web app's own screenshots live under `records/web-<env>-<date>`), and `docs/` (the runbook `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, dated analyses and design notes under `docs/analyses/`, `docs/nocodb/` with the NocoDB service template, `deploy.sh`, the `grants.sql` template and its README, and since 12 October 2026 `docs/import/` with the legacy time import's step-by-step and its COPY file), `tools/` (since 12 October 2026: `legacy-sessions-to-staging.mjs`, plain Node without dependencies, which turns an earlier system's session export into the import's staging file and writes the person map statement, with `--dedupe-sessions latest-logout` for an export that holds one session twice (Decision log, 12 October 2026); its synthetic fixtures in `tools/fixtures/` and its check `verify-legacy-sessions.mjs`, `npm run verify:import` from `web/`), and `hubspot/` (since 10 October 2026: one HubSpot developer-platform project template rendered into the apps CMA ingest dev and CMA ingest prod, static auth and private distribution, with `render.mjs`, its verifier and a README; Decision log, 10 October 2026), and `ingest/` (since 10 October 2026: the ingest service `cma-ingest`, plain Node 22 on `pg` and the Cloud SQL connector, with `src/server.mjs`, `src/core/` (database, secrets with a 5-minute cache, event recording, claim and finish, the budgeted read-back, the keyed hash, structured logs) and `src/adapters/hubspot/` (`verify.mjs`, `map.mjs`, `read.mjs`, and since B8 `forms.mjs`, `reconcile.mjs`, `backfill.mjs`), `src/adapters/automation/` (since 11 October 2026: the bearer-authenticated message route of 0008a), `src/adapters/aircall/` (since B9, 13 October 2026: `verify.mjs`, `map.mjs`, `read.mjs`, `reconcile.mjs`), `src/adapters/shopify/` (since B10, 13 October 2026: `verify.mjs`, `map.mjs`, `client.mjs`, `read.mjs`, `match.mjs`, `reconcile.mjs`) and HubSpot's `match.mjs` and `write.mjs` (the contact lookups and the outbox writer), the jobs in `jobs/` (`sweep.mjs`, `poll-forms.mjs`, `reconcile.mjs`, `backfill.mjs`, and since B10 `outbox.mjs`, entry points over `src/jobs/`), its Dockerfile and a Cloud Build file not wired to a trigger, the verifiers `verify/signature.mjs`, `mapping.mjs`, `hash.mjs`, `forms.mjs`, `aircall-token.mjs`, `aircall-mapping.mjs`, `aircall-scripts.mjs`, `shopify-signature.mjs`, `shopify-mapping.mjs`, `shopify-client.mjs`, `shopify-scripts.mjs`, `outbox.mjs` (pure, `npm test`), `flow.mjs` and `jobs-flow.mjs` (against a local PostgreSQL and a fake HubSpot), `aircall-flow.mjs` (against a local PostgreSQL and a fake Aircall), `shopify-flow.mjs` (against a local PostgreSQL, a fake Shopify and a fake HubSpot, with a burst of 200 orders) and, since B10.1, `outbox-flow.mjs` (the generic outbox worker against a local PostgreSQL and a fake HubSpot), and `tools/replay.mjs` (HubSpot and, since B9, Aircall); its deploy, front and alert scripts (`deploy.sh`, `front.sh`, `alert.sh`), README, `hubspot-owners.mjs`, `shopify-subscribe.mjs`, `aircall-webhook.mjs` and `aircall-users.mjs` in `docs/ingest/`). `web/` is Next.js 16 (App Router, Turbopack, TypeScript strict), Tailwind v4 with the Pulse4all tokens in `src/app/globals.css`, Montserrat shipped with the app; screens import data only through `src/lib/data` and identity only through `src/lib/auth/identity`, both with a mock implementation chosen by environment, so the API replaces the mocks without touching a component. Since the night build of 9 October 2026 `src/lib/configuration.ts` (the configuration screens' logic) is pure too, with `src/lib/api/configuration.ts` for the routes' parameter checks, and `web/jobs/close-forgotten-workdays.mjs` is the scheduler's job: plain JavaScript on `pg` and the Cloud SQL connector (a server-external package since then, so the image carries it next to `pg`; the Dockerfile copies `jobs/`), run by a Cloud Run job on the same image (`docs/scheduler/deploy.sh`). All copy is in `src/lib/copy.ts` (English and Dutch). The Postgres side lives in `src/lib/db/client.ts` (connector, pool, transaction-local tenant context, SQLSTATE translation), `src/lib/data/postgres.ts` (the `CmaData` implementation) and `src/lib/api/respond.ts` with `src/app/api/v1/me/**`, `src/app/api/v1/team/**` and `src/app/api/v1/settings` (the JSON API); `src/lib/csv.ts` writes the export files and `src/lib/dashboard.ts` computes the Dashboard's groups and series, both pure and without runtime imports; since the night build of 8 October 2026 `src/lib/team.ts` (the Team screen's logic) and `src/lib/roster.ts` (the quick-typing parser, the week, coverage states, adherence) are pure too, with `src/lib/api/roster.ts` for the roster routes' parameter checks; `src/lib/db/client.ts` has a local-only `CMA_DB_HOST` path (a plain connection, never set on Cloud Run) so the API verifier can run against a local PostgreSQL before dev sees a change. The shell and its navigation live in `Shell.tsx` (an `async` server component), `NavGroup.tsx` (client) and `src/lib/nav.ts`, which holds the navigation model (see API, Shell and navigation); the Welcome page is `src/app/page.tsx` with `ClockInCard.tsx` (client, the one write on it), My day is `src/app/day/page.tsx`, My account is `src/app/account/page.tsx` with `GET /api/v1/me/profile`; since 11 October 2026 Messages is `src/app/messages/page.tsx` and `src/app/messages/send/page.tsx` with `MessageCenter.tsx`, `Inbox.tsx`, `MessageComposer.tsx`, the pure `src/lib/messages.ts` and the routes under `src/app/api/v1/me/messages/**` and `src/app/api/v1/messages/**`. In mock mode the in-memory state lives on `globalThis`, because every page and route handler is its own bundle in the standalone server (found 7 October 2026 when Clock in moved to a route). Verifiers in `web/verify/` (copy with 1059 keys since the night of 13 October 2026, corrections, CSV, dashboard, live, team, roster, configuration, messages, theme, layout, IAP token, API, scheduler; `verify:import` runs `tools/verify-legacy-sessions.mjs`), each with a `--provoke` mode that must fail; the API verifier runs against dev through the proxy and closes its own leftover days through the corrections route. `verify:api`, `verify:scheduler` and the clock-ins of `db/07` run only inside the workday window (07:00-22:00 Europe/Amsterdam for Pulse4all), and the writing verifiers refuse a non-dev database (`verify/scheduler.mjs` exits 2 unless `PGCONN` holds the dev seed's `agent.two@example.com`). Theme and layout need the app in mock mode on port 8080: `npm start` serves `.next/standalone`, which lacks `.next/static` (the Docker image copies it in), so after every local `npm run build` run `cp -r .next/static .next/standalone/.next/` (and `cp -r public .next/standalone/` when `public` exists) before `CMA_AUTH_MODE=mock CMA_DATA_MODE=mock PORT=8080 npm start` in a second terminal tab; otherwise every page renders without CSS (Times New Roman, `#0000ee` links) and both verifiers fail on browser defaults. Prod is unaffected. The layout verifier writes its screenshots to `records/layout/`, which go into the next commit. People with a Google login were added per tenant with `db/people_add_prod.template.sql` (6 to 8 October 2026, PR #21, retired with the night build: the People screen and `cma.add_person()` do this now, and the template's rules live in the function), copied into the Studio editor and filled in there, never in the repository; `.gitignore` refuses filled copies (`db/people_add_*.sql`). It takes tenant, email, display name, employer key, role key, login id, login system (`google` for Pulse4all) and an optional zone in one place; refuses unfilled placeholders, a Google id that is not the numeric `sub`, an id that belongs to another person, a different id for an existing person, an inactive person, an unknown tenant, employer, role or zone; writes user, login id and role in one statement, so a refusal writes nothing; is rerunnable; records the person running it as `granted_by` when they are a person in that tenant; and ends with a verdict that shows the login id only as matched. Best run by the person being added, so the id never travels.

**Working on the code** (step by step in `docs/RUNBOOK.md`): from a clone in Cloud Shell (`~/cma`, logged in to GitHub with `gh` as Pulse4all-DEV, Node 22 through `nvm` to match the Docker image). Every change goes on a branch, passes `npm run build` with the repository's own TypeScript settings and its verifiers in Cloud Shell, then a pull request (`gh pr create`) and a squash merge; `main` takes changes only through a pull request (repository rule, a direct push is refused with `GH013`), records and README passes included. The real build runs before anything reaches `main`, which deploys to prod. A change can also come from **Claude Code on the web** (claude.ai/code), a second way to change the code (9 October 2026): the Claude GitHub App is installed on the Pulse4all-com organisation for `cma` only, and the cloud environment `CMA` has trusted network access, no setup script and no secrets. Claude Code works on a branch and opens a pull request, never merges; it runs `npm run build`, typecheck, lint and the pure verifiers itself. Martin checks the branch out in Cloud Shell with `gh pr checkout <number>`, runs the verifiers that need a server or a database and any `db/` script, then squash-merges (`docs/RUNBOOK.md`, section 2a). The session rules are in the root `CLAUDE.md`.

**Where a command runs (7 October 2026).** Every block in a runbook, handover or chat is labelled with where it runs: **Studio** (SQL in Cloud SQL Studio, always with the instance, `cma-dev-pg` or `cma-prod-pg`), **Terminal** (Cloud Shell, in `~/cma` unless stated), **Browser** (a page or a click path in the Console or the Workspace) or **File** (an edit in the repository, with its path). SQL and shell never share a block. Before running in Studio, check the instance in its breadcrumb. In Studio the first statement is `set role cma_owner;` (a personal login has no direct rights on schema `cma`; `cma_owner` is not subject to RLS, so it reads across tenants); Studio shows no notices and one result per statement, so a failed check raises `FAIL …` and the last result is the verdict, and earlier results sit in the results dropdown. A script from the repository can also run from the Terminal through the Auth Proxy (`cloud-sql-proxy --auto-iam-authn --port 5433 <project>:europe-west4:<instance>`, then `psql "host=127.0.0.1 port=5433 dbname=cma user=$(gcloud config get-value account) sslmode=disable" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f <script>`), which writes the verify output straight into `records/` as text; a provoked run then uses a temporary copy with the switch set and `ON_ERROR_STOP=0`, so every block shows its own failure. Patches reach Cloud Shell as files (⋮ → Upload), applied on a fresh branch with `git apply`; this is the fallback when Claude Code is not used.

**Build and deploy:** Cloud Build in europe-west4, not GitHub Actions, so a later move to another Git host only changes the source setting. GitHub connection `cma-github` (Cloud Build repositories 2nd gen, authorised with the Pulse4all-DEV account, app installed on the Pulse4all-com organisation for `cma` only). Trigger `cma-web-main` runs `cloudbuild.yaml` on every push to `main` that touches `web/**` or `cloudbuild.yaml`, with `web/verify/**` as ignored files since 7 October 2026 (Cloud Build drops the ignored files from a commit first and builds only if something else matches, so a change to the verifiers alone builds nothing): Docker build with the short SHA as version, push to Artifact Registry repository `cma` (europe-west4), `gcloud run deploy cma-web`. The build runs as service account `cma-build@p4a-cma-prod.iam.gserviceaccount.com` (Artifact Registry writer, Cloud Run admin, log writer, may act as `cma-web`), not the default Cloud Build account. The legacy Cloud Build account `467777891162@cloudbuild.gserviceaccount.com` holds only the custom role `cmaBuildCreate` (`cloudbuild.builds.create` only) since 12 October 2026, instead of `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor`, because the trigger's invocation checks `cloudbuild.builds.create` on it (Decision log, 11 and 12 October 2026); never Builder. The trigger's own account stays `cma-build`. Manual builds use `gcloud builds submit` with staging bucket `gs://p4a-cma-prod-build-src` (europe-west4; the default staging bucket is in the US and the EU-only folder policy refuses it) and always pass `--service-account=projects/p4a-cma-prod/serviceAccounts/cma-build@p4a-cma-prod.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, because the project's default build account is the Compute Engine default service account, which holds no role (both build files set `logging: CLOUD_LOGGING_ONLY`, which a named build account requires). The Cloud Build service agent holds only `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor` on the connection's regional token secret `cma-github-github-oauthtoken-7f556b` (europe-west4), not Secret Manager Admin on the project (11 October 2026); regional secrets are listed with `gcloud secrets list --location=europe-west4`. The ingest image is built the same way (since 11 October 2026): `docs/ingest/deploy.sh` builds it in `p4a-cma-prod` as `cma-build` with that staging bucket, skips the build when `ingest:<short sha>` already exists, and deploys it by tag to dev and prod alike. A build takes about two minutes on `E2_HIGHCPU_8`.

The deploy sets all of the service's environment variables at once (`--set-env-vars`), so `cloudbuild.yaml` and the trigger's substitutions are the one place `cma-web` is configured; a variable changed on the service by hand disappears at the next build. Substitutions: `_AUTH_MODE`, `_DATA_MODE`, `_IAP_AUDIENCE`, `_DB_INSTANCE`, `_DB_USER`, `_DB_NAME` (default `cma`), `_DB_POOL_MAX` (default 5), `_MAX_INSTANCES` (default 4); the file defaults to mock data. A first step, `check-config`, stops the build before anything is built or deployed when `_DATA_MODE=api` lacks `_DB_INSTANCE` or `_DB_USER`. The trigger is a 2nd-gen repository trigger: `gcloud builds triggers update github` refuses its substitutions (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`), so they are changed with `gcloud beta builds triggers export` → edit → `gcloud beta builds triggers import`, then `gcloud builds triggers run cma-web-main --branch=main`. Build status from the command line needs `--region=europe-west4`.

### Release flow: sandbox to production in small steps

- Dev is the sandbox, prod is live. Every increment that works and is verified in dev is promoted to prod; dev then moves on to the next iteration. Production exists from the first increment (migration 0001). We do not build the whole platform first and launch at the end.
- An increment ships with its migration, its verify script and a short release note. Promotion to prod: confirm point-in-time recovery is on (or take an on-demand backup), run the migration as `cma_owner` under a personal login, run the verify script, keep its output, then release the matching application or sync change.
- The verify output (screenshots or exported results) is kept per environment and date, for example `records/0001-dev-2026-10-04` and `records/0001-prod-2026-10-04`, next to the scripts in the repository. Records and the README pass of an increment are committed before the next increment starts and checked in the repository (`ls records/`, `git log -- README.md`), not assumed from a PR title. A verdict that never reached `records/` is rerun and recorded under the date it actually ran, never backdated (7 October 2026: the 0003b verdicts and the README's seventh and eighth passes had not reached the repository).
- With a web change (since 6 October 2026, because only prod's build makes images): migration and its verify script in dev, then in prod, then the merge to `main` (which builds and deploys prod), then the same image on dev by its short SHA for the API verifier and its `--provoke` run, then a hand check in prod. A migration therefore never breaks the web app already running, and a web change keeps working on the database it meets. A web change without a migration starts at the merge (c2.1).
- Migrations are forward-only and rerunnable. A mistake is fixed with a new migration, never by editing one that has shipped. Should a business line ever get its own database, the same migration runs there too.
- Prod holds real data from the first agent hours onward, dev never. Test data for a new feature lives in dev; a feature is only done when it is verified in prod.
- A verification is not verified until it has been made to fail on purpose (every verifier has a `--provoke` mode). Inherited from the AgentUI trial.
- Every commit reaches `main` through a pull request, the records and README passes of an increment included (repository rule since before 7 October 2026; the API records of increment f were refused as a direct push and merged as PR #38). During a GitHub incident the merge can land without the webhook that starts the build: `gcloud builds list` then lacks the SHA and `gcloud builds triggers run cma-web-main --sha=<full sha>` starts it by hand (7 October 2026); a late delivery that builds the same SHA again is harmless. After every merge that touches `web/**` or `cloudbuild.yaml`, confirm with `gcloud builds list --project=p4a-cma-prod --region=europe-west4 --limit=1` that a build exists for the merge's short SHA, because a trigger that cannot create a build leaves no trace in the build list (11 October 2026).

### Data model: foundation (migration 0001)

Designed 3 October 2026 as the second half of Roadmap step 1; run and verified in dev and prod on 4 October 2026. Five scripts, run in Cloud SQL Studio for now, all safe to rerun:

| Script | Run as | Runs in | Does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `00_roles.sql` | `postgres`, once per database server | dev, prod | creates the database roles and the `bq_reader` login, grants them to the team logins; the only script that needs `postgres` |
| `01_foundation.sql` | your own IAM login | every database, unchanged | migration 0001: schemas, helpers, first tables, tenant separation, audit log, reporting views, permission catalog. Universal: no customer, tenant or vendor specifics |
| `02_seed_pulse4all.sql` | your own IAM login | dev and prod | customer configuration: the two tenants, their organisations and channels, the Subscriptions work statuses (since 7 October 2026, after 0002), the export settings (after 0003b) and the app links (after 0003d); rerun after each of those additions |
| `03_seed_dev_test_data.sql` | your own IAM login | dev only | fictional users and roles, including an analytics user since 7 October 2026 (no clock), and a dev-only app link that needs `workday.team`; the CRM user ids were written for the V1 login screen, which is not built |
| `04_verify.sql` | your own IAM login | dev and prod | proves ownership, separation, fail-closed behaviour and the audit trail; block A is universal, B to H use the seeds |
| `05_time_model.sql` | your own IAM login | every database, unchanged | migration 0002: the time model, see the next section |
| `06_seed_dev_time_model.sql` | your own IAM login | dev only | mock login ids for the test users, a personal time zone, a short time history written through the write functions, with the statuses picked by flag from the tenant's list (since 7 October 2026, no key in the fixture) |
| `07_verify_time_model.sql` | your own IAM login | dev and prod | blocks A to K; A, B, C, D, F, G, I, J are universal (they create throwaway users inside a rolled-back transaction), E, H, K use the dev fixture; after 0003 block I fails with `CMA06` instead of `CMA04`. Block H assumes the fixture's open day of two days ago: once the verifier has run, Agent Two's only open day is today's, and the block's end at 18:00 is refused as a time in the future (seen 7 October 2026; the two aborted-transaction lines that follow belong to it) |

| `57_verify_commerce_phone_hash.sql` | your own IAM login | dev only (refuses a database without the dev seed's `agent.two@example.com`, as `53`) | blocks A (structure and privileges, 5 checks: the migration, the two columns with their check and partial indexes, nothing else personal on the two tables (since 0008h it also accepts the three link columns of `58` once 0008h is recorded), the reporting views without the hash, the upserts the application's only) and B (behaviour on throwaway tenants, rolled back, 7 checks); one PASS or FAIL row per check and a verdict row (12 PASS); the `verify.provoke` switch fails every check |
| `58_commerce_contact_link.sql` | your own IAM login | every database, unchanged, after `56` | addition 0008h: a commerce customer's matched CRM contact kept in the CMA (`crm_connection_id`, `crm_contact_source_id`, `matched_at` on `cma.commerce_customer`, both checks, a foreign key and an index), `cma.set_commerce_customer_contact()` and `cma.forget_commerce_contact_links()` (`ingest.write`); nothing written to the CRM; see Data model: commerce contact link; one transaction; then `59` (dev) and `57` (dev), `37`, `33`, `31` (unchanged) |
| `59_verify_commerce_contact_link.sql` | your own IAM login | dev only (refuses a database without the dev seed's `agent.two@example.com`, as `53`) | blocks A (structure and privileges, 4 checks: the migration, the columns with their checks, foreign key and index, the functions the application's only, nothing else of 0007b, 0007c or 0008g changed) and B (behaviour on throwaway tenants, rolled back, 9 checks); one PASS or FAIL row per check and a verdict row (13 PASS); the `verify.provoke` switch fails every check |
| `people_add_prod.template.sql` | | | retired 8 October 2026 (night build): `cma.add_person()` and the People screen replace it; the script was deleted from `db/` |

Every script except `00_roles.sql` starts with a guard that refuses to run as `postgres` (override for a declared emergency: `set cma.emergency = 'on'`), followed by `set role cma_owner`. Another customer gets its own seed file; the migration stays untouched. On a fresh database the order is migrations first (`01`, `05`, …), then the customer seed (`02`), then dev seeds; on an existing database a seed that touches a new column is rerun after the migration that adds it (`02` is guarded for this).

**Tenant = business line.** A tenant is one isolated environment with its own people, configuration and data, inside the CMA database. Pulse4all Subscriptions and Pulse4all Invest are the two tenants; every tenant-scoped table carries `tenant_id`. `cma.create_tenant(slug, name, timezone)` creates a tenant with the default role ladder.

**Tables**

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `tenant` | global | one row per business line: slug, name, time zone, status |
| `permission` | global | what the application code can check (`roster.manage`, `leads.accept`, …); changed only by migrations |
| `schema_migration` | global | which migration scripts have run |
| `organisation` | tenant | employers of the people in a tenant: the company itself and partners such as the call center. `name` is the display name the Workspace shows (Pulse4all's own: `Pulse4all`, since 7 October 2026); a legal entity name for invoices or payroll files becomes a separate `legal_name` column when needed, never folded into `name` |
| `channel` | tenant | customer contact channels with an `is_synchronous` flag; phone and email today, sms, whatsapp, chat, the Customer App and more as rows later. Skills, routing rules, SLAs and metrics reference a channel, nothing assumes phone |
| `app_user` | tenant | people who use the CMA, any employer, any email domain; one row per person per tenant, never hard-deleted |
| `app_user_external_id` | tenant | the same person in other systems, keyed by system; for Pulse4all `google` (login), `hubspot_owner` (lead assignment), `aircall_user` (call matching). Another customer uses its own system keys |
| `app_role`, `role_permission` | tenant | the role ladder per tenant (agent, supervisor, manager, analytics), seeded identically for every tenant and adjustable per tenant |
| `user_role` | tenant | role grants, optionally scoped to a team or market; the same role can be granted per scope |
| `audit_log` | tenant | every insert, update and delete on every tenant-scoped table, with before and after image, timestamp and actor; insert-only |

**Tenant separation**

- Hard separation between customers is the database boundary (see Customers, databases and tenants). Everything below separates business lines within one customer.
- Row-level security on every tenant-scoped table. The application sets its context once per transaction with `set_config(..., true)`: `app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id` (the acting CMA user) and optionally `app.actor_label` (the acting process, for example `ingest-api:hubspot`). No tenant set means nothing is visible and every write is rejected.
- Composite foreign keys on `(tenant_id, id)`, so a role, team or employer from one tenant can never be attached to a row of another.
- `cma.setup_tenant_table(table)` applies RLS, grants and the audit trigger. One line per new table in every migration.
- This protects against a forgotten `WHERE`, not against a hostile application: the app is trusted for the tenant it authenticated.

**Database roles and logins**

| Role | Kind | Rights | Members |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cma_owner` | group | owns `cma` and `cma_read` and everything in them; used via `SET ROLE` for migrations and base-table fixes | Martin, Joshua, Finn (SET ROLE only); `postgres` for emergencies |
| `cma_app` | group | read and write on `cma` within the current tenant; insert-only on `audit_log` | ingest API service account (`cma-ingest@<project>.iam`); the CMA web app (`cma-web@<project>.iam`) |
| `cma_readonly` | group | SELECT on the `cma_read` views only, no rights on `cma`; sees all tenants of the customer unless the login user carries an `app.tenant_id` setting | `bq_reader`, later NocoDB readers; the team's own logins by default |
| `bq_reader` | login | BigQuery federated connection; the one password login, password in Secret Manager (`cma-<env>-bq-reader-password`), set via `gcloud sql users set-password` at Sync (dev: set on 11 October 2026; prod: not yet) | |

Team members default to the reader view (the same surface BigQuery and NocoDB get), switch to `cma_owner` for migrations and to `cma_app` to test exactly what the application can see. Martin and Joshua hold ADMIN on the three roles, so Finn and service accounts are granted without using `postgres`. Each database has its own logins and secrets.

**Conventions for every migration**

- System names (hubspot, aircall, zoho, activecampaign, …) appear only as data values, never in table, column, function or role names
- Every migration starts with the personal-login guard and `set role cma_owner`, is rerunnable, and ships with its verify script, because it runs in dev, then in prod, and unchanged in any later database
- Every script runs under a personal IAM login; `postgres` only for a declared emergency, so audit rows always name a person
- `timestamptz` everywhere; the business day is derived from the configured time zone (tenant now, site or market later)
- Facts such as time entries, attempts, lead assignments and notifications are append-only, with `occurred_at` (when it happened) and `recorded_at` (when we wrote it); a correction is a new row with reason and approver, and closed periods are locked
- Facts that affect pay, billing or reporting over time (employment, team membership, skills, rates, targets) carry `valid_from` and `valid_to` and are never overwritten
- Configurable catalogs carry semantic flags (paid, billable, productive, synchronous, outcome, sets do-not-call), never names only, so billing, adherence, SLAs and the queue never depend on a string match
- Synced data carries `source_system`, `source_id`, `synced_at` and the original payload in `raw`; ingest is idempotent on system and id
- Consequences carry their cause (`closed_by_task_id`, `dnc_set_by_task_id`, `supersedes_event_id`), so nothing self-repairs silently
- Every table that reporting needs gets its `cma_read` view in the same migration, with its columns listed explicitly
- `uuidv7()` primary keys; status columns instead of hard deletes

**Access rules**

- No authorized networks. Nothing connects directly; access only goes through the Cloud SQL Auth Proxy, Cloud SQL connectors or Cloud SQL Studio, with a Google login. Enforced by `connectorEnforcement=REQUIRED` on cma-dev-pg and cma-prod-pg since 11 October 2026 (audit finding 04, Decision log).
- Make, n8n and other external tools never connect directly; they go through the ingest API, and the CMA reaches them through the outbox.
- Everyone works under a personal IAM database login. Access is granted through the Google group `cma@pulse4all.com` (Cloud SQL Client, Instance User, Viewer on both projects) plus membership of the database roles above.
- Readers (BigQuery, NocoDB, analytics) never see base tables, only `cma_read`. Each connection gets its own login user; a reader is limited to one tenant by giving its login an `app.tenant_id` setting. Hard separation for dashboards within a customer lives in BigQuery (authorized views or row access policies per dataset).
- `bq_reader` is the only password login, because BigQuery federated connections authenticate with username and password, not IAM. Its password lives only in Secret Manager.
- The built-in `postgres` user is for setup and emergencies only. Its password lives only in Secret Manager (`cma-dev-postgres-password`, `cma-prod-postgres-password`), never in chat, email or screenshots. Generate passwords in Cloud Shell and set them with `gcloud`; the prod instance requires upper and lower case, a digit and a symbol, dev does not.
- The web app is reachable only through the load balancer and IAP; the Cloud Run address refuses direct traffic. Team access during the build through `cma@pulse4all.com`, agents through their pulse4all.com account plus an active `app_user` row
- A budget alert per project emails the billing admins (dev: €75 per month, prod: €150 per month).

### Data model: time model (migration 0002)

Designed and run on 5 October 2026 as the first half of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev (blocks A to K) and prod (A, B, C, D, F, G, I, J) the same day, records in `records/0002-dev-2026-10-05` and `records/0002-prod-2026-10-05`. Universal, no customer specifics; tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL.

**Tables**

| Table | Scope | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `work_status` | tenant | the configurable status list (available, training, meeting, break, lunch by default), each with semantic flags `is_working` (the clock runs), `is_productive`, `is_paid`, `is_billable`, one `is_default` per tenant. Seeded for every tenant by `cma.seed_default_work_statuses()`, which `create_tenant` now calls; adjustable per tenant (Pulse4all Subscriptions' own list since 7 October 2026, see Display rules) |
| `workday` | tenant | one row per user per business day in the user's zone, with the zone snapshotted at open. A header: `status`, `started_at` and `ended_at` are derived from the events by `cma.refresh_workday()`, never set by hand |
| `time_event` | tenant | the facts: `start`, `status`, `end`, append-only, with `occurred_at` and `recorded_at`. `source` is `user`, `system` (scheduler) or `correction`, and since addition 0008d `import` (an earlier system's sessions, with their batch). A correction is a new row with `reason` and `approved_by`; it adds a missing event, replaces one (`supersedes_event_id`) or cancels one (kind `void`). One correction per event; a later correction supersedes the earlier correction |

Time zones: `app_user.timezone`, then `organisation.timezone`, then `tenant.timezone` (`cma.user_timezone()`); all three validated against the IANA names Postgres knows. Newco is `Europe/Madrid` in the Pulse4all seed.

**The write path.** The application never inserts into these tables. It calls `cma.open_workday()`, `cma.set_status()`, `cma.end_workday()` and `cma.correct_time_event()` as `cma_app` with `app.tenant_id` and `app.user_id` set; the same functions serve the scheduler (source `system`, `app.actor_label` set, no user) and any later tool. Rules enforced there: a user opens and ends only their own day; today's day is returned if it exists, open or ended (an ended day stays ended); a correction needs a reason, an approver, and `workday.team` for both the acting user and the approver; the resulting day must have exactly one start, at most one end and nothing after the end. Errors carry stable SQLSTATEs `CMA01` (no acting user) to `CMA05` (tenant configuration missing) so the API maps codes, not text. `cma_app` has no `update` or `delete` on `time_event` and no `delete` on `workday` and `work_status`. Migration 0003 sharpens the correction rules (next section).

**Views.** Each computation exists once as an unfiltered core view owned by `cma_owner` and granted to nobody (`time_event_effective_all`, `time_interval_all`, `workday_summary_all`), with two faces: `cma.<name>` for the application, filtered on the current tenant and empty when none is set, and `cma_read.<name>` for readers, filtered with `reader_sees()`. `workday_summary` gives working, productive, paid and billable seconds per day plus `is_capped`, `needs_correction` and `has_correction`. An open day that was never ended keeps running until the end of its business day, is capped there and flagged; a manager closes it through a correction, later the scheduler does. Reporting views added: `work_status`, `workday`, `time_event` (including superseded rows and corrections), `time_interval`, `workday_summary`; `organisation` and `app_user` gained `timezone`.

**Identity helpers.** `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(system, external_id)`, the one `SECURITY DEFINER` function, returns tenant ids only and is what the login calls before a tenant is known; `cma.user_permissions()` and `cma.has_permission()` resolve the role ladder for the API (scopes are ignored until teams and markets exist).

**Display rules taken from the demo (6 October 2026, Martin decides):** a status's colour is derived from its flags, never stored; since 7 October 2026 (Martin) four groups: working and productive → Leaf Green, working but not productive → Deep Blue, not working and paid → Light Blue, not working and unpaid → Soft Rose, always with the label; on My day the two pauses share Light Blue, because whether a pause is paid is not agent information; the shift belongs to the business day it started on; a change to an end time afterwards is always visible to the agent and the manager; Pulse4all Subscriptions' own list is seed data in `02_seed_pulse4all.sql` (decided and run 7 October 2026, Martin): Available - Sales (the default) and Available - Operations, both working, productive, paid and billable, so availability hours report per work type from the first day; Training, Meeting and Coaching working, not productive, paid and billable; Break and Lunch not working, unpaid and not billable, with time on another project booked as Break; the universal `available` retired (inactive, history keeps its stretches); the demo's other eight statuses dropped. Invest keeps the universal ladder until its own list is decided (Roadmap step 10).

### Data model: corrections (migration 0003)

Designed and run on 6 October 2026 as increment c1 of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev and prod the same day, records in `records/0003-dev-2026-10-06`, `records/0003-prod-2026-10-06` and `records/corrections-dev-2026-10-06`. Universal; no new tables, only functions. Tested locally on PostgreSQL 16 before Cloud SQL.

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.correct_workday(user, date, changes, reason)` | one edit of one person's day in one transaction: the changes (`start`, `status`, `end`, `void`, each with `at`, `statusKey`, `supersedes`) are written in order and the day is validated once at the end, so an edit may pass through states that are invalid on their own. On a date without a day it creates the day (Add day); the first change must then be its start. Returns the day |
| `cma.team_hours(from, to, user?)` | hours per person per day with employer, zone, working and paid seconds and the `is_capped`, `needs_correction`, `has_correction` flags; at most 92 days |
| `cma.team_day(user, date)` | every event of one day, effective or not, with reason and approver, for the day editor |
| `cma.assert_permission(key)` | the shared check: the acting user, active in the tenant, holding the permission; `CMA01` or `CMA06` otherwise |

Rules, enforced in the functions: the person correcting holds `workday.team` and is the approver (V1); nobody corrects their own day, managers included; a reason of 3 to 500 characters on every row of the edit; every time lies within the corrected business day in the day's zone, not in the future, and carries an explicit offset (`Z` or `+hh:mm`), because one local hour occurs twice at the autumn clock change; `refresh_workday` now also refuses a status or end before the start. `correct_time_event` keeps its signature for SQL tools, raises `CMA06` instead of `CMA04` for a missing permission, refuses one's own day and accepts only the acting user as approver. New SQLSTATE `CMA06`: not permitted. The team reads check `workday.team` inside the function, in the same transaction as the read; until teams exist (migration 0004) the scope is the whole tenant.

**Addition 0003a (6 October 2026, increment c2).** `cma.team_people()` lists the people whose time is kept (active, holding `workday.own`) with employer and zone, for people holding `workday.team`, checked inside the function like the team reads. Add day and the person filter need it: `team_hours` only knows people who have a day, and Add day exists for someone who never clocked in. Scripts `db/11_team_people.sql` and `db/12_verify_team_people.sql`; verified in dev (blocks A to D, 4 people) and prod (A and E, 2 people) the same day. Numbered 0003a so the planned migrations keep 0004 to 0009. The verify blocks switch role first (`cma_owner` to read, `cma_app` to act as the app), because a personal login has no direct rights on schema `cma`; Studio shows one result per statement, and the verdict is the last one.

### Data model: tenant settings and exports (migration 0003b)

Designed and run on 6 October 2026 as the database half of increment c3 of Roadmap step 2; verified in dev and prod the same day. Those verdicts were never committed, so the verify was rerun on 7 October 2026 through the Auth Proxy, normal and provoked, in both instances (`records/0003b-dev-2026-10-07`, `records/0003b-prod-2026-10-07`, PR #27): blocks A, B and C pass, and fail with `FAIL A2`, `FAIL B5` and `FAIL C1` when provoked. The closing summary rows read PASS in a provoked run because they only show the stored values; Studio and `ON_ERROR_STOP` never reach them after a failure. Universal. Numbered 0003b so 0004 to 0009 stay.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.setting` | global catalog of the settings the code may read: key, type (`text`, `boolean`, `integer`), allowed values, a universal default and a description. Changed only by migrations, like `cma.permission`; `cma_app` reads it, never writes it |
| `cma.tenant_setting` | a tenant's own value per setting; no row means the catalog default. Tenant-scoped through `setup_tenant_table` (RLS, audit trigger); a trigger validates every write against the catalog (`CMA02` unknown key, `CMA04` invalid value), whatever path writes it |
| `cma.tenant_settings()` | the effective settings of the current tenant (own value or default, with `is_default`); no tenant set returns nothing. No permission: formats are not personal data |
| `cma.set_tenant_setting(key, value)` | writes one value for the current tenant, `null` resets it to the default; needs `tenant.configure`. The configuration screens of step 5 will use it |
