| `cma.import_session` | the file's sessions: account id and name in the earlier system, session id (unique per batch), login and logout in local time (no logout: never logged out), role. Written only by COPY into `cma.import_session_staging` (the staging file's columns, the batch by its key) by a person holding `tenant.configure`, into a staged batch; never changed |
| `cma.import_person_map` | which person of the tenant an account is (inactive people too, system users never); written by the same person into a staged batch; an account without a row is skipped and counted |
| `cma.import_day` | one row per day a load wrote: the workday, its end event, the sessions and merges behind it, the seconds, and whether it was closed by the open-session rule or clipped to the window |
| `cma.stage_import_batch(key, source_system, file_name, from, to, zone, work_status, gap_status)` | a staged batch; the statuses must exist and the period must end before today in the batch's zone; called again with the same values it returns the batch |
| `cma.load_import_batch(key)` | one transaction, rules below; returns the batch with its counts |
| `cma.void_import_batch(key, reason)` | withdraws the batch, below |
| `cma.workday_summary_all` | as in 0008b, same columns, two changes: `needs_correction` also covers an imported day closed by the open-session rule or clipped, while the import's end is effective (a correction that replaces the end clears it, as for a system end); a day without an effective start, which only a withdrawn import leaves, is no day |
| `cma.team_day()` | gains `import_batch_key`; the day editor's history shows "Imported from an earlier system, batch <key>" |
| `cma_read.import_batch`, `cma_read.time_event` | the batch without names (no file name, no free-text reason); the events gain `import_batch_id`. The sessions and the map have no reader view |

All four tables are tenant tables (`setup_tenant_table`: row-level security, the application's policy, audit). The application never deletes a batch, never changes a session, a map row or an imported day, and writes them only through the functions and the COPY target; the functions run as the caller (`cma_app`) and check `tenant.configure` inside, so an agent and a manager are refused and an admin is allowed.

**The load**, per mapped account and local login date, in the batch's zone with its snapshots (`cma.business_day_time`):

- a session without a logout ends at the close time (`workday.auto_close_at`); one that starts at or after it without a logout is dropped (`sessions_open_dropped`); the day is flagged closed by rule, as a system end is;
- every session is clipped to the window (`workday.window_start` to `window_end`); a session with nothing left inside it counts as `sessions_outside_window`; the day is flagged clipped;
- overlapping and touching sessions are merged (`sessions_merged`);
- one day from the first login to the last logout: the start in the work status, at every logout a status change to the gap status and at the next login back to the work status, the end at the last logout; source `import`, `occurred_at` the local time in the batch's zone, `recorded_at` now; then `refresh_workday`. So the working seconds of a day are exactly the sessions after the rules, and the gap status holds the gaps;
- a date that already has a day with effective events is skipped (`days_skipped_existing`: the CMA's own days win); the day of a withdrawn import is used again; a day under one minute is dropped (`days_dropped`);
- the work status is seeded inactive and never offered: no screen and no write path lets anyone choose an inactive status, and the load accepts it as long as no effective event of the tenant uses it (nothing was ever booked on it; after a void the withdrawn events no longer count, so a reload works too). The load still retires it, a no-op for a status that is inactive already; the counts: `sessions_in`, `sessions_unmapped`, `accounts_skipped`, `sessions_open_dropped`, `sessions_outside_window`, `sessions_merged`, `days_written`, `events_written`, `days_closed_by_rule`, `days_clipped`, `days_dropped`, `days_skipped_existing`.

Refusals: a batch not staged (`CMA03`); an unknown status (`CMA02`); a work status that is not working, is the default, or is inactive with time behind it (an effective event of the tenant in it); a gap status that is working or paid; a map row outside the tenant; a session in the spring clock change gap of the batch's zone; a session that starts outside the period; a batch without sessions of a mapped account (all `CMA04`). A refused load writes nothing.

**The void** adds one void correction per effective event of every day the batch wrote, corrections made on those days since included, by the caller as approver with the reason "Import <key> withdrawn: <reason>". No fact is deleted and the day history keeps every row; the days then have no effective event, so the summary, Team hours, the exports and the Dashboard no longer count them, and a new batch may load the same person-days. Chosen over a status flag that hides a batch's events, because a void is the time model's own way to say an event did not count, with who and why, and it needs no change to the effective-event rule every read uses. A staged batch is simply marked voided. A reloaded day shows "Corrected afterwards", because its first import was withdrawn by a correction.

Imported days are kept in the batch's zone (Europe/Amsterdam for Pulse4all, the same clock as Newco's Europe/Madrid). The autumn change is outside Pulse4all's period; an ambiguous local time would take PostgreSQL's reading (the later, standard-time instant). The verify (`51`) proves structure and privileges, then on throwaway tenants with the dev window: agent and manager refused and the admin allowed; one batch with two overlapping sessions, an open session before and one after the close time, a session across the window end and one wholly after it, a login before the window start, three sessions with two gaps, a sub-minute day, an account without a map row, a date with a CMA day and an inactive person; the exact counts, seconds and gap seconds per day, the flags, the events and their batch, Team hours, the status-changes export (entered by import), the day history's batch key, a reader's view without the sessions; the work status retired, a second load and rows into a loaded batch refused; another tenant sees and loads nothing; the void and a reload on the same workdays; seventeen refusals, among them an inactive work status with time behind it; and an inactive work status nothing was ever booked on, as the import status is seeded, that loads.

### Data model: sources configuration (addition 0008e)

Written in the night of 12 to 13 October 2026 (`52_sources_configuration.sql`, `53_verify_sources_configuration.sql`) and amended in place on 13 October 2026 after the review of PR #110, before it ran anywhere (the reserved names and the adapter rule below). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `52` twice, and refused by its pre-flight on a copy holding an adapter with an underscore; `53` 16 PASS normally and failing A1, B1 and B8 under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; fifteen mutations caught; `04` to `51` unchanged afterwards (`docs/night-2026-10-13/local-records/0008e/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/night-2026-10-13/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008e-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008e-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `53` 16 PASS, provoked 13 PASS and 3 FAIL (A1, B1, B8); in prod the reruns of `31` and `33` PASS. It needs 0008d, and refuses to run while an existing connection's adapter is not lower-case letters and digits only (it lists tenant id, adapter and name).

Why: on 12 October Martin set the intake configuration of the live portal (pipelines, forms, call outcomes, aliases, the assignment rule, owners on persons) by SQL in Studio. The screens Configuration → Sources, Markets and Alert rules and the linked accounts on People do the same through the functions that exist since 0006 to 0008a; this addition holds what had no function, and replaces three functions for two rules of the review (the last three rows of the table). Every write of those screens goes through an existing function (`set_connection_settings`, `set_connection_status`, `upsert_connection` for the name, `set_connection_pipeline`, `set_pipeline_lead`, `set_form`, `set_call_outcome`, `set_telephony_number`, `set_telephony_tag`, `upsert_commerce_store`, `upsert_market`, `set_market_status`, `set_market_alias`, `remove_market_alias`, `set_business_hours`, `set_business_holiday`, `remove_business_holiday`, `upsert_message_rule`, `upsert_assignment_rule`, `set_message_rule_enabled`, `set_user_external_id`, `remove_user_external_id`).

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.integration_connection.kind` | `crm`, `telephony`, `commerce` or `automation`: the kind of source in the data model's terms (the tables its data lands in), never a vendor; null until set. At the migration an existing connection takes the one kind its rows imply (a connection with rows of two kinds, or none, stays unset). `cma_read.integration_connection` gains the column |
| `cma.set_connection_kind(connection, kind)` | sets it (`tenant.configure`); refuses an unknown kind (`CMA04`) and a kind that contradicts the rows the connection holds (`CMA03`): a connection with CRM records cannot become telephony |
| `cma.sources()` | every connection of the tenant with its kind, the kinds its rows imply, status, account id, non-secret settings, the names of its two secrets (names, never values), events in the last 24 hours, waiting and parked events, the last event, and the size of its catalogs (pipelines, forms, call outcomes, fields, lines, tags, the store's handle); `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.connection_catalogs(connection)` | its forms, call outcomes, lines and tags with their configuration, and the newest record id per record type for the record link preview (ids only); `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.markets_all()`, `cma.business_hours_all()`, `cma.business_holidays_all()` | every market with its aliases, inactive ones included; the office hours per market and weekday (`*` the tenant's default); the holidays; `tenant.configure` |
| `cma.message_rules()` | every rule of both triggers (`record_created`, `record_assigned`) with record type, pipelines, audience (permission, language match, clocked in only, fallback), urgency, maximum age, the switch, and its alerts in the last 30 days with the last one (the sound follows the urgency: an urgent alert chimes); `tenant.configure` (brief B14) |
| `cma.linked_account_systems()` | the ids a person can hold: `<adapter>_owner` per CRM connection, `<adapter>_user` per telephony connection (the vocabulary the reads of 0007d match on), plus every such system someone already holds an id for, so an id never disappears from the screen when its connection is switched off or changes kind; `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all` |
| `cma.linked_accounts()` | the ids held by the people the caller may see on People (as `cma.directory()`: `users.manage_all` everyone, `users.manage_agents` the non-managing people and themselves); never a sign-in id |
| `cma.set_user_external_id()`, `cma.remove_user_external_id()` | as in 0007, with the People rule instead of `users.manage_all`: a manager links the people they may edit, an admin anyone (`cma.assert_may_manage` of 0004). Checks in order: the permission, the system reads `<system>_owner` or `<system>_user` and the id has 1 to 100 characters without spaces (`CMA04`), the person exists (`CMA02`), the caller may manage them (`CMA06`), the id is not another person's (`CMA03`) |
| `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(system, external id)` | replaced: as in 0002, plus a system name ending in `_owner` or `_user` never matches, so a linked id never signs anyone in; still the one `SECURITY DEFINER` function, owned by `cma_owner`, executable by `cma_app` only |
| `cma.add_person()` | replaced: as in 0004, plus a login system ending in `_owner` or `_user` is refused (`CMA04`, "a login system cannot end in _owner or _user; those name linked accounts"), checked right after the login system's form; signature, every other check and their order unchanged |
| `cma.upsert_connection()` | replaced: as in 0006, plus an adapter name that does not match `^[a-z0-9]+$` is refused (`CMA04`, "an adapter name is lower-case letters and digits only"); signature and grants unchanged |

The functions run as the caller (`cma_app`, except the `SECURITY DEFINER` login lookup), are executable by `cma_app` only and check their permission inside, so a reader and an agent are refused. The verify (`53`) proves structure and privileges, then on throwaway tenants: the kind set, refused against held rows, unknown, or for an agent; the sources with their kinds and catalog sizes and a manager refused; the catalogs with the newest record id; markets with aliases, hours and holidays; rules of both triggers with their alert counts; linked-account systems from connections and held ids; the directory's scope; five refusals of the People rule; the other tenant seeing nothing; `add_person` refusing a login system ending in `_owner` or `_user`; `find_tenants_for_identity` finding no tenant for a linked id while it still finds the tenant's Ingest user; `upsert_connection` refusing an adapter with an underscore: 16 checks (4 in block A, 12 in block B). Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants in a rolled-back transaction next to the dev seed.

### Data model: uncounted lines (addition 0008f)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`54_uncounted_lines.sql`, `55_verify_uncounted_lines.sql`; brief B9a). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `54` twice, and on a copy at 0008e holding calls on two lines already marked not counted (6 calls, 6 taggings and 1 link removed, the counted line and the CRM call untouched, a rerun zeros); `55` 17 PASS and every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; seventeen mutations caught; two sessions at once proving the line lock; `04` to `53` unchanged afterwards (`docs/b9a-uncounted-lines/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b9a-uncounted-lines/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008f-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008f-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `55` 17 PASS, provoked all 17 FAIL; in prod `54`'s one-off removed the calls on the 5 lines of Aircall Subscriptions not counted (12 calls, 2 taggings and 12 links), and the reruns of `31`, `33` and `35` PASS. It needs 0008e.

Why: one telephony account can serve several business lines (tenants). Pulse4all's Aircall account carries both the Subscriptions and the Invest lines, and the Subscriptions connection received the Invest calls too. A line that does not count is another business line's (or an internal or test line), so its calls are never stored in this tenant (Decision log, 13 October 2026). The ingest adapters are unchanged: every call write (webhook, sweep, reconcile, backfill) goes through the one function below.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.ingest_upsert_telephony_calls(connection, calls)` | as in 0007a, plus: a call whose `numberRef` is a line of this connection with `is_counted` false is not written, outcome `skipped_uncounted_line` (one row per call, like the other outcomes; the ingest's sync runs count it as skipped). A call on a line not yet in the catalog is stored as before. The batch's lines are locked shared (advisory, in line order) before `is_counted` is read |
| trigger `remove_uncounted_calls` on `cma.telephony_number` | after a line is added as not counted, or `is_counted` goes from true to false (`cma.set_telephony_number()` from the Sources screen, or any other update), the connection's calls on that line are deleted in the same transaction with their taggings and links, retired links included; the CRM calls stay. `set_telephony_number` itself is unchanged (returns nothing). Switching a line back on deletes nothing and stores its new calls again. Its earlier calls come back only through a read that takes them as new: the reconcile and the backfill skip a call they listed before with the same fingerprint (`sync:telephony_call:<id>:<fingerprint>`, B9), whether it was stored or skipped then (Open decisions). Its function `cma.remove_calls_of_uncounted_line()` is `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `cma_owner` with a pinned search path and executable by nobody, because the application cannot delete from the telephony tables; the catalog refresh never writes `is_counted`, so it never fires it |
| `cma.remove_uncounted_line_calls(tenant, connection, line)` | the one place that deletes telephony calls: takes the line's exclusive lock (a call write on the line in flight finishes first, and the delete then sees it), refuses a missing line (`CMA02`) or a counted one (`CMA03`), answers the calls, taggings and links removed; the owner's only |
| `cma.remove_all_uncounted_line_calls()` | the rule applied to every line already marked not counted, in every tenant: one row per connection with lines (tenant, connection, lines not counted, calls, taggings and links removed); the owner's only. `54` runs it once as its last result |
| `cma.audit_row()` | as in 0001, plus one narrow exception: a delete made by the removal on `telephony_call`, `telephony_call_tag` or `call_link` keeps the row's ids only (its id, tenant, connection, the source call id, the tag id, the linked call ids) and `"reason": "uncounted_line"`, not the row image; table, time and actor as always. The removal switches it on (`cma.audit_keys_only`, transaction-local) for its own deletes only; every other change, deletes elsewhere included, keeps its full image. The earlier insert and update rows of a removed call keep theirs (Open decisions) |

The application still never deletes from the telephony tables (`35` A3 unchanged); a removal runs with the owner's rights through the trigger, and the audit names the person (`actor_user_id`), the login and `cma_owner` as the database role. On Configuration → Sources → Lines the Counted column's hint says that calls on a line that does not count are not stored and that switching a line off removes its stored calls, and switching a line off asks for confirmation with that sentence (both languages); switching it on does not ask. The verify (`55`) proves structure and privileges and, on throwaway tenants: a counted line stored; a line that does not count skipped with its outcome (a deletion and a padded line id too; nothing of the call or its new tag written); a line not in the catalog stored; switching a line off removing its calls, taggings and links (a retired link included) while the CRM calls, the connection's other lines and the tag catalog stay; the audit of those deletes holding ids only; another connection's and another tenant's calls on a line of the same id untouched; switching back deleting nothing and a call on the line stored again; a line added as not counted removing the calls stored while it was unknown; six refusals (an agent and the Ingest user switching a line off, the application calling either removal, a counted and a missing line); the lines already off removed per connection and a rerun answering zeros; the audit exception narrow (an owner's delete outside the rule, the line's own update and a call's insert keep their images, the switch is off again); and the line lock taken shared by the write and exclusively by the removal: 17 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: commerce phone hash (addition 0008g)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`56_commerce_phone_hash.sql`, `57_verify_commerce_phone_hash.sql`; brief B10). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `56` twice; `57` 12 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; fifteen mutations caught; `04` to `55` unchanged afterwards apart from `07` and `10`, which list the new migration row (`docs/b10-shopify/local-records/db/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b10-shopify/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008g-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008g-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `57` 12 PASS, provoked all 12 FAIL; in prod the reruns of `31`, `33` and `37` PASS. It needs 0008f.

Why (Decision log, 13 October 2026): people request information with one email address and order with another, so an order is matched to earlier calls by its phone number when indirect sales are measured (Open decisions). The calls already carry a keyed hash of the other party's number (0007, 0007a), so the smallest addition is the same hash on the order and its customer, made by the ingest service with the same per-tenant pepper (`ingest-hash-pepper-<tenant>`) and the store's market as the default region, the number dropped in memory. Nothing else personal is added.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.commerce_customer.phone_hash`, `cma.commerce_order.phone_hash` | `HMAC-SHA256(tenant pepper, E.164)` in lower-case hex (64 characters, a check refuses anything else, so a plain number can never land), nullable; a partial index on `(tenant_id, phone_hash)` where not null on each, the shape of `crm_call` and `telephony_call`'s `counterpart_hash`. The order's hash is its own phone's, else its customer's (the Shopify adapter, B10); never a billing or shipping address phone |
| `cma.ingest_upsert_commerce_customers()`, `ingest_upsert_commerce_orders()` | as in 0007b, plus the item key `phoneHash`: stored trimmed and lower-cased; refused (`CMA04`) when it is not 64 hex characters or not a text; a newer read without it clears the stored hash (the store withheld or removed the phone, and the read is the current state); a stale read changes nothing. A customer's deletion clears its hash and its orders' hashes in the same connection; an order's deletion clears the order's. Signatures, grants and every other behaviour unchanged (`37` passes unchanged) |

The reporting views of 0007b are unchanged and leave both hashes out, as they leave out `counterpart_hash`. Retention: the hash follows the tenant's retention for call data (1 year), but no retention job exists yet for call data or these columns (Open decisions); the audit log keeps the row images with the hash, as it does for calls. The verify (`57`) proves structure and privileges (the migration row, the columns with their check and partial indexes, the two tables holding exactly 0007b's columns plus `phone_hash`, the views without a hash, phone or email column, the upserts the application's only and in the caller's rights) and, on throwaway tenants: a customer's hash stored lower-cased and audited as a hash, the 0007b item shape without a hash still taken; a read without the hash clearing it, a stale read leaving it, a newer read setting it; a plain number, 63 characters, a letter beyond f and a JSON number refused for customers and orders with nothing written; orders first, repeat and renewal with their hashes and lines, a read without the hash clearing it, a deletion clearing it and keeping ids, times and kind; a customer's deletion clearing its hash and its orders' in that connection only; an order's hash meeting an earlier CRM call's and telephony call's `counterpart_hash`, the lookup by hash using each partial index; permissions and tenant isolation: 12 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.

### Data model: commerce contact link (addition 0008h)

Written on 13 October 2026 (`58_commerce_contact_link.sql`, `59_verify_commerce_contact_link.sql`; brief B10.1). Run and verified locally on PostgreSQL 18.4 on a database built from `00` in README order: `58` twice; `59` 13 PASS, every check failing under provoke, refusing a database without the dev seed; the earlier verifies unchanged apart from `57`, which now also accepts the three columns of 0008h once 0008h is recorded (`docs/b10-1-local-contact-link/local-records/`). Run in dev and prod on 13 October 2026 per `docs/b10-1-local-contact-link/MORNING_RUNBOOK.md` (`records/0008h-dev-2026-10-13`, `records/0008h-prod-2026-10-13`). In dev `59` 13 PASS, provoked all 13 FAIL, and `57` rerun 12 PASS; in prod the reruns of `31`, `33` and `37` PASS. It needs 0008g.

Why (Decision log, 13 October 2026): the HubSpot–Shopify app already writes the Shopify fields on the contact and creates a Self Serve deal per new order, and every property has one writer, so the CMA writes nothing back for a store's customers. The match itself stays, because indirect sales need each order's CRM contact; it is kept in the CMA's own database instead of in HubSpot's contact properties.

| Object | Does |
|---|---|
| `cma.commerce_customer.crm_connection_id`, `crm_contact_source_id`, `matched_at` | the CRM connection and the contact id the customer was matched to, and when; all three set or all three null (a check); a foreign key keeps the connection in the customer's tenant; a contact id is 1 to 100 characters without outer spaces; a partial index on `(tenant_id, crm_connection_id, crm_contact_source_id)` finds a contact's customers |
| `cma.set_commerce_customer_contact(store connection, customer id, CRM connection, contact id)` | the ingest write path (`ingest.write`): links a store's customer to a contact of an active CRM connection of the same tenant (kind `crm`, or unset with only a CRM's rows, 0008e), or clears the link with a null contact; answers `linked`, `relinked`, `unchanged` (nothing written, `matched_at` stays the time of the match), `cleared`, `unlinked` (nothing to clear) or `unknown` (a clear for a customer the store does not hold, so a deletion the CMA never saw does not fail); an unknown customer for a link or an inactive CRM connection `CMA02`, another kind of connection, the store itself or a contact id over 100 characters `CMA04` |
| `cma.forget_commerce_contact_links(CRM connection, contact id)` | the CRM's privacy deletion of a contact (`ingest.write`): clears every customer link to it in the tenant and answers how many; the ingest service calls it next to `cma.ingest_contact_forget` |

No other change: 0007b's tables, upserts and reporting views (the `cma_read.commerce_customer` view leaves the link out), 0007c's outbox and 0008g's hash stay as they are; the 0007b upserts leave the link alone, a customer's deletion included (the ingest service clears it with the deletion). A contact id is an identifier, not personal data in itself; no email, name or number is stored. The ingest service sets the link (`docs/ingest/README.md`, section Shopify): exactly one contact links; none or several clear; a linked contact the CRM deleted or merged into another is found by one batch read at the customer's next read, and the customer is matched again; a customer's deletion clears its link. The verify (`59`) proves structure and privileges (the migration row; the columns, both checks, the foreign key and the index; both functions the application's only and in the caller's rights; nothing else of 0007b, 0007c or 0008g changed) and, on throwaway tenants: a link set with its time, the same link again writing nothing, a relink to another contact and connection (a CRM connection known by its rows), a clear, clearing again and an unknown customer's clear changing nothing, seven refusals with nothing written (an unknown customer, another store's customer, an inactive CRM connection, the store itself even with its kind forced to `crm`, a telephony connection, a connection without kind or rows, an over-long id), the table refusing half a link and another tenant's connection, the upserts of 0007b and 0008g leaving the link alone (a newer read and a deletion), the privacy forget clearing that contact's links only (in every store, another tenant's untouched), permissions and tenant isolation: 13 checks. Dev only, because it writes throwaway tenants next to the dev seed.
