---

### API: the web app's data layer (since 5 October 2026)

The web app reads and writes Postgres **in-process**: screens and route handlers call `data()` from `src/lib/data`, which returns the Postgres implementation when `CMA_DATA_MODE=api` and the in-memory mock otherwise. There is no HTTP hop between the screens and the database. The ingest API for Make and n8n (Roadmap step 3) is a separate Cloud Run service with its own service account and per-tenant keys; it can lift `src/lib/db/client.ts`.

**Rules in every call.** One transaction per call with `app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id` and `app.actor_label` set by `set_config(…, true)`, so a pooled connection can never carry one request's tenant into the next. Own data only through the database: every read filters on `cma.current_user_id()`, the transaction's acting user, never on an id passed in. The database clock decides: `open_workday()`, `set_status()` and `end_workday()` are called without a time. Today is the business date in the user's zone, computed in SQL. Minutes on screen are working seconds rounded down per day (which hours the agent sees stays open, see Open decisions). Errors keep their SQLSTATE; SQL text never reaches a screen.

**Login (`findPrincipal`).** `cma.find_tenants_for_identity(provider, subject)` without a tenant; exactly one tenant continues, none or several (no tenant picker yet) is "no access yet". Inside that tenant the `app_user` row, its employer, its zone (`cma.user_timezone`), its role and, since 6 October 2026, its permission keys (`cma.user_permissions`) for showing or hiding screens: screens check a permission, never a role key, and the database checks again on every call. A user without a role is "no access yet". With several roles the earliest grant is shown, without ranking role names. A database that cannot be reached is an error, never "no access".

**JSON API**, versioned, behind the same gate and identity as the screens, no tenant parameter anywhere and no user parameter on the `/me` routes, `cache-control: no-store`:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/me` | who the caller is: name, tenant, employer, role, zone | `findPrincipal` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/day?date=YYYY-MM-DD` | the caller's workday, default today in their zone; reading never clocks in; `data` is null when there is no day. Since addition 0008b the day carries `dayEndsAt`, the window end of its date, where the clock stops counting | `getWorkday` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/day/start` | Clock in (since 7 October 2026, increment e1): opens the caller's day today, the same path as the Clock in button. Answers the day in every case: opened now, already open, or ended and unchanged, so a client compares `startedAt` rather than status codes. Refused by the database for someone whose time is not kept (`CMA06` → 403), and since addition 0008b before the workday window starts (`CMA04` → 400, message "the workday starts at HH:MI", the one database text the route passes on, so the card can say when the day starts) | `startWorkday` → `cma.open_workday()` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/day/end` | ends the caller's day today, same path as the button; an ended day is returned unchanged | `endWorkday` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/hours?from=&to=` | the caller's hours per day, at most 92 days per request | `getHours` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/statuses` | the work statuses the caller can choose: the tenant's active `work_status` list in its order, with `key`, `name`, `isWorking`, `isProductive` (since 7 October 2026, for the status colour) and `isDefault`; pay and billing flags are not exposed | `listStatuses` |
| `POST /api/v1/me/status` | sets the caller's status on today's day: body `{ "key": "…" }` with a key from `/statuses`; answers the day. Never opens a day: no day today or an unknown key is 404, an ended day 409 (changing it is a correction) | `setStatus` → `cma.set_status()` |
| `GET /api/v1/settings` | the tenant's effective settings: every catalog key with its value and `isDefault`; any person of the tenant (since 7 October 2026) | `listSettings` → `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/app-links` | the app links the caller may see (since 7 October 2026, increment e1): the tenant's buttons to other applications, filtered by the database on the caller's permissions, in the tenant's order; `key`, `label`, `address` | `listAppLinks` → `cma.app_links()` |

**Team routes (since 6 October 2026)**, for people holding `workday.team` (the exports `workday.export`, time per status `performance.team`, the team now `monitoring.live`). They take a user id; whether the caller may use it is answered by the database function in the same transaction (`CMA06` → 403), never by the route alone:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/team/hours?from=&to=&userId=` | hours per person per day (all people of the tenant, or one), with employer, zone, working and paid minutes and the capped, needs-correction and has-correction flags; at most 92 days | `getTeamHours` → `cma.team_hours()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/people` | the people whose time is kept (active, holding `workday.own`) with employer and zone, for Add day and the person filter | `listTeamPeople` → `cma.team_people()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/days/{userId}/{date}` | one person's day and every event of it, effective or not, with reason and approver: `{ day, events }`, `day` null when there is none | `getTeamDay` → `cma.team_hours()`, `cma.team_day()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/days/{userId}/{date}/corrections` | body `{ "reason": "…", "changes": [ { kind, at, statusKey, supersedes } ] }`; on a date without a day this is Add day. The caller is the approver. Answers `{ day, events }` | `correctWorkday` → `cma.correct_workday()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/exports/hours?from=&to=&userId=` | hours per person per day as a CSV download (since 7 October 2026), for people holding `workday.export`; at most 92 days | `exportHours` → `cma.export_hours()`, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/status-time?from=&to=&userId=` | time per status (since 7 October 2026, increment d1): one row per person per business date per status with key, name, sort order, active, the four flags, seconds, stretches and capped, for people holding `performance.team`; at most 92 days. Named after the data, not the screen | `getTeamStatusTime` → `cma.team_status_time()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/exports/status-changes?from=&to=&userId=` | one row per stretch in a status as a CSV download, same permission and bound | `exportStatusChanges` → `cma.export_status_changes()`, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/now` | the team now (since 7 October 2026, increment b): one entry per person whose time is kept with employer, zone, today in that zone, `day` (today's day shaped exactly as `GET /api/v1/me/day`, null without a day) and `status` (the current status with name, active and the four flags; null without a day and once ended), plus `statusFlags`, the tenant's active statuses' working, productive and paid flags for the board's tiles (keys and names left out). No parameters, today only, the whole tenant until teams exist; for people holding `monitoring.live`. Named after the data, not the screen | `getTeamNow` → `cma.team_now()`, then the active `work_status` flags in the same transaction |


**Team screen routes (night build, 8 October 2026, migration 0004)**, for people holding `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`; the database decides who is listed and who may be edited (`CMA06` → 403). `GET /api/v1/team/people` stays the time-kept list for Add day, so the directory has its own path:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/team/directory` | the people with employer, zone, role, `isManaging`, `timeKept`, teams, skills with levels and `mayEdit` | `listDirectory` → `cma.directory()` |
| `POST /api/v1/team/directory` | add a person: `{ email, displayName, organisationKey, roleKey, loginSystem, loginId, timeZone? }`; answers `{ userId }` with 201, the same id for the same person again, 409 for a different id or an inactive person, 403 for a managing role without `users.manage_all` | `addPerson` → `cma.add_person()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/role` | `{ roleKey }`; own role or a managing role from a manager 403 | `setPersonRole` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/active` | `{ active }`; oneself 403 | `setPersonActive` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/teams` | `{ teamKeys }`, the full list; unknown team 404 | `setPersonTeams` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/skills` | `{ skills: [ { key, level? } ] }`, the full list; needs `skills.manage` too; a level outside the scale 400 | `setPersonSkills` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/roles`, `/teams`, `/skills`, `/organisations` | the ladder with `assignable`, the current teams with member counts, the catalog with its scale per dimension, the active employers with their zone | `listRoles`, `listTeams`, `listSkills`, `listOrganisations` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/directory/linked-accounts` | the ids a person can hold (`systems`: `<adapter>_owner` per customer-records connection, `<adapter>_user` per telephony connection, with the connections' names and whether one is active) and the ids held by the people the caller may see (`accounts`: user id, system, id); since addition 0008e | `getLinkedAccounts` → `cma.linked_account_systems()`, `cma.linked_accounts()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/linked-accounts` | `{ system, externalId }`, `externalId` null or empty unlinks; a person the caller may not edit 403 (also oneself), an id another person holds 409, a system that is not `<name>_owner` or `<name>_user` or an id with spaces 400; answers the caller's linked accounts; since addition 0008e | `setLinkedAccount` → `cma.set_user_external_id()`, `cma.remove_user_external_id()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/memberships` | current team memberships of everyone, for the Live board's team filter | `listTeamMembersNow` → `cma.team_members_now()` |
| `GET /api/v1/team/shifts-today` | today's published roster entry per person whose time is kept (migration 0005), for the board's shift line; `monitoring.live` | `getRosterToday` → `cma.roster_today()` |

**Roster routes (night build, 8 October 2026, migration 0005)**, for people holding `roster.manage` except where stated; a week is named by its Monday:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/roster/weeks?team=&from=&to=` | the weeks of a team (or the tenant without `team`) in the range as a calendar, one row per week with state, version and counts (an unwritten week is a draft at version 0); the planner's browser shows the written ones; at most two years | `listRosterWeeks` → `cma.roster_weeks()` |
| `GET /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}?team=` | the planner's week: `{ header, people, entries, coverage }` | `getRosterWeek` → `roster_week()`, `roster_people()`, `roster_entries()`, `roster_coverage()` |
| `PUT /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/cells` | `{ team?, userId, date, cell }` with `cell` as `{ kind: "shift", start, end, note? }`, `{ kind: "absence", absenceKey, note? }` or `{ kind: "clear" }`; answers the current cell, null after a clear; the past, a date outside the week, a person off the grid, bad times 400; another roster that day 409; unknown absence 404 | `setRosterEntry` → `cma.roster_set_entry()` |
| `POST /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/publish` | `{ team? }`; answers the header with its new version | `publishRoster` → `cma.roster_publish()` |
| `POST /api/v1/roster/weeks/{monday}/copy` | `{ team?, from }`; answers `{ copied }` | `copyRosterWeek` → `cma.roster_copy_week()` |
| `GET /api/v1/roster/absence-types` | the active absence types in order; any person | `listAbsenceTypes` → `cma.absence_types()` |
| `GET /api/v1/me/roster?from=&to=` | the caller's own schedule, published entries only, one row per date with `isPublished`; `roster.view`; at most 92 days | `getMyRoster` → `cma.my_roster()` |

**Configuration routes (night build, 9 October 2026, migration 0005a)**, for people holding `tenant.configure` (the admin in the default ladder), named after the data; every write answers the full list as the database now holds it, so a screen never shows a guess:

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/statuses`, `PUT` | every status with its four flags, the default, active or retired and its usage; `PUT { key, name, isWorking, isProductive, isPaid, isBillable, sortOrder }` adds, changes or reactivates; frozen flags and a non-working default 409 | `listConfigStatuses`, `upsertConfigStatus` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/statuses/{key}/default`, `…/retired` | the default (an active working status, else 409); retire (the default or the last working status 409) | `setDefaultStatus`, `retireStatus` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/app-links`, `PUT`, `PUT …/{key}/retired`, `GET /api/v1/configuration/permissions` | the links, active or retired; `PUT { key, label, address, permissionKey, sortOrder }` (https only 400, unknown permission 404); the permission catalog for the choice | `listConfigAppLinks`, `upsertAppLink`, `retireAppLink`, `listPermissions` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/absence-types`, `PUT`, `PUT …/{key}/retired` | the absence types, active or retired; `PUT { key, name, isPaid, sortOrder }` | `listAbsenceTypes`, `upsertAbsenceType`, `retireAbsenceType` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/coverage-targets`, `PUT` | every target across teams; `PUT { teamKey, skillKey, weekday 1..7, minCount 0..99 }`, 0 clears; needs `roster.manage` as well | `listCoverageTargets`, `setCoverageTarget` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/teams`, `PUT …/{key}/dissolved`, `PUT /api/v1/configuration/skills`, `PUT …/{dimension}/levels` | `{ key, name, markets, sortOrder }` adds, changes or revives a team; dissolve ends it and its memberships; `{ dimension, key, name, sortOrder, active }` adds, changes, retires or reactivates a skill; `{ levels: [ { level, name } ] }` replaces a dimension's scale (empty is binary; levels in use 409) | `upsertTeam`, `dissolveTeam`, `upsertSkill`, `setSkillLevels` |
| `PUT /api/v1/configuration/settings` | `{ key, value }` writes one tenant setting, `value` null resets it to the catalog default (unknown key 404, a value outside the catalog 400); reading stays at `GET /api/v1/settings` | `setTenantSetting` → `cma.set_tenant_setting()` |

`GET /api/v1/roster/absence-types` and the planner leave retired absence types out since 0005a (nothing could retire one before).

**Intake report routes (B11, 12 October 2026, migration 0007d)**, read only, named after the data. Each route answers 403 itself when the caller holds none of its function's permissions, and the function checks again in the same transaction (`CMA06` → 403); every range is at most 92 days, refused with 400 by the route and again by the database (`CMA04`). Ids, times, codes and counts only: no customer name, email or phone number, and an owner id is never returned as an agent:

| Route | Does | Permission | Data |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/reports/speed-to-lead?from=&to=&group=` | per group (`day`, `market`, `agent` or `pipeline`; `day` when left out, anything else 400): deals, called, median and 80th percentile business minutes over the called deals, % within target and % before the next noon over the deals whose outcome is known, open not called; an agent whose owner is not mapped is the key `unknown` with a null label | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getSpeedToLeadSummary` → `cma.speed_to_lead_summary()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/speed-to-lead/rows?from=&to=` | one row per lead deal: the deal's and the first call's ids in the CRM, market and its source, pipeline, status (`called`, `not_called_open`, `not_called_closed`), the call times, the agent's user id (null when not mapped), the measures, and `recordUrl` from the connection's `record_url_deal` template (null without one) | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getSpeedToLeadRows` → `cma.speed_to_lead_rows()`, `cma.record_url()` (0008) |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/intake?from=&to=` | per business date and market: form submissions, deals and tickets created and closed, calls in and out, tagged calls, new customers, first and repeat orders, renewals; with `connections` (`telephony`, `commerce`) and the tenant's `startDate` (`intake.start_date`). A telephony figure is null when the tenant has no telephony connected, a commerce figure null without a shop: never 0 for a source that is not there | `reports.view` or `performance.team` | `getIntake` → `cma.intake_per_day()`, the connection state, `cma.tenant_settings()` |
| `GET /api/v1/reports/data-quality` | every check of `cma.data_quality_checks()` in its order with key, description and total (null for a check whose source is not installed) | `reports.view` or `tenant.configure` | `getDataQuality` → `cma.data_quality()` |

The connection state is read in the same transaction, after the report function has checked the permission, under row-level security: telephony counts as connected when an active connection has a line in the telephony catalog (`telephony_number`) or a telephony call (0007a), commerce when an active connection has its store (`commerce_store`, 0007b, one store per commerce connection). No function answers this for a report reader: `cma.connections_all()` is `tenant.configure` only and names adapters, which are vendor values. The Dashboard's Intake row also reads `getCrmRecordsPerDay` → `cma.crm_records_per_day()` (0006, `performance.team`) for the deals and tickets created; it has no route of its own.

**Sources, markets and alert rules routes (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e)**, for people holding `tenant.configure`; each route checks the permission itself and the function checks it again (`CMA06` → 403). Every write goes through the function that existed for it (Data model: sources configuration) and answers what the database now holds. No route accepts or returns a secret value: a connection shows the names of its secrets only, and a setting key containing secret, token, password or key is refused (400) before the database refuses it too. A connection is never created here, because a connection names its secrets (the runbook does that).

| Route | Does | Data |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/sources` | every connection with id, adapter, name, account id, kind, the kinds its rows imply, status, secret names, non-secret settings, events in 24 hours, waiting, parked, the last event and its catalog sizes | `listSources` → `cma.sources()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/sources/{id}`, `PUT` | one connection with its pipelines, forms, call outcomes, fields, write-back fields, lines, tags, store and record link samples; a malformed id 400, an unknown one 404. `PUT { name?, isActive?, kind? }`: the name, switching it off or on, the kind (one the rows contradict 409) | `getSource`, `setSourceConnection` → `cma.sources()`, `cma.connection_catalogs()`, `cma.connection_config()`; `upsert_connection` with the stored secret names, `set_connection_status`, `set_connection_kind` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/settings` | `{ settings }`, every non-secret setting as a flat object of text, numbers and switches, at most 4 kB (the screen previews a record link template `record_url_<record type>` with the newest record id and shows no link unless the result is a plain https address) | `setSourceSettings` → `cma.set_connection_settings()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/pipelines` | `{ recordType, pipelineId, isCounted, isLead }`; lead for deals only (400) | `setSourcePipeline` → `cma.set_connection_pipeline()` (current routing kept), `cma.set_pipeline_lead()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/forms` | `{ forms: [ { formId, isCounted, leadSource, market } ] }`, one form or a rule's whole preview in one transaction (current kept fields kept) | `setSourceForms` → `cma.set_form()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/call-outcomes` | `{ outcomes: [ { outcomeRef, isConnected } ] }`, one or a rule's preview in one transaction | `setSourceCallOutcomes` → `cma.set_call_outcome()` |
| `PUT …/sources/{id}/lines`, `…/tags`, `…/store` | `{ lineRef, market, isCounted }`; `{ tagRef, isCounted }`; `{ handle, name, market, currency, timeZone }` (a handle another store holds 409) | `setSourceLine`, `setSourceTag`, `setSourceStore` → `cma.set_telephony_number()`, `cma.set_telephony_tag()`, `cma.upsert_commerce_store()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/markets`, `PUT` | every market (inactive ones too) with its aliases, the office hours per market and weekday (`*` the default) and the holidays; `PUT { code, name, timeZone, languageCode, currency, languageSkillKey, minLanguageLevel, sortOrder, active }` adds or changes one | `getMarkets`, `upsertMarket` → `cma.markets_all()`, `cma.business_hours_all()`, `cma.business_holidays_all()`; `cma.upsert_market()`, `cma.set_market_status()` |
| `PUT …/markets/aliases`, `…/hours`, `…/holidays` | `{ alias, code }` (`code` null removes); `{ market, days: [ { weekday, hours } ] }` (`hours` like `9:00-17:30`, empty for closed; `market` `*` the default); `{ market, day, name }` (`name` null removes) | `setMarketAlias`, `setOfficeHours`, `setHoliday` → `cma.set_market_alias()`, `cma.remove_market_alias()`, `cma.set_business_hours()`, `cma.set_business_holiday()`, `cma.remove_business_holiday()` |
| `GET /api/v1/configuration/alert-rules`, `PUT` | every rule of both triggers with its alerts in 30 days, the pipelines a rule can name and the default age limit (`alerts.max_age_minutes`); `PUT { name, trigger, recordType, pipelineIds, urgency, maxAgeMinutes, audiencePermission?, matchLanguage?, onlyClockedIn?, fallbackPermission? }` (the audience for new-record rules only; a name another trigger's rule holds 409) | `getAlertRules`, `upsertAlertRule` → `cma.message_rules()`; `cma.upsert_message_rule()`, `cma.upsert_assignment_rule()` |
| `PUT …/alert-rules/{id}/enabled` | `{ enabled }` switches a rule off or on; rules are never deleted | `setAlertRuleEnabled` → `cma.set_message_rule_enabled()` |

Status codes:
 401 no identity, 403 no access, `CMA01` 403, `CMA02` 404, `CMA03` 409 (an ended day, or since 0004 and 0005 a conflict: an existing person with other details, another roster's entry that day), `CMA04` and invalid input 400, `CMA05` 500, `CMA06` 403, database unreachable 503. Every POST and PUT requires the header `x-cma-request: 1` and refuses a cross-site `Sec-Fetch-Site`, so another website cannot end someone's day, change their status, correct a day, change a role or plan a shift through their IAP session; `/logout/end` keeps its same-origin check.

**Status and clock (since 6 October 2026).** A workday carries `statusKey`, `statusSince` (start of the current status) and `clock`: `closedSeconds` worked in closed stretches plus `runningSince` while the current status has `is_working`, read from `cma.time_interval`. Nothing in it changes between two status changes, so the screen ticks locally and two reads of an unchanged day are equal. The clock counts the same time as My hours (`is_working`), so it pauses on Break and Lunch with the seeded flags. My day shows the tenant's statuses as buttons in the tenant's order; no key or name is in code or copy. Keyboard: digits switch pages, S moves into the status buttons, arrows and Enter choose. The buttons call `POST /api/v1/me/status`, the path the verifier proves.

**Team hours (since 6 October 2026, increment c2).** A Report screen at `/team/hours`, shown to people holding `workday.team` and checked again by the database on every read and correction. Page keys follow the visible order of the pages a person may see (agents 1 and 2, supervisors 1 to 3). The period bar (today, week, month, custom range up to 92 days; T, W, M, C) is shared with My hours; a person filter (P) lists `team_people`. One row per person per day: date, person, employer, clocked in, clocked out, worked (`is_working`) and paid (`is_paid`) minutes, note chips (Still clocked in, Not clocked out, Needs a correction, Corrected afterwards) and Correct; totals below. Times are shown in each day's own zone, with the zone's short name when the person's clock differs from the viewer's. Correct is disabled on the viewer's own rows. **Correct a day** opens the day's effective events as rows in the day's zone: a 24-hour `hh:mm` time (905, 9:05 and 0905 all become 09:05), a status from the tenant's list or Clocked out, Remove and Add row; the earliest row is the clock-in. On the repeated autumn hour the editor asks which of the two times is meant, a time in the skipped spring hour is refused, and a time in the future, a clock-out first, two clock-outs or anything after the clock-out are explained before saving. Saving sends the smallest diff to the corrections route (a changed row replaces its event, a removed row voids it, a new row adds one, the start first) with one reason of 3 to 500 characters; the history of the day shows every event with source, approver, reason and replaced rows struck through. **Add day** (A) asks for a person (not oneself) and a date up to today and opens the same editor. Keyboard: Down or Up enters the table, the arrows move between days, Enter opens the editor, Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes (twice with unsaved changes) and returns focus to the row (c2.1). The logic lives in `web/src/lib/corrections.ts`, pure and without runtime imports. Mock mode has a fictional team of five over two employers and three zones, with a day still open, a forgotten clock-out, a missing day and a corrected day; the dev seed's `supervisor` and `manager` identities hold `workday.team` there.

**Exports (since 7 October 2026, increment c3).** Team hours shows Download hours (H) and Download status changes (X) to people holding `workday.export`; the files cover the period and person on screen. The export function checks the permission and the tenant's settings are read in the same transaction, so a file never mixes two configurations. The format follows the settings (separator, decimal mark, date format, duration format, byte order mark), with CRLF line ends and RFC 4180 quoting; a decimal comma with a comma separator stays one quoted cell. Times are 24-hour `hh:mm` in each day's own zone, the zone named per row; durations are whole minutes first (as on screen), then written in the chosen format. Column names follow the viewer's language from `copy.ts`. Every text cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or carriage return gets a leading apostrophe, so a name typed by a person never runs as a formula. Answers carry `content-disposition: attachment`, `no-store` and `nosniff`; the file name holds only the dates. Hours file (provisional until compared with Steam's figures, see Open decisions): date, person, employer, time zone, clocked in, clocked out, worked, paid, billable, not clocked out, corrected afterwards. Status-changes file: date, person, employer, time zone, status, from, to (empty while open), duration, working, paid, billable, entered by (person, system, correction, import since addition 0008d), not clocked out. Known limit: on the autumn clock-change night a time between 02:00 and 02:59 reads twice; durations stay right. Mock mode uses Dutch conventions as fixture settings; the mock `manager` holds `workday.export`, the mock `supervisor` does not, as in the default ladder.

**Welcome and Clock in (since 7 October 2026, increment e1).** The landing page at `/` for everyone with a role, built as blocks per permission: the greeting by time of day in the person's zone with the display name as stored (there is no first-name field, and splitting a name is a guess), the date (the shift line comes with the roster); Updates for everyone (an empty state until Messaging, migration 0008, then the demo's list with the urgent chip, title, body, time and sender); Before today's shift for `workday.team`, Steam's to-do line: the days not clocked out in the last 31 days from Team hours (`needsCorrection`, today excluded) with a link to Team hours; Reports for `performance.team`, a link to the Dashboard that grows into the one view of all KPIs and metrics for analytics (Roadmap step 9, each KPI as its source connects); the Clock in card for `workday.own` when there is no day today (the tenant's default status name from `/me/statuses`, the button focused so Enter clocks in, `POST /api/v1/me/day/start`), the open day's state otherwise (status with its colour, clocked in at, time worked so far, a link to My day), or the ended day; and the tenant's app links, each in a new tab. A visit opens nothing: the hand check in prod no longer opens the checker's day, IAP's silent re-login and an evening look at hours open nothing, and someone being added gets no day until they clock in. My day moved to `/day` and shows the same Clock in card when there is no day, so page 2 never dead-ends on an empty status grid; Clock out stays on My day and Log out. The end-of-day texts say "clock in tomorrow" instead of "log in". Mock mode has fictional links, one of them for `workday.team` only.

**Messages (since 11 October 2026, migration 0008, brief B12).** `GET /api/v1/me/messages?since=<ISO instant>&limit=` lists the caller's own messages newest first with `unreadCount` (the whole unread count, not only the listed ones); the first return of a message sets its delivered time; `since` lists only messages created after it, which is what the open Workspace polls. `POST /api/v1/me/messages/read` with `{ ids }` marks own deliveries read and answers how many changed (other people's ids change nothing); `POST /api/v1/me/messages/<id>/ack` acknowledges one own message (404 when it is not the caller's). `POST /api/v1/messages` with `{ title, body?, urgency?, target }` sends a message for `messages.send`: the target names exactly one of `{ "everyone": true }`, `{ "teams": [keys] }`, `{ "language": { "skill", "minLevel" } }` or `{ "users": [ids] }`, optionally with `"onlyClockedIn": true`; the body is checked by the pure `src/lib/messages.ts` before the database (a 400 with a code), the database resolves the recipients and refuses a target that reaches nobody (CMA04, 400) or an unknown team, skill or person (CMA02, 404); the answer is the id and the recipient count (201). `GET /api/v1/messages/stats?from&to` (at most 92 days) gives per message recipients, delivered, read, acknowledged and the median seconds to read, for `messages.send` or `performance.team`. The screens: `MessageCenter.tsx` (client, in the Shell's top bar for everyone: the poll loop, the badge and title, toasts, desktop notifications, the urgent dialog), `Inbox.tsx` with `src/app/messages/page.tsx`, `MessageComposer.tsx` with `src/app/messages/send/page.tsx` (the sent list is server-rendered from the stats); `verify/messages.mjs` (24 checks) proves the validation and the poller's decisions. In mock mode three fixture messages exist per tenant and a synthetic "New deal" alert arrives every three minutes, so the toast and the inbox can be seen without a source.

**Live board (since 7 October 2026, increment b).** A Live screen at `/live/board`, the first page of a new Live group, shown to people holding `monitoring.live` (supervisor, manager and analytics in the default ladder) and checked again by the database on every read. One read per page (`team_now`); everything else is computed in `src/lib/live.ts`, pure, with `web/verify/live.mjs`. Tiles with counts: the flag groups through the Dashboard's rule (`groupOf` in `lib/dashboard`, imported with its `.ts` extension so Node's type stripping can run the verifier; one definition for both screens), shown only for a group that at least one active status belongs to (Pulse4all has no Paid pause tile), plus Clocked out (today's day ended) and Not clocked in (no day today); a tile is also a filter. Then one row per person: name, employer, status with its colour dot (an inactive status marked), since, clocked in and clocked out in the person's own zone with the zone's short name only when the person's clock differs from the viewer's (Madrid next to Amsterdam shows none, London shows BST), worked so far; rows by group then name, no ranking (the employee-monitoring decision). Since and worked so far tick each second on the client from `statusSince` and the clock's inputs, as My day does; the page re-reads itself with `router.refresh()` every 10 seconds while the tab is visible, pauses while hidden and reads once more on return (the fallback until the realtime service). Filters: Group (G) and Employer (E), client-side, kept across refreshes; team later (0004). Reserved, not shown: today's shift (roster, 0005) and the on-call state from telephony (Sync, step 3). In mock mode today's fictional team is laid out relative to now, so every state shows at any hour. Compliance: live per-person status to supervisors, managers and analytics is monitoring in the Spanish and Dutch sense; the board goes to Yordi together with the Dashboard point before real agent hours (Open decisions). Checked in prod on 7 October 2026 (`a2b805e`): two people, Martin in Available - Operations with the clock's inputs equal to his own-day read, Finn without a day, seven status flags, no Paid pause tile.

**People (since the night build of 8 October 2026, migration 0004).** A Team screen at `/team/people`, the first page of a new **Team** group (people icon) after Live and before Time, shown to people holding `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all` and checked again by the database on every read and write. One row per person: name and email, employer, role (with "manages people" under a managing one), teams and skills as chips (`Dutch · Fluent`, `Sales`), Active or Inactive and whether the clock runs, and Edit, disabled on yourself and, for a manager, on managing roles, as `may_edit` says. Filters: team (T) and Show inactive (I). **Edit person** is one native dialog: the role from the assignable part of the ladder (the held role shown but not choosable when the caller may not assign it), the active flag, the teams as checkboxes, the skills per dimension (a level per language from the tenant's scale, yes or no per work type), read-only without `skills.manage`; Save sends the smallest set of writes (`src/lib/team.ts` `editChanges`: the role, the active flag, the full team list, the full skill list, each only when it changed, the active flag last), Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes (twice with unsaved changes). **Add a person** (A) takes name, email, employer (from `cma.organisations()`), role (assignable only), the sign-in provider (prefilled with the one the app runs with, `google` behind IAP, `mock` in dev, as a data value) and the account id, written once and never shown again, and an optional zone; the database writes user, login id and role in one statement. Access requests are out: the manager adds people. Keyboard: Down or Up enters the table, the arrows move, Enter opens the row. Pure logic in `src/lib/team.ts` with `web/verify/team.mjs` (18 checks, provoked). Mock mode has a fictional team with teams and skills, a `manager` (`users.manage_agents`, `skills.manage`, `roster.manage`) and an `admin` identity. **Linked accounts (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e):** Edit person shows one field per id the tenant's customer-records and telephony connections use (`Owner id in <connection>`, `User id in <connection>`, and an id still held for a system whose connection is gone as `Id in <system>`), saved with the dialog through `PUT /api/v1/team/directory/{userId}/linked-accounts` (one call per changed id; an emptied field unlinks). Whoever may edit the person may link them: the data layer reads `may_edit` from `cma.directory()` and the database applies the People rule again; nobody links their own ids on this screen, while the database lets an admin link their own (a seed or Studio path). An id another person holds answers 409 and the dialog says so without changing anything. The list gains a **Linked** column, `n of total` per person with a header hint. Ids are typed as the system shows them; there is no owner sync (Open decisions). `team.mjs` has 26 checks since.

**Roster planner, print view and My schedule (since the night build of 8 October 2026, migration 0005).** The **planner** at `/roster/planner` is the second page of the Team group (`roster.manage`): a week bar (previous, this, next week as links, P, W, N, the ISO week number and its dates), the team (T; the tenant's first team by default, or Everyone for a tenant without teams), a browser of the saved weeks with their states, and the grid: people by seven days, one text field per cell, today underlined. Typing `9-17:30`, `9.30-18`, `0830-1700`, `16 to 24` or an absence by key, name or unambiguous start (`si` is Sick) and pressing Enter, or leaving the cell, parses it (`src/lib/roster.ts` `parseCell`) and saves it at once through the cells route; the database keeps every version. Each cell shows its own state (saving, saved, a problem with its reason: the format, an end before the start, no such absence, the past, a conflict with another team's roster, a person off the grid), so nothing is lost silently and there is no unsaved state to guard; a cell in the past is read-only. Enter and the arrows move down and up a column, Tab across, Esc reverts. Per person the planned hours of the week, per day the headcount. **Publish** (U, with a confirmation) makes the week visible to agents as it stands; the header shows Draft or Published as version n on date by person, and "changed since the last publish" after a later edit until Publish changes. **Copy previous week** (C) fills this week from the one before. **Coverage per work type** below the grid: per work type per day the people on shift who hold it, as Nobody, One person (a single-person dependency), n of target (short) or Covered, with the names on hover. The **print view** (X, a new tab at `/roster/planner/print?week=&team=`) is the same grid without the shell on an A4 landscape sheet (the 1280px gate never applies on paper: `DesktopOnly` carries `print:` variants since the first print in prod on 8 October 2026) per the style guide's document rules (Deep Blue title with a thin divider, Background Blue table header, the logo, a footer with the date), with the version or a draft note and the note that the agents see an earlier version when the week changed; the Print button opens the browser's print dialog, whose Save as file gives the PDF. **My schedule** at `/schedule` (Time group, after My hours, `roster.view`): the person's own week from the published rosters in their own zone, one row per day with the shift, the absence, No shift or Not published yet, the note and the team, today marked, the planned total; week bar P, W, N. The **Live board** gained a Team column and filter (T) from the memberships and the shift line: today's published entry per person (`09:00–17:30`, the absence, No shift, Not published) with a flag derived in the web from the plan and the clock (`src/lib/roster.ts` `adherence`): Late (clocked in after the start plus the tolerance), Left early, Expected (the start has passed, nobody clocked in), Absent (an absence planned, on the clock anyway shows Not planned), with `roster.adherence_tolerance_minutes` from the tenant's settings. **Welcome** gained the shift sentence the greeting waited for: Your shift today: 09:00–17:30, Today: Leave, No shift planned for today, or Today's roster has not been published yet, for people holding `roster.view`. Pure logic in `src/lib/roster.ts` with `web/verify/roster.mjs` (31 checks, provoked); `web/verify/live.mjs` grew to 26 with the team filter. Mock mode mirrors the dev fixture: this week published for Team NL and Team Nordics with one cell changed afterwards, next week a draft.

**Configuration screens (since the night build of 9 October 2026, migration 0005a).** A **Configuration** group (gear icon) after Reports, shown to people holding `tenant.configure` (the admin in the default ladder; agents, supervisors and managers never see it) and checked again by the database on every read and write. Its pages carry no digit key (the admin's rail already has nine numbered pages): the group opens from the rail and with `C`, which focuses its first page so Enter opens it (the `C` keycap shows only while the group is open, Martin 9 October 2026; the key works either way and the button carries `aria-keyshortcuts`); a page's own control with the same letter wins (the planner's Copy previous week, the period bar's Custom range), a one-line precedence rule in `useKeyboardShortcuts` (controls inside `main` before the rail). Five pages, each one card with native dialogs (Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc closes, A adds, R shows or hides retired rows): **Statuses** (`/configuration/statuses`: the list with its four flags in one line each and their meaning, the default, usage, add, edit, reorder, Make default, Retire and Reactivate; the dialog freezes the flags of a status with time behind it and says why; the rules the database applies are explained before sending, from `src/lib/configuration.ts`), **Teams and skills** (`/configuration/teams`: the current teams with markets, members and order, add, edit, dissolve with a confirmation; the skills per dimension with the level scale as one comma-separated line of names, add, edit, retire, reactivate), **Absences and coverage** (`/configuration/absences`: the absence types with the paid flag; the coverage targets as a grid per team (T): one row per work type, one cell per weekday, saved when the cell is left or Enter is pressed, 0 or empty clears), **Exports** (`/configuration/exports`: the five CSV settings of 0003b as choices saved at once, each with Use default, and a preview of the hours file's header and one fictional row in the chosen format, byte order mark named; plus the two minute settings, the adherence tolerance and the forgotten clock-out grace), **App links** (`/configuration/app-links`: label, address, who sees it from the permission catalog, order; add, edit, retire, reactivate). Mock mode keeps a configuration copy of the fixture catalogs on `globalThis` with the same refusals as the database. Pure logic in `src/lib/configuration.ts` with `web/verify/configuration.mjs` (30 checks, provoked).

**Sources, Markets and Alert rules (night of 13 October 2026, addition 0008e).** Three more pages in the Configuration group (`tenant.configure`, checked by the page, the route and the database), so the intake configuration Martin set by SQL on 12 October 2026 is a screen for any tenant and any connection kind the data model knows (`crm`, `telephony`, `commerce`, `automation`); nothing on them names a vendor, pipeline, form, market, owner or outcome, and they show no customer data, only ids, counts and names from the source's own catalogs. **Sources** (`/configuration/sources`): every connection, one row each, with its vendor as stored, kind, account id, status, events in 24 hours, waiting and parked events and the last event; a row opens the connection's page (`/configuration/sources/{id}`; another tenant's or an unknown id gives a calm "does not exist" card). There: the connection (name with N, vendor, account id read-only, kind chosen from those its rows allow with Set kind, Switch off with a confirmation, the address key and the names of its signing and access secrets, read-only and never a value), the non-secret settings (S adds one; a key that names a secret is refused before sending; a record link template `record_url_<record type>` shows its live preview with the newest record id), then the sections its kind has: **Pipelines** (record type, label, Counted, Lead for deal pipelines only, each switch's effect in its header hint), **Forms** (name, Counted, lead source, market picker; F applies a rule by name pattern), **Call outcomes** (label, Connected; O applies a rule), **Fields** read-only with the hint that a change goes through the runbook, the write-back fields read-only, **Lines** (market, Counted; unticking Counted asks first, because a line that does not count keeps no calls and switching it off removes its stored calls, addition 0008f: the confirmation opens on Cancel, so Enter or Esc cancels, its confirm button is the outlined error style without a keycap, and the box stays ticked until the switch-off is confirmed and saved; ticking it again does not ask) and **Tags** (Counted) for telephony, the **Store** (A: handle, name, market, currency, time zone) for a shop; an automation connection has its settings only. A rule dialog takes the words a name contains and does not contain and what the rule sets (forms: counted, the lead source from the first matching word in the name, the market from the name's two-letter prefix read through the tenant's aliases, a name without a prefix keeping its market, and optionally the non-matching forms set to not counted; outcomes: connected or not, and optionally the opposite for the rest), shows every change row by row before anything is saved, and Save sends exactly that preview, written in one transaction (`src/lib/sources.ts` holds the plans the preview and the verifier share). **Markets** (`/configuration/markets`): the markets with zone, language, currency, language skill and order (A adds, an inactive market stays listed), their aliases as chips (I adds, × removes), the office hours per weekday as text (`9:00-17:30`, empty for closed) with the tenant's default row and a market's own week in a dialog that starts from the default ("Use the default" clears a market's own hours: a market with hours of its own uses only those), and the holidays (H). **Alert rules** (`/configuration/alert-rules`, brief B14): the rules of both triggers, each with its effect in plain words under it ("When a deal in <pipeline> gets an owner who is linked to a person on People, that person gets one alert per record and owner.", followed by the urgency and the sound), its alerts in 30 days and the last one; A adds, Edit changes record type, pipelines, urgency (the sound follows: urgent chimes and pops up), the age limit and for a new-record rule the audience, with the effect previewed in the dialog; Switch off and on; never deleted, and name and trigger fixed once the rule exists. **Linked accounts** on People: Edit person shows a Linked accounts section with one field per id the tenant's connections use (`Owner id in <CRM connection>`, `User id in <telephony connection>`), saved through `set_user_external_id` and editable by whoever may edit that person (a manager the agents, an admin everyone; nobody their own ids on screen, where the own row's Edit stays disabled as before); an id another person holds is refused with a clear message and nothing changes; the People list gains a **Linked** column (`n of total`, with a header hint). Data quality's "Owners without a person" and "Telephony users without a person" link to People for those who may link. There is no owner sync yet: ids are typed as the system shows them (Open decisions).

**The scheduler (since the night build of 9 October 2026, migration 0005a).** A Cloud Run **job** `cma-close-forgotten-workdays` on the web image, command `node jobs/close-forgotten-workdays.mjs`, started by Cloud Scheduler `cma-close-forgotten-workdays-hourly` at every full hour (UTC) through the job's run endpoint with an OAuth token of the service account `cma-web@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`, which runs the job too (its IAM database user is a member of `cma_app`, as for the service; it needs `roles/run.invoker` on the job); created or updated with `docs/scheduler/deploy.sh <dev|prod> <short sha>`, dev first. The job lists the tenants it serves with `cma.find_tenants_for_identity('scheduler', 'scheduler')`, then per tenant, in one transaction with `app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id` set to that tenant's Scheduler user and `app.actor_label = 'scheduler'`, calls `cma.close_forgotten_workdays()`: every day whose workday window ended more than the tenant's grace ago is ended by a system event at the person's published planned shift end, else at the tenant's default time (`workday.auto_close_at`), never before the day's last event and never after the window end (addition 0008b, 12 October 2026; before it, at the business day's end). The person gets a message, the holders of `workday.auto_close_notify_permission` one digest per run, and the day stays flagged and waits for a manager's correction on Team hours (the review flag and Welcome's to-do line keep showing it). The rule is in the database, the schedule is only a sweep: hourly, so a day is closed within the hour after its grace in every zone, and a second run in the same hour finds nothing; a tenant changes when its days close with the grace on the Exports page. The log holds tenant slugs, counts and dates, never a person. `pg_cron` on Cloud SQL was looked at and not chosen: the `cloudsql.enable_pg_cron` flag restarts the instance (prod runs with high availability and real time), the extension lives in one database per instance, and a job scheduled inside the database would run as a database login, so the audit would not name the tenant's scheduler user. Proof: `web/verify/scheduler.mjs` (10 checks, provoked; `PGCONN` for the forgotten day as the owner on a free date 400 days back, the convention of the API verifier, then the job as it runs on Cloud Run): the forgotten day ended by a system event at the tenant's close time (its business day's end for a tenant without settings) and flagged, today's open day untouched, a second run closes nothing, the log names nobody. The default time and the notice to the agent came with addition 0008b; the job's log line is unchanged.

**Shell and navigation (since 7 October 2026, increments c4 and c4.1; Welcome since e1).** The top bar is white, the left rail Background Blue `#EAF4FC` (the current page white, hover white at 60 %), the content area Sand `#FFFDF6`, cards white with a `#D9E2EC` border; all palette tokens, so the theme verifier stays green. The rail holds Welcome as a page of its own at the top (home icon, key 1, no permission: everyone with a role), then navigation groups. The model lives in `src/lib/nav.ts` (since 7 October 2026, d1; entries since e1): `navEntries()` lists the rail in order, a bare page or a group (id, label, icon, items), every page with the permission it needs or null; `visibleNav()` filters by permission, never by role, and numbers the visible pages across the rail; `visiblePages()` flattens them and `firstPage()` gives a person's first visible page, Welcome for everyone. **Live** (since 7 October 2026, increment b; a signal icon) holds the Live board (`/live/board`, `monitoring.live`) and sits before Time, the screen a supervisor keeps open (Martin, 7 October 2026); **Team** (since the night build of 8 October 2026; a people icon) holds People (`/team/people`, `users.manage_agents` or `users.manage_all`, the first page with an any-of permission, `holdsAny()`) and Roster (`/roster/planner`, `roster.manage`); **Time** (clock icon) holds My day (`/day`) and My hours (`workday.own`), My schedule (`/schedule`, `roster.view`) and Team hours (`workday.team`); **Reports** (chart icon) holds the Dashboard (`performance.team`) and, since B11 (12 October 2026), Speed to lead and Intake (`reports.view` or `performance.team`) and Data quality (`reports.view` or `tenant.configure`), numbered after the Dashboard, so the supervisor gains two pages and the manager, admin and analytics three; **Configuration** (gear icon, since 9 October 2026, 0005a) holds the five configuration pages (`tenant.configure`), unnumbered, with the group's own `C`. An agent sees Welcome, My day, My hours and My schedule (1 to 4), unchanged by the Live and Team groups; a supervisor seven pages (Welcome, Live board, My day, My hours, My schedule, Team hours, Dashboard); a manager or admin nine (People and Roster between the Live board and My day), the admin also the Configuration group without numbers; analytics Welcome, the Live board and the Dashboard. A person without `workday.own` who opens My day or My hours is sent to Welcome. A new module adds a group to `navEntries()`. Digit keys number the visible pages across all groups in order and keep working when a group is closed. `NavGroup.tsx` (client) is a button with `aria-expanded`; a group starts closed and its state is remembered per browser in the cookie `cma-nav-<id>` (`open` or `closed`, path `/`, one year, `samesite=lax`, `secure`), read on the server by `Shell` through `cookies()`, so the page never flickers; a closed group that holds the current page gets a white background. The cookie prefix lives in `src/lib/nav.ts`, because a constant exported from a `"use client"` module becomes a client reference on the server. `Shell` is therefore an `async` server component. Copy keys `nav.time` (`Time`, `Tijd`), `nav.reports` (`Reports`, `Rapportages`) and `nav.dashboard`. The Workspace has no separate footer; the bottom of the rail (shortcut hint, version) plays that role. The rail is 256 px (`--spacing-rail`, since 9 October 2026, PR #55: at 216 px Configuration with its keycap overflowed and flexbox squeezed the icons to dots); icons, chevrons and keycaps never shrink and a label too long truncates. **My account** (`/account`, since 9 October 2026, addition 0005b) opens from the person's name in the top bar, for everyone with access, and is never a rail item: Your details (name, email, employer, role, time zone), Teams and skills (teams as chips, skills per dimension with level), Your time (whether the clock runs for them, the forgotten clock-out rule as the scheduler applies it, links to My day and My hours), all read only.

**Dashboard (since 7 October 2026, increment d1).** A Report screen at `/reports/dashboard`, the first page of the Reports group, shown to people holding `performance.team` (supervisor, manager and analytics in the default ladder) and checked again by the database on every read. The period bar is shared with My hours and Team hours (T, W, M, C, up to 92 days); the person filter (P) lists the people with time in the period, so it needs no team list. One read per page; everything else is computed in `src/lib/dashboard.ts`. Tiles: Worked and Paid (whole minutes per person per day, added up exactly as Team hours does, so both screens show the same figure), Productive as a share of worked time, and People with the number of person-days. A legend gives the time per group; Time per status shows one bar per status in the tenant's order with its time and share of clocked time (an inactive status is marked); Hours per day stacks the groups per day up to today (shown for periods longer than a day); Per person shows one stacked bar per person by name with worked and paid, no ranking until the employee-monitoring decision. A note says how many days in the period were not clocked out. Charts are plain SVG and CSS, no chart library: gridlines Border Grey, axis labels 12 px Footnote Grey, every mark with its label or a title. My day shows the status colour as a dot on the badge and the buttons. Mock mode has an `analyst` identity (analytics permissions) and meetings and training in the fixture days, so every group has something to show. **Intake row (since 12 October 2026, B11).** Between the period bar and the person filter, for the selected period and the whole tenant (the person filter does not apply to it): Leads (counted form submissions, `intake_per_day`), Deals created and Tickets created (`crm_records_per_day`, counted pipelines), Speed to lead (the median business minutes to the first outbound call over the period's lead deals, % within target and open not called, computed from `speed_to_lead_rows` exactly as `speed_to_lead_summary` computes a group: `percentile_cont`, rounded to one decimal, shares over the deals whose outcome is known), Calls (in and out) and First orders (with new customers, repeat orders and renewals). Calls show "No telephony connected" and First orders "No shop connected" when the tenant has no such source, never 0; a period that starts before `intake.start_date` says so under the row. Each tile links to its report. The time tiles and charts below are unchanged. **Header hints (since 12 October 2026).** Every column header of Speed to lead, Intake and Data quality and every Dashboard tile (the Intake row and the time tiles) explains itself in a hint: an info mark after the label (`src/components/HeaderHint.tsx`, its own tab stop) opens a short text on hover, keyboard focus or a tap and closes on Esc, with the texts in `src/lib/copy.ts` (`hints`, both languages), the speed-to-lead target filled in from the tenant's settings (`speed_to_lead.target_minutes`, carried by the rows) and the office hours named as the market's own (tenant configuration per market), so neither value is written into the copy.

**Planned routes** (same gate, same rules): the wrap-up rule for `POST /api/v1/me/status` (a non-working status chosen during a call takes effect at wrap-up) once the CMA knows call state (Sync, step 3), `GET /api/v1/call-context?phone=` (resolve a number through the mirror and read the record through the adapter; audited), `POST /api/v1/ingest/messages` and the other ingest routes on the separate ingest service, and the desk routes `next-task`, `book`, `search`, `lookup-by-number` once the queue engine exists.

Locally 218 against a database at `53` with `reports-fixture.sql`, which holds no call outcome, so that check is left out) (12 October 2026: 17 intake-report checks, 16 in dev, where the `invest-analyst` identity of a tenant without telephony or a shop does not exist; `--reports` runs only these, against mock or api data, and `web/verify/reports-fixture.sql` gives a local database their data, refusing to run without a local switch), between 07:00 and 22:00 Amsterdam time since addition 0008b (the own day carries `dayEndsAt`, the window end of its date: 22:00 for Pulse4all; the refused clock-in before the window start is proven by `47`, because it cannot be forced from the verifier) (22 messaging checks since 11 October 2026: the own inbox, since and limit, read and acknowledge on own rows only, one urgent message per run from the test supervisor to Agent Two, the stats) (the six team-now checks of increment b: an agent cannot read the team now, the supervisor reads it uncached with people and status flags, the agent's own entry equals the own-day read field for field, the entry's status carries the day's key with its flags, someone without a day is listed as not clocked in with nothing else, the analyst reads the board and is not on it; since 7 October 2026 the time-per-status flag check accepts a retired status in history, `statusActive` false, after the Subscriptions list retired `available`): identity in two tenants and unknown identity refused, tenant from the identity only, two users with two days, hours show the caller's own day, a `userId` parameter cannot redirect hours, no past day open or capped, the end's request header and cross-site guard, end ends the own day, login after end does not reopen, a second end and a log out change nothing, the other user's day untouched, the 92-day bound; for statuses (6 October 2026): the list has exactly one default, the status change's request header and cross-site guard, the change is stored on the own day, its `statusSince` moves to the change, an unknown key is a 404, an ended day refuses a change with 409; and for corrections (6 October 2026, as the test supervisor): an agent cannot read team hours, the supervisor closes the test agent's open past days, the correction's request header and cross-site guard, a reason is required, nobody corrects their own day, an agent cannot correct, an unknown person is a 404, a future date is refused, Add day creates a missing day, an event before the start is refused, a replaced event stays visible; for team people (c2): an agent cannot list the team, the supervisor lists the people with the agent included; for exports (c3, as the test manager, who is never logged in so no day opens): any person reads the tenant's five export settings, an agent cannot download hours, `workday.team` alone cannot download status changes, the manager's file is a private download (`text/csv`, `no-store`, attachment), the file follows the tenant's own settings (byte order mark and column count, the separator read from `/api/v1/settings`), one hours row per day as Team hours, at least one status stretch per day, the 92-day bound; for time per status (d1, 7 October 2026): an agent cannot read it, the supervisor reads it uncached, worked and paid minutes per past day equal Team hours, the flags follow the tenant's own status list, the person filter returns that person only, the 92-day bound; and the status list shows no pay or billing flags; for Clock in as an action and app links (e1, 7 October 2026, with the dev `analyst`, who holds `performance.team` and no `workday.own`): a visit to Welcome and to My day opens no workday, the database keeps no time for someone without a clock (the start route is a 403 from `CMA06`), start needs the request header and refuses a cross-site request, start opens the own day today and a second start is the same day, start on an ended day changes nothing (where "login after end does not reopen" was), a link that needs a permission is hidden from the agent and shown to the supervisor, and every link is https for anyone with a role. The checks pick statuses and formats from the tenant's own configuration and assume no key or label. For the Team screen (night build of 8 October 2026, 23 checks, as the dev `manager` and `admin`): an agent cannot read the directory, the manager lists the non-managing people and themselves and not the admin, the admin lists everyone and may not edit themselves, teams and skills come from the tenant's catalogs, the manager assigns non-managing roles only, employers are listed for any person, the role change's header and cross-site guards, nobody changes their own role, the manager cannot assign a managing role nor touch the admin, nobody deactivates themselves, the full team list replaces the memberships and restoring it gives the original, an unknown team is a 404, a level outside the scale is refused, the supervisor cannot set skills, adding the same person again answers the same id, a different login id is refused, the manager cannot add a managing role, deactivating keeps the person listed, the memberships for the board's filter. For the roster (20 checks, two weeks ahead on the agent's own team, cleared again at the end): the absence types for any person, an agent cannot read the planner, a week must start on a Monday, the manager reads the week with the agent on the grid, the cell write's header and cross-site guards, an agent cannot write, a shift is written and read back with its times, the past, a date outside the week, a person off the grid and an end before the start are refused, the same person on another roster that day is a conflict, after the publish the agent sees the shift, an edit after the publish is flagged while the agent keeps the published shift, the second publish raises the version and shows the absence, copying the week carries the cell into the next one, the weeks list shows both states, the own schedule has one row per day and is bounded to 92 days, the analyst holds no roster, an agent cannot read today's shifts, the supervisor reads them with one row per person whose time is kept. For the configuration (night build of 9 October 2026, 24 checks, as the dev `admin`; everything undone at the end): an agent and the manager cannot read the status configuration, the admin reads every status with flags, default and usage, a status write's header and cross-site guards, the manager cannot add a status, the admin adds one and reads its flags back, a bad key is a 400, flags and name change while no time is recorded in it, the flags of a status with time behind it are frozen (409), the default cannot be retired, the new status becomes the one default, retire keeps it inactive and the agent's list drops it, an unknown status is a 404; the manager cannot read the links, a plain http address is a 400, an unknown permission a 404, a link with a permission reaches the supervisor and not the agent, a retired link leaves Welcome, the permission catalog is the admin's; an absence type is added and retired and the planner's list drops it, a coverage target is set per team, work type and weekday, weekday 8 is a 400, a setting is written and read back by any person, a value outside the catalog is a 400, the agent cannot write one, the forgotten clock-out grace exists with its default. For `?as=` in mock mode (11 October 2026, 4 checks): a 303 to the same path with a relative Location and the cookie, other parameters kept, an invalid subject a 400, the cookie honoured. All 138 pass; with `--provoke` all 138 fail (the night's run against the local database; the dev run of 9 October goes to `records/configuration-2026-10-09`). Since addition 0005b 143, since 11 October 2026 147 (`records/as-redirect-dev-2026-10-11`). Before 0005a: 111 (`records/team-2026-10-08`, `records/roster-2026-10-08`). Records in `records/api-dev-2026-10-05`, `records/status-dev-2026-10-06` (21 checks), `records/status-followup-dev-2026-10-06` (22) `records/corrections-dev-2026-10-06` (34), `records/c2-2026-10-06` (36), `records/c3-2026-10-07` (44), `records/d1-2026-10-07` (51), `records/e1-2026-10-07` (59), `records/b-2026-10-07` (65) and `records/f1-2026-10-07` (66). `verify:api`, `verify:scheduler` and the clock-ins of `07` run only inside the workday window (07:00-22:00 Europe/Amsterdam for Pulse4all), where outside it the two verifiers stop with one FAIL line (`clock-in refused: outside the workday window …`) and exit 1 instead of crashing, and the writing verifiers refuse a non-dev database: `verify:scheduler`, the one that writes through `PGCONN`, first checks for the dev seed's active `agent.two@example.com` and otherwise exits 2 without writing (12 October 2026). Since B9a.1 (13 October 2026) `verify:layout` needs the mock's workday window (07:00-22:00) as well, as `verify:api` and `verify:scheduler` do: it clocks agent-two in through the start route to open End workday's confirmation, and outside the window it reports `End workday: agent-two could not clock in`. Since 12 October 2026 the check that no past day stays open counts a past day to its workday window end when it ended later, and accepts one minute less than the span because `cma.time_interval_all` sums each stretch in whole seconds; it prints the dates where either applied.

`web/verify/corrections.mjs` checks the day editor's logic without a browser or a database, 21 checks with a `--provoke` mode: the clock changes of 29 March and 25 October 2026, a zone west of UTC, typed times, the order rules and the diff. It runs in `npm run verify` next to the copy, CSV, dashboard, live, theme and layout verifiers. `web/verify/reports.mjs` (B11, 12 October 2026) checks `src/lib/reports.ts` the same way, 28 checks with a `--provoke` mode: the four groups and G, the 92-day bound, `percentile_cont` and the summary figures as the database computes them, Not mapped, the open deals in waiting order, the not-connected states, market order and filter, the inline bars, the data-quality order and the formats; the theme verifier covers the three report pages and the Intake row (27 pages), the layout verifier the report pages at 1280 px, their keycaps, the not-connected state and the rails with the new Reports pages. Since 12 October 2026 (report hints) the theme verifier opens one header hint on each report page and requires its text in Inkt Body on white like the other text, the layout verifier opens one on each report page and the Intake row and checks that it shows on focus, stays inside the viewport and leaves the header it explains uncovered, and that the Intake table scrolls inside its card so the main area never scrolls sideways at 1280 px, and `web/verify/hints.mjs` (`npm run verify:hints`, app in mock mode) checks that every header of the three reports (Speed to lead in each grouping) and every Dashboard tile has exactly one info mark described by a filled-in hint, then focus, hover, Esc, Tab, a tap and the tile links; with `--provoke` it removes one mark and fails. `web/verify/configuration.mjs` (night build of 9 October 2026) checks `src/lib/configuration.ts` the same way, 30 checks with a `--provoke` mode: the frozen-flags and retire rules as the database applies them, the list order, the form checks, market and level parsing, coverage targets, the minute settings and the export preview in every format; `web/verify/scheduler.mjs` is described under The scheduler. Since the night of 13 October 2026 `configuration.mjs` also checks `src/lib/sources.ts` and `src/lib/alertRules.ts`, 92 checks in all: the sections per kind and the kinds offered against held rows, the settings key and value rules as the database applies them (a secret-looking key, the word key, upper case, nested values, the size as the database measures it), typed values kept, the record link as `cma.record_url` builds it and no link without a host, with a placeholder left or without https, the name pattern words, include and exclude, the first word, the market prefix through the aliases, the form rule on a mixed catalog (a second run changes nothing, a form without a prefix keeps its market), the outcome rule, office hours as typed, overlaps and the default for a market without its own, UK refused, the alert rules' record types, pipelines, age, duplicates and the effect in words; `team.mjs` 26 checks with the linked-account editor (state, changes, an id with a space, the count and the labels). The theme verifier covers 33 pages with the Sources list, a connection page of each of three kinds, Markets and Alert rules, the layout verifier the Configuration group with eight pages and the three new pages at 1280 px; both, and `hints.mjs`, acknowledge the mock fixture's urgent message first (`acknowledgeUrgent()`), because its pop-up covered the manager's pages and failed them on `main` too (found and proven on a checkout of `main` that night). `web/verify/team.mjs` and `web/verify/roster.mjs` (night build of 8 October 2026) check `src/lib/team.ts` (18 checks: skill grouping and labels, the editor's state and payload, the smallest set of writes, role options, filters, the Add a person checks) and `src/lib/roster.ts` (31 checks: the parser in every typed form, labels, the week across a month end, ISO week numbers, planned minutes and headcount, coverage states and grid, adherence in a zone and across the autumn clock change) the same way. `web/verify/live.mjs` (b) checks `src/lib/live.ts` without a browser or a database, 26 checks with a `--provoke` mode (team keys per person, the team filter alone and combined, the teams on the board since 0004): the group from the flags of the current status and from the day's state, the row order, the tiles with and without a paid pause status and with no status at all, the filters and the employers, since and worked from fixed instants, the compact format. `web/verify/dashboard.mjs` (d1) checks `src/lib/dashboard.ts` without a browser or a database, 17 checks with a `--provoke` mode: groups from flags with misleading keys and names, the agent's three groups, minutes per person per day as Team hours, totals, status order with an inactive status, every day on the axis across a month end, people by name, the hours axis, and every group colour a palette token. The theme verifier checks eighteen pages (the five configuration pages as the admin since 9 October 2026; Welcome, My day without a day and, as Agent Two after the start route, with the clock and the status buttons, the Dashboard and the Live board as the test supervisor, People as the admin, the roster planner and its print view as the manager, My schedule as the agent), SVG fills and strokes as well; the layout verifier also checks the navigation per identity for five identities (analyst, agent, supervisor, manager, admin: the rails with the Live, Team and Time groups, and since 9 October 2026 the Configuration group's five unnumbered pages for the admin and for nobody else) (everyone lands on Welcome, pages numbered without gaps, the Clock in button and the shift sentence only for a person whose time is kept and who has a roster, My day sends the analyst to Welcome) and writes one Welcome screenshot per identity. `web/verify/csv.mjs` (c3) checks the export files without a browser or a database, 15 checks with a `--provoke` mode: settings fallback, formula guard, quoting, the four date formats, the three duration formats, times in each day's zone across the autumn change, byte order mark and CRLF, both files end to end. `npm run typecheck` runs `next typegen` first, so route types for a new page exist before `tsc`.

The verifier clocks the test agent in through the start route and leaves that day open; on its next run it closes every open past day of the test agent itself through the corrections route, one minute after the day's last effective event (since 7 October 2026, c3.1: an end one minute after the start was refused once the status checks had changed the status later that day), and adds one day per run on the first free date more than 400 days back. Fixture 08, which did this by hand in Cloud SQL Studio, was retired on 6 October 2026.

## Source of truth

Every data type has exactly one system that owns it. Postgres mirrors and combines; it does not overrule the owner. The rule of thumb since 6 October 2026: **customer facts are owned by the CRM, Postgres holds a mirror of the fields the Workspace needs; work facts live only in Postgres.** A result code and a call engagement go to the CRM because they are about the customer; a lease, a status interval, a roster shift, a message delivery or a productivity figure never does. The owners below are Pulse4all's; another customer fills in its own systems, the rule stays the same.

| Data | Owner (source of truth) |
|------|-------------------------|
| Contacts, companies, leads, deals, owner assignment | CRM (HubSpot); mirrored in Postgres for the fields the Workspace serves |
| Call facts: attempts placed, durations, talk time, recordings | Telephony (Aircall), through webhooks into Postgres, for a connection's counted lines only (addition 0008f); client-side events are a live hint, never the record |
| Work queue: campaigns, tasks, leases, attempts, dispositions (result codes), callbacks, counters, ceilings | CMA (Postgres); the CRM receives each outcome as an engagement with the result code in a custom property, and optionally a callback as a CRM task (tenant setting) |
| Result-code catalog, scoped by business line and call type | CMA (tenant configuration) |
| Support tickets | CRM (HubSpot) |
| Form submissions | CRM (HubSpot), polled into Postgres, contact id only |
| Call engagement on the contact | CRM (HubSpot, logged by the telephony integration); the CMA reads it for the contact link |
| Customer contact details (name, address) | To decide: Shopify or customer portal |
| Invoices, payment status, billing address | NetSuite |
| Orders (id, time, amounts, first, repeat or renewal) | Shopify; read directly per store since brief B10 (ids, times, amounts, kind, the keyed hash of the phone, never the email); HubSpot holds the copy the HubSpot–Shopify app writes (the Shopify contact fields, a Self Serve deal per new order), not the CMA (Decision log, 13 October 2026) |
| A Shopify customer's CRM contact | The CMA (addition 0008h): the contact id the ingest service matched the customer to, kept in `cma.commerce_customer`, never written to HubSpot; HubSpot stays the source of the contact itself |
| Complaints, vulnerability, SLAs | CRM (HubSpot ticket category, contact field) |
| Call transcripts, sentiment, playbook results | Telephony AI (Aircall AI) |
| Customer App interactions: self-service orders and changes, service requests, in-app messages | Customer App, synced as activity events and tickets into the CRM where they need follow-up |
| Devices, installations, service status, battery and pad expiry, replacements | Asset Management Database (system and access to confirm) |
| Payments, days to pay, dunning stages | NetSuite |
| Subscriptions, terms, cancellations | Shopify and Juo, synced into HubSpot |
| Reviews | To decide: review platform |
| Agents, roles, teams, skills, rosters, statuses, time entries, lead assignments, quality scorecards, messages, change history | CMA (Postgres) |

**What is written back to the CRM:** field diffs the agent made on a served record, the engagement with the result code, do-not-call, owner assignment, through the outbox (0007c, its worker `ingest-outbox` since brief B10), and optionally callbacks as CRM tasks. Nothing for Shopify: the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify contact fields and creates a Self Serve deal per new order, and the CMA keeps each customer's matched contact in its own database (addition 0008h; Decision log, 13 October 2026; one writer per property). Everything else stays in Postgres by default. Write-back goes through the outbox; the mirror is updated from the CRM's answer.
