Later on 7 October: Pulse4all Subscriptions' status list as seed data in `02` (Available - Sales as the default and Available - Operations, Coaching, Break and Lunch unpaid and not billable, `available` retired), with the dev fixture `06` picking statuses by flag, run in dev (`07` and `16` rerun, unchanged) and prod (`ed04596`, PR #33), no web change. Afternoon of 7 October: increment b, the Live board as the first page of a new Live group before Time (`a2b805e`, PR #35): addition 0003e (`team_now`) verified in dev and prod, `GET /api/v1/team/now`, `lib/live.ts` with its verifier (21), the API verifier at 65 checks, theme nine pages, layout with the new rails, all provoked; the team-now read checked in prod. Remaining: Joshua in prod when V1 goes live, Finn to `agent` then (Decision log, 7 October), the first Newco agent once the conditions below are met, the exact portal addresses for the app links, the Steam sample (hours columns and Pulse4all's duration format), Martin's decision on the hours shown, heads-up to Yordi, prod high availability and `min-instances=1`, a review of the web dependencies (`npm audit` reports 5 high-severity advisories; two install scripts are blocked), the baseline for business-case KPIs 1, 3, 5 and 7, then the gate opens.*
   Control-layer increments, from the demo analysis of 5 October (about 8 to 11 days together; one migration, 0003, plus a view): (a) agent day at parity: status grid (done 6 October, without the derived colours), timer, header chip, greeting and day line, pauses column and note chips on My hours; (b) a current-status view and the Live board; (c) Hours and export in three slices: c1 corrections in the database and API (done 6 October), c2 the Hours screen with Correct a day and Add day (done 6 October, with addition 0003a), c3 the two CSVs (done 7 October, with addition 0003b); (d) Dashboard (d1 done 7 October, with addition 0003c). Outside the demo's list: c4 and c4.1, the shell and navigation groups (done 7 October); (e) the Welcome page with an explicit Clock in (e1 done 7 October, with addition 0003d; chosen before (b) because it changes what starts a workday); (b) the Live board (done 7 October, with addition 0003e; team and skill filters, the shift line and the on-call state follow their migrations). With (b) the control layer's demo list is done. (f) Go-live hardening, 7 October: the retired-status fix (a row in a retired status is named in the day editor and stays as it is, a changed row needs a current status; My day and Welcome name a retired current status; Team hours on `sameOffset`; `0ccc8e7`, PR #37, API verifier 66 checks), prod on a dedicated core with high availability, `web/verify/**` out of the deploy trigger, and Martin's decisions on shifts past midnight, the `admin` role and the hours an agent sees (Decision log); still in (f): the heads-up to Yordi, `npm audit` (5 high, two install scripts blocked) and the KPI baselines. Next (Martin, 7 October): step 5's first two slices, teams and skills (migration 0004) with the Team screen, then the configuration screens, before step 3.
3. **Sync and mirror.** Ingest API on Cloud Run with its service account as `cma_app` member and one key per tenant. HubSpot and Aircall adapters (Make scenarios, n8n on Cloud Run EU) mapping into the canonical entities through the ingest API; the customer-field mirror with the E.164 index (migration 0010); Aircall webhooks as the record of call facts; the outbox worker. BigQuery connection on `cma_read` with `bq_reader` (brought forward to the intake track for dev on 10 October 2026, Decision log; the dev connection `cma_dev_read` exists since 11 October 2026; prod after Yordi's answer), BigQuery in the same region as Cloud SQL. Second heads-up to Yordi before the mirror lands in prod.
4. **Data first (in parallel).** The data prerequisites from the KPIs section in HubSpot and Aircall, plus Shopify orders into HubSpot, plus the Subs result-code catalog confirmed by Kira. First KPI reports for Kira can run from BigQuery as soon as sync and clean data are in place, before the CMA UI exists.
5. **CMA V1 for Pulse4all subscriptions, rest of the control layer.** Teams and skills (migration 0004) with the Team screen and the roster (migration 0005) with the planner, My schedule, the print view and the shift line were written in the night build of 7 to 8 October 2026 (slices 1 to 4, verified locally, dev and prod on 8 October through `MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`); The configuration screens (statuses with flags, teams, skills and level scales, absence types, coverage targets, export formats and the minute settings, app links) and the scheduler for forgotten days (migration 0005a, the Cloud Run job, the hourly schedule) were written in the night build of 8 to 9 October 2026 (slices 1 to 3, verified locally, dev and prod on 9 October through `MORNING_RUNBOOK.md`); My account (addition 0005b, `/account` from the name in the top bar) was built and verified on 9 October 2026 (PR #57, dev and prod); still in this step: shift templates per team (0005c) and a supervisor's read-only planner (`roster.view_team`), the rest of the night's slice 4; access requests are out.
6. **Messaging and speed to lead.** Messaging (migration 0008): composer with targets, sent list with read and acknowledged counts, the Home list, the urgent pop-up, polling first and the realtime service when chosen; the ingest route for automations. Speed to lead: assignment, push via the outbox, accept click, accept window and expiry, the event log, the CRM adapter action for owner assignment.
   **Intake track** (since 10 October 2026, Martin; design `docs/night-2026-10-10/DESIGN.md`): I1 intake core (0007: markets and office hours, HubSpot call engagements, associations, forms, sync audit), I2 HubSpot through project apps into the ingest service with jobs, I3 telephony (0007a, Aircall), I4 commerce (0007b, Shopify per store) with the outbox and the CRM contact write-back (0007c), I5 lead metrics and data quality (0007d), I6 messaging core with new-deal alerts (0008), I7 reports in the Workspace; then S2 routing and S3 offers (0009). Progress is recorded in this README.
7. **Queue engine.** Migration 0011 ported from the AgentUI trial's verified design under CMA conventions: campaigns, scoped result codes, tasks, leases, attempts, callbacks, counters and ceilings, `fn_serve_next`, `fn_book_disposition`, lease release and queue peek; the seven concurrency gaps the trial closed become verify blocks. Owner of tasks and dispositions written into Source of truth (done above).
8. **Agent desk for Subs.** The directive screen at parity with the Subs agent UI mockup: next task, record from the mirror with live refresh, field blocks, button grid with shortcuts, requirement-only dialogs, callback picker, memo, search outside the queue, incoming-call pop, Aircall embedded with one-click dial where possible, write-back through the outbox, who-saw-what audit. Heads-up to Yordi and the data processing view on customer data on the desk before real agents use it. Daily practice then shows whether Newco needs the desk or settles on HubSpot and Aircall; the assumption is that it does.
9. **Manager and analytics views.** Productivity (Total Calls Today first, then talk time per worked hour, Prepare and Finish time), benchmarking, effectiveness, coverage, gamification, live and quality monitoring, Kira's KPI dashboards and the first cohort reports (retention and payment behaviour per entry cohort from HubSpot, Shopify and NetSuite).
10. **Copy to Pulse4all Invest.** Configuration in the same database: `create_tenant`, organisations, channels, teams, skills, roles, the Invest campaign with its field blocks, result codes and the prospectus compliance hook for investor emails.
11. **Pulse4all US, when it starts.** A third tenant, set up the way Invest is (`create_tenant`, its own HubSpot portal and Aircall account as adapter instances, currency, time zones and languages in configuration), after the region and residency question is settled (see Open decisions).

Later modules follow the Target scope table.

**Convergence: the version where everything comes together.** After step 10 the remaining sources are connected one adapter at a time, each as an increment that adds its activity events and its cohort attributes to the same model: the shared Gmail mailbox, SMS and chat, the Customer App (as a channel and as a self-service source), NetSuite (payments and dunning), Shopify and Juo directly where HubSpot's copy is not enough, and the Asset Management Database (devices and service). The version in which all of them feed the Workspace is the convergence version; its number is set when that part of the roadmap is cut. From then on the Workspace is the one point where the agent's next task, the team's day and every management layer's reporting come from one database.

---

## Out of scope

- **Replacing the CRM.** Contacts, deals and tickets live in the CRM; the CMA mirrors what it needs and writes outcomes back. The CMA never becomes the customer record.
- **Telephony itself.** IVR, inbound routing, recording and the phone app stay with Aircall; the CMA embeds the softphone and consumes events.
- **Callpit and the Subs agent UI as code.** The AgentUI trial's cockpits (`callpit.netlify.app`, `p4a-agent-ui-subs-dev.netlify.app`) are input: their queue-engine design, result-code registry, Aircall findings, screens, copy and decisions are ported under the CMA conventions in Roadmap steps 7 and 8; their code is not carried over as-is (decided 6 October 2026).
- **The Neon/Netlify trial** (Workspace, agent UI, queue engine; see the AgentUI handover of 4 October 2026). It was concepting and testing, not production. Its UI source and decisions are input for the CMA; its code is not carried over as-is. The trial's Workspace schema and validated hours export are not available, so the hours export is rebuilt and re-validated against an anonymised copy of the Steam hours CSV. The demo at `contactcenter.pulse4all.app` (Firebase) is the UI and interaction blueprint for the management screens and the agent's day; its data layer is not adopted.
- **Customer data on the management screens.** Live, Report and Data show no customer data; alerts carry case status and a link. The agent desk shows the record it serves (see Architecture, Customer data).
- **Invoicing.** The CMA produces approved hours and outcomes per employer; invoices are made in NetSuite.
- **A second CRM or telephony adapter before a business line needs one.** Design for it, do not build it.
- **Offering the CMA to other companies.** The CMA is built for Pulse4all's own business lines. It is developed as standalone technology, but selling or licensing it is not part of this project.

---

## Decision log

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 3 Oct 2026 | The CMA is a CRM- and telephony-agnostic management layer: configured per tenant, system names only as data values; canonical entities with an adapter per tenant (wording updated 9 Oct 2026) | Pulse4all's business lines already run separate HubSpot portals and Aircall accounts; these abstractions are cheap in the foundation and expensive later, while another adapter type is only built when a business line needs one |
| 3 Oct 2026 | One database for Pulse4all; business lines are tenants sharing that database with RLS; Pulse4all Subscriptions and Invest are one database (wording updated 9 Oct 2026) | Simple analytics across business lines, no schema per business line; a business line that needs its own region (US) can get its own database later |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Every verified increment goes to production; dev is the sandbox for the next iteration; the prod project is created right after migration 0001, not before Sync | Learn from running software early; no big-bang launch |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Authentication V2 is the tenant's identity provider, not HubSpot OAuth by definition | A business line without HubSpot must be able to log in; identity already matches on external id |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Make writes through an ingest API on Cloud Run, never directly to Postgres | Make's outbound IPs are shared; the database stays closed |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Tenant = business line; no separate business-line table | The README already defines the separation boundary as the business line; the database holds all of Pulse4all, so no grouping table is needed |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Shared schema with `tenant_id` and row-level security, context set per transaction (`app.tenant_id`, `app.user_id`, `app.actor_label`), composite foreign keys | Separation enforced in the database, fail closed; one schema to migrate and report on |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Three group roles (`cma_owner`, `cma_app`, `cma_readonly`); team logins default to reader and escalate with `SET ROLE` | Nobody works with owner rights by accident; everything stays under personal logins |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Readers (BigQuery, NocoDB) only see the `cma_read` views; analytics sees all business lines by default, a reader can be limited per login; hard separation for dashboards in BigQuery | `EXTERNAL_QUERY` cannot set a tenant per query; all tenants in the database are Pulse4all business lines; base tables can change freely; exposed columns are deliberate |
| 3 Oct 2026 | `bq_reader` is a password login, the one exception to IAM-only | BigQuery federated connections authenticate with username and password |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Audit log on every tenant-scoped table from the first migration, insert-only, with acting user and process | History that was not captured cannot be recovered; needed for pay corrections, disputes and compliance |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Customer contact channels are per-tenant configuration (`channel`); skills, routing, SLAs and metrics reference a channel key | Email, sms, whatsapp and chat join phone without schema changes |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Speed-to-lead steps are append-only timestamped events; the accept click stays mandatory | Confirmation is provable and reportable per agent |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Every CMA user has an employer (`organisation`) | Hours and results per employer for reconciliation with the call center partner |
| 3 Oct 2026 | Working title renamed to Contactcenter-Management-App; the abbreviation CMA and everything carrying it (project IDs, group, roles, secrets) stay | Channels are configuration and nothing assumes phone; "call center" in the title contradicted that. Still a working title: the final name is chosen with Mark |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Customer configuration (tenants, organisations, channels) and dev test data live in separate seed scripts, not in the migration | The same migration runs unchanged in dev, prod and any later database; test data can never reach prod by accident |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Prod starts on the same shared-core machine as dev; dedicated core with high availability before real data lands | Nothing to keep available while the database is empty; saves about €80 a month until real data flows; the switch is an instance edit with a short restart |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Every migration, seed and verify script runs under a personal IAM login; a guard refuses `postgres` unless `cma.emergency` is set | The 0001 seed in prod was applied via `postgres` under `cma_owner`; those audit rows stay as recorded (`actor_login = postgres`), the audit trail must name a person from now on, and the emergency path remains available on purpose |
| 4 Oct 2026 | The Neon/Netlify Workspace and agent UI were a trial; the permanent setup is the CMA on Cloud SQL and Cloud Run at workspace.pulse4all.app. Trial schema, designs and tests are ported under the CMA conventions, not copied | One foundation with tenants, RLS, audit and EU hosting; the trial proved the concepts |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Workday and hours (Roadmap step 2) come before Sync; Authentication V2, prod high availability and the heads-up to Yordi move forward with it | Hours need no integrations, and a working screen early beats weeks of backend without anything to use |
| 4 Oct 2026 | workspace.pulse4all.app runs through a global external Application Load Balancer with IAP in front of Cloud Run; DNS stays at IONOS with one A record | Cloud Run domain mapping is preview and not recommended for production; IAP keeps the site closed to anyone outside the team during the build |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Authentication V2 for Pulse4all is Google Workspace through IAP; the CMA's `app_user` is the single list of who may work; identity matched on the Google account id | Every user, Newco agents included, has a full pulse4all.com account: no extra login system or licence cost, offboarding follows the Google account. The V1 HubSpot-id screen is not built |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Code in the private GitHub repository `Pulse4all-com/cma`, owned by a Pulse4all organisation; builds in Cloud Build | Ownership independent of personal accounts; a later move to an EU-hosted Git service stays cheap. EU sovereignty of the code is revisited later together with the rest of the stack |
| 4 Oct 2026 | The CMA is desktop only (minimum 1280 px, keyboard-first, dense UI scale) and has no offline mode | Agents work at office workstations; without internet calling stops too. Mobile views for managers would be a separate decision |
| 4 Oct 2026 | Migration 0002 is written fresh, not ported; the trial's UI source and decisions are input | The trial's Workspace schema was never kept as files; the port was a rewrite under CMA conventions anyway |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Web app stack: Next.js 16 (App Router, Node runtime, standalone server in a Docker image), TypeScript strict, Tailwind v4 with the Pulse4all tokens as CSS `@theme` variables and Tailwind's default palette removed | Current stable; the token file is the single place for brand values and lets the theme verifier prove that nothing off-palette renders |
| 5 Oct 2026 | The dense UI type scale and the proposed semantic colours from Pulse4all-Style.md (sections 3.3 and 2.3) are adopted as the CMA's standard; Montserrat ships with the app from the brand's variable font file | Operational screens need the dense scale; no font request leaves the browser. Any deviation is flagged to Mark per the style guide's change control |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Runtime modes fail closed: `CMA_AUTH_MODE` and `CMA_DATA_MODE` default to iap and api; mock must be switched on explicitly on the Cloud Run service | Nobody can forget to switch the mocks off before real data lands |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Identity and data are seams: `proxy.ts` sets the verified identity in request headers, `src/lib/data` is the only data entry point, and the `app_user` check (`findPrincipal`) belongs to the data layer | Screens never change when the API replaces the mock; the user list is data, so it follows the same swap |
| 5 Oct 2026 | An ended workday stays ended; the next workday starts at the next day's login. Resuming after an accidental end is a correction, not a button | Time data is append-only; IAP's silent re-login after log out must not open a new day by itself |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Log out is a plain form POST (`/logout/end`) that ends the workday and answers a relative 303 to `/logout/done?gcp-iap-mode=CLEAR_LOGIN_COOKIE` | A client-side redirect cannot follow IAP's cookie-clearing round trip; the absolute host behind the load balancer is the container's, not the public domain |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Every user has a time zone (mock: Europe/Madrid); calendar days and week boundaries follow it, instants are UTC on the wire | Newco works in Spain, Pulse4all in the Netherlands and Denmark; in Postgres this is a per-user setting with an organisation default |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Cloud Build runs as its own service account `cma-build` with the minimum roles; manual builds stage source in an EU bucket; first deploy of each pipeline change by hand before the trigger carries it | The default Cloud Build account is broader than needed; the EU-only policy refuses the default US staging bucket; a pipeline is proven once before it runs unattended |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Time writes go through database functions (`open_workday`, `set_status`, `end_workday`, `correct_time_event`); the workday header is derived from the events by `refresh_workday` | One write path with the rules in one place for the web app, the scheduler and later tools (Open principle); the API stays thin; impossible states are refused at the source |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Facts are append-only by privilege: `cma_app` cannot update or delete `time_event`; corrections are new rows with reason and approver, chained one per event | The database enforces the convention, not the application; a voided or replaced event stays visible for pay disputes |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Views are built as one unfiltered owner-only core per computation with a tenant-filtered face in `cma` and a `reader_sees` face in `cma_read`; `security_invoker` views are not used | An owner view bypasses RLS and a `security_invoker` view cannot sit under a `cma_read` owner view (the check falls through to the reader); one definition, two faces, both fail closed |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Status catalog per tenant with semantic flags (`is_working`, `is_productive`, `is_paid`, `is_billable`, `is_default`), seeded with a default ladder for every tenant | Billing, adherence and hours depend on flags, never on a status name; the flags are tenant configuration, changing them is an update, not a migration |
| 5 Oct 2026 | A forgotten clock-out stays open, is flagged `needs_correction` and its hours are capped at the end of its business day; a manager closes it through a correction, later the scheduler | Nothing is invented: the end time is unknown, so the row says so until a person or the scheduler decides |
| 5 Oct 2026 | The Google account id is stored as the bare numeric id; dev test identities use system `mock`, not `google` | The numeric id is what Google's APIs return and what the prod people seed will carry; a mock subject can never match or impersonate a real Google row |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Verify scripts prove behaviour in prod too: blocks create throwaway users inside a rolled-back transaction instead of depending on seeded people | Prod has no people before the first agent hours; structure alone is not a verification |
| 5 Oct 2026 | `db/` numbering continues `05`, `06`, `07` per increment; `records/<migration>-<env>-<date>` is reserved for migration verify output, the web app's screenshots go to `records/web-<env>-<date>` | The folder name tells which increment a record proves; the earlier `0002-prod` web screenshots were renamed on 5 October |
| 5 Oct 2026 | The web app reaches Postgres in-process through `src/lib/data`, not through its own HTTP API; the ingest API for Make is a separate Cloud Run service (Roadmap step 3) | Route handlers for the screens would add a hop, a second authorisation check and a public URL for a caller in the same process; the ingest API has another caller, identity and ingress |
| 5 Oct 2026 | `cma-web` connects with the Cloud SQL Node.js connector and automatic IAM database authentication; its service account is the database user, member of `cma_app` with inheritance and without `SET ROLE` | No password exists to leak or rotate; the audit log's session user names the service; the database stays closed to IP addresses |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Own data is enforced through `cma.current_user_id()` in every query, never through an id passed to the query; the database clock decides; today is computed in SQL in the user's zone | A bug that hands over a colleague's id still cannot read their hours; nobody can backdate a start through the app; the app server's zone never matters |
| 5 Oct 2026 | The identity carries its provider; IAP's subject is stored and matched as the bare numeric Google id, a non-Google `sub` is refused | Another identity provider is another value, not a code path; the prod people seed carries the id `userinfo` returns |
| 5 Oct 2026 | An identity matching several tenants gets "no access yet" until a tenant picker exists; so does a user without a role | Never guess a tenant; a person without a role may not work yet |
| 5 Oct 2026 | A versioned JSON API under `/api/v1/me` (me, day, end, hours) behind the same gate; POST guarded by a custom header and `Sec-Fetch-Site` | Verifiers compare data instead of HTML; first piece of the Open principle; IAP sends its session with any request, so a write needs a cross-site guard |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Dev runs the image Cloud Build made for prod, by tag; dev's Cloud Run service agent may read the `cma` repository in prod | Dev tests exactly the bytes prod gets; no second pipeline that could drift |
| 5 Oct 2026 | `cloudbuild.yaml` and the trigger's substitutions are the one place `cma-web` is configured, including the database settings and at most 4 instances; the build stops before deploying when api mode lacks database settings | `--set-env-vars` replaces everything on each deploy, so hand edits vanish; 4 instances × pool 5 stays well under the instance's 50 connections; a misconfigured prod never gets deployed |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Prod switched to `CMA_DATA_MODE=api`; Martin, Joshua and Finn get role `manager` in prod for now | The team builds and tests everything; nobody else is in prod yet, so the extra rights reach nobody. Separating builders from call center management is revisited before agents join |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Google account ids never go into the repository; `db/09_seed_people_prod.sql` holds placeholders filled in only in the Studio editor | A Google account id is a personal identifier |
| 5 Oct 2026 | Code changes are made in a Cloud Shell clone: branch, `npm run build` with the repository's settings on Node 22, verifiers, pull request with `gh`, squash merge | The first red build of the day surfaced only in Cloud Build after the merge; the real build now runs before anything reaches `main`, which deploys to prod |
| 6 Oct 2026 | The demo at `contactcenter.pulse4all.app` is the UI and interaction blueprint for the management screens and the agent's day: its screens, flows, copy and behaviour rules are adopted (about 99 percent), its Firebase data layer is not | The screens and copy are right and in the house style; the data layer trusts the device clock, has no tenant, audit or server-side rules, and migration 0002 already covers that half better. Analysis of 5 October 2026 in the project artifacts |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Callpit and the Subs agent UI contribute patterns, the My account panel, the Aircall findings and the queue-engine design; their code is not carried over | Both run on mock data with a public Make webhook; the engine design is verified and the screens are the shortlist for the agent desk. Analyses of 5 October 2026 in the project artifacts |
| 6 Oct 2026 | The CMA becomes a directive contact-center suite: an agent desk that serves the next task (Steam Connect-like) plus the management layer, on one foundation. HubSpot stays the CRM; Postgres owns the work queue; the agent never waits on an integration | Agent productivity dropped moving from Steam to HubSpot with the Aircall plugin: picking tasks from lists and clicking through records is not built for working fast at scale. Daily practice will show whether Newco needs the desk; the assumption is that it will |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Configurable by default is a development rule: every feature is built so a Pulse4all business line with its own CRM portal and telephony account can start from configuration alone; business-line specifics are tenant configuration and seed data; vendor specifics sit behind adapter interfaces (wording updated 9 Oct 2026) | Subs, Invest and later US each run their own portals and accounts; what the CMA adds on top of CRM plus phone is the workforce, productivity and management layer |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Teams and skills: 0 teams (membership, markets: Team EN, NL, DE, FR, Nordics), 1 languages with the four-step scale Basic, Good, Fluent, Native, 2 work types (Sales, Operations, Debt, …); channel kept as an empty dimension; eligibility derived at serve time | One model serves the queue's hard filters, the roster's coverage, message targeting and the My account panel; team and language must be separate for Nordics |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Postgres on Cloud SQL stays the operational core; the CRM is never squeezed. Customer facts are owned by the CRM, Postgres holds a mirror of the fields the Workspace needs (filled by the sync, updated from the CRM's answer, following CRM deletions), work facts live only in Postgres. Setup: front = Workspace; backend = PostgreSQL, CRM, Aircall, Make, n8n, Cloud Run EU | The desk must serve the next task and show the record instantly whether or not the CRM is fast or up; the CRM keeps the customer record; data minimisation bounds the mirror to declared fields |
| 6 Oct 2026 | n8n joins Make as an automation runtime, on Cloud Run EU; both reach the CMA only through the ingest API and the outbox webhooks | Same reasoning as the 3 October ingest decision; n8n next to the CMA keeps that automation in the same region and project |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Write-back to the CRM is limited to field diffs, engagements with the result code, do-not-call, owner assignment and optionally callbacks as CRM tasks (tenant setting); everything else stays in Postgres | Customer facts to the CRM, work facts in Postgres; CRM-only colleagues stay informed where it matters |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Statuses within the workday cover pauses, training, meetings and work on other projects or business lines from V1, with an optional activity code per status; a status change also sets the agent's telephony availability through the adapter, mapping per status as tenant configuration | One clock, one action: the phone follows the status, time on other projects is reportable without a second system; the set-availability call is verified before it is marked done |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Reporting for every management layer from the combined sources plus staff is the CMA's main reporting purpose: every outcome attributed to an agent and a time interval through the canonical activity event; productivity per agent per worked hour and benchmarking are first-class | The join of channel events with clock, statuses, teams and employer is what no CRM, telephony tool or shop platform can make; what Kira, and Peter for Invest, need to steer |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Cohorts are a first-class reporting concept: customers by entry month, country, source, proposition and converting agent or team, followed over time for retention, payment behaviour, reviews, service and value; agents as cohorts too | Period reports hide whether a cohort stays and pays; cohort definitions are tenant configuration in the metric catalog, confirmed by Kira and Peter |
| 6 Oct 2026 | The Customer App is a channel (a `channel` row and a source of activity events); the Asset Management Database is a data source for cohort attributes and agent work; both are connected after step 10 as part of the convergence version | Channels are configuration; the model carries every source from the start and fills them one adapter at a time |
| 6 Oct 2026 | The business case is written into the README as ten KPIs with baseline, target and ROI line, reviewed quarterly; baselines are taken before each increment reaches agents | A business case without a baseline cannot be proven or disproven; the targets are changed in the table when they turn out wrong, not argued over later |
| 6 Oct 2026 | One CMA for all Pulse4all entities (Subs, Invest, later US), each with its own HubSpot portal and Aircall account: one adapter instance per tenant with its own credentials and mappings; aggregation across business lines in `cma_read` and BigQuery; CRM-agnostic is therefore a V1 fact, not a future option | No portal can see another portal, so the operation comes together only outside them; three portals already behave like three CRMs and need exactly the per-tenant adapter a second CRM type needs |
| 6 Oct 2026 | Status changes go through `POST /api/v1/me/status` with the status `key` from `GET /api/v1/me/statuses`; the route never opens a day (no day 404, ended day 409); the buttons on My day call that route; the list exposes key, name, `isWorking` and `isDefault` only | `cma.set_status()` takes the key, which stays stable when a tenant renames a status; changing an ended day is a correction; the screen uses the path the verifier proves; pay and billing flags are not agent information |
| 6 Oct 2026 | The clock on My day counts statuses with `is_working` only, from server-provided closed seconds plus the running stretch, and pauses otherwise; "since" is the start of the current status, the workday start is shown separately | A clock running through lunch while My hours does not count it contradicts the agent's own hours; the inputs are stable between status changes, so a tab left open all day stays right without polling. | 11 Oct 2026 | Push trigger restored in p4a-cma-prod: PR #87 (`bbea843`, the `?as=` redirect fix) was merged at 10:03 UTC, but trigger `cma-web-main` built nothing, and a manual `gcloud builds triggers run cma-web-main` was refused with "Permission 'cloudbuild.builds.create' denied on resource 'projects/0000006ce9bc2b5a'" (hex for project number 467777891162, p4a-cma-prod). Martin's own rights were intact (`testIamPermissions` returned all permissions, `roles/owner` on the project, no deny policies on the project or the folder). Cause: audit finding 02 (row above) removed `roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder` from the legacy Cloud Build service account `467777891162@cloudbuild.gserviceaccount.com`; the trigger's invocation checks `cloudbuild.builds.create` on that account, not on the trigger's own account `cma-build` (which never held a Cloud Build role while triggers worked for weeks), so every push-triggered build since about 08:22 UTC failed silently. Google's troubleshooting page ("Build trigger fails due to missing cloudbuild.builds.create permission") says the permission can sit on either the user-specified or the default account. Fix: `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor` (not Builder) granted to the legacy account in p4a-cma-prod; a temporary `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor` on `cma-build` did not help and was removed again, so `cma-build`'s roles are unchanged. The legacy account is Google-managed; `gcloud iam service-accounts describe` on it is refused even for an Owner, which is expected. A manual run of `cma-web-main` then built `bbea843` (build `5142fad6-c336-4fb1-b34d-cb1afd1e3e38`, SUCCESS) and deployed it to prod (image `europe-west4-docker.pkg.dev/p4a-cma-prod/cma/web:bbea843`). Narrowed to the custom role `cmaBuildCreate` on 12 October 2026 (row below). | Editor holds cloudbuild.builds.create without Builder's storage and registry rights; which account the trigger checks is Google's choice, so the narrowest role goes where the check happens. A merge that builds nothing fails silently, so the build is checked after every web merge. |

---

## Open decisions

- `npm audit` reports 5 high-severity advisories, one chain under `eslint-config-next` (a devDependency; `npm ls braces --omit=dev` is empty, so nothing of it ships in the image); no fix exists and `npm audit fix --force` is never run; reviewed again with every Next.js update. The heads-up to Yordi was drafted on 7 October 2026 (four questions: Spain's daily registration and retention, Wet DBA, employee monitoring including the Live board and later benchmarking, retention of staff data and the change log and what Newco may see); whether it was sent is to be confirmed by Martin.
- Timing of the heads-up to Yordi and of prod high availability: both were set before real data lands, and since 5 October prod holds the team's own working time. High availability on a dedicated core is in place since 7 October 2026 (Decision log); the heads-up to Yordi is the open part: send it now, before the first Newco agent. A second heads-up, plus the processor view and who-saw-what audit, before the mirror (step 3) and the desk (step 8).
- Tenant picker: needed before anyone works for two tenants (in dev `agent-one` shows the refusal); a screen after login, or a default tenant per person; plus the "all business lines" reporting view for readers across business lines in the Workspace.
- Pulse4all US as the third tenant: region and residency (EU-only folder policy versus a US region, which would mean its own database and project), currency, time zones and languages in configuration; decide when US is set up.
- Matching the same customer across the Subs, Invest and US portals for cohort reporting: purpose limitation and legal basis across business lines, with Yordi, before it is built.
- Aircall accounts per tenant: one webhook endpoint per account with its own secret on the ingest API; per-number settings checklist per account.
- Roles in prod: Martin `manager` until the morning run of 8 October 2026 sets him `admin` with the runbook's Studio statement (migration 0004 brought the role), Finn `supervisor`; Joshua joins prod as `admin` through the People screen when V1 goes live, and Finn stays `supervisor` until then (Martin's decisions of 7 October 2026, see Decision log). The first Newco agent is added as `agent` (employer `newco`) on People once the conditions for real agent hours in Roadmap step 2 are met; before the gate opens to the domain, an agent passes IAP through an IAP-only grant on `cma-web-backend` (`roles/iap.httpsResourceAccessor`), never through `cma@pulse4all.com`.
- A PR check trigger (typecheck, lint, build and the pure verifiers, no deploy) when a second person commits; `web/verify/**` is out of the deploy trigger since 7 October 2026. A build-only trigger for branch images, so dev verifies a web change before prod has it, belongs to the same step.
- Aggregation of agent data older than 1 year (Decision log, 12 October 2026): an addition that keeps per-team, per-market and cohort figures and removes the link to a person from workdays, status intervals, corrections and deliveries older than 12 months; needed before January 2027, when the first imported days (batch `steam-2026`, from 2 January 2026) pass 12 months.
- Copy language of the Workspace for multilingual Newco agents: English and Dutch exist in `web/src/lib/copy.ts`, default English via `CMA_DEFAULT_LOCALE`; per-campaign copy and theme become tenant configuration (from the Subs agent UI); whether locale becomes a per-user setting (with Kira).
- Forgotten clock-outs: since addition 0008b (12 October 2026) the scheduler ends the day at the published planned shift end, else at the tenant's default time (Pulse4all 17:00), within the workday window, after the grace from the window end (Pulse4all 60 minutes), with a notice to the person and a digest to management, and the day stays flagged for a manager (Decision log, 12 October 2026). Still open: a richer rule once Sync runs (last Aircall or HubSpot activity, or the trial's rule using the last disposition), whether a clock-in after the window end (accepted today, it counts nothing) should be refused (the night of 13 October 2026 proposes refusing it at the window end exactly, as the start is refused before the window start, with the message "The workday ended at {time}"; the grace stays the scheduler's alone; design and question in `docs/night-2026-10-13/NIGHT_NOTES.md`, Part 4), and whether the agent may propose an end time from a dialog, stored as a correction awaiting a supervisor's approval (the demo lets the agent enter it, flagged). The four-eyes step for corrections belongs here: in V1 the person correcting is the approver.
- The employee-monitoring point with Yordi now covers the Live board too: live per-person status to supervisors, managers and analytics, besides the Dashboard's per-person time. Whether analytics (Kira, Peter, Bas) should hold `monitoring.live` is part of the ladder review with Arno and Kira; the board checks the permission, so changing that later is a grant, not code. Raised again by the seventh Gemini audit (13 October 2026): agents are live since 12 October, and Yordi's answers of that day cover processing, informing the agents and retention, not explicitly live per-person status; to confirm with Yordi.
- Which hours the agent's screen shows: decided 7 October 2026, working time plus a Pauses column on My hours, own data only (Decision log); the Pauses column is a small web follow-up. The status flags: the flags and the Subscriptions list were decided on 7 October 2026 (Decision log), a change to them is an update on `work_status`, not a migration. Related: which money column the management screens show. Team hours and the Dashboard show paid; with Pulse4all's flags paid equals billable, and Billable may be the word Arno needs. Proposal: a tenant setting (paid or billable, default paid), settled together with the hours columns when the Steam sample is compared.
- Flag changes with history behind them: the clock, the reports and the exports read a status's flags as stored, so changing `is_paid` or `is_billable` on a status that already has stretches would rewrite past pay and billing figures. The configuration screen of 0005a therefore freezes the flags of a status with time behind it (Decision log, confirmed 9 October 2026): retire the old status and add a new one; flags with a validity stay the alternative if daily use shows the need. Pulse4all decided its flags while prod held only the team's own time.
- Pulse4all Invest's own status list, with Peter, at the copy to Invest (step 10); until then Invest has the universal ladder.
- Spanish (ES) leads: a Spanish language skill and who takes them (Arno).
- Newco agents' HubSpot owner mapping: their Google accounts' local part equal to their Newco address or not (decides the `--also-domain` run or one line per agent).
- App links: the exact HubSpot portal and Aircall addresses per tenant replace the generic `app.hubspot.com` and `dashboard.aircall.io` when Martin provides them, now on the App links page (0005a) rather than in `02`; links for Shopify, NetSuite and the shared mailbox follow as those teams use them.
- Shifts that end after midnight: decided 7 October 2026, none at Pulse4all, the business day ends at midnight as built (Decision log). A `business_day_end` tenant setting stays the option for a tenant with night shifts, with the roster (0005).
- Hours CSV columns and Pulse4all's duration format: provisional (see API, Exports). The Steam sample arrived on 12 October 2026 and holds login and logout sessions only (no statuses, no breaks), so it is loaded as working time (addition 0008d) rather than compared column by column. With the sessions in the CMA the hours comparison is now possible: the hours export over the imported period against Steam's own logged-in time per account; then the parallel run below.
- Roster cells: structured start and end plus absence types with the demo's quick-typing parser, built in the night build of 8 October 2026 (Decision log); confirm the entry method and the absence types with Arno on the first real week. Coverage targets per team, work type and weekday are set on Absences and coverage (0005a); shift templates, swaps and requests, and a server-generated PDF file of the week are later.
- The repository is public until launch (Martin, 8 October 2026, Decision log), so a build chat can read `main` without a zip. While it is public, the README and `records/` name staff (roles, emails, Finn's first clock-in): tell Yordi, and strip or keep as he says. New for Yordi since 0005a: the scheduler ends forgotten days as a system actor and the audit records it; the rosters are staff data (8 October). The fourteenth README pass never reached the repository: PR #39 carries the eleventh pass (943 lines) under the fourteenth's title, so the fifteenth pass replaces the file whole.
- Supervisors and the planner: a supervisor holds `roster.view` (own schedule) and `monitoring.live` (the shift line), not `roster.manage`; whether a supervisor should see the team's planner read-only (a `roster.view_team` permission, the night's unbuilt slice 4), or plan their own team, is a grant question for Arno and Kira, not code.
- Slice 4 of the night build of 9 October 2026: My account is done (9 October, addition 0005b, PR #57); next are shift templates per team (`cma.shift_template`, presets `1` to `5` in the planner, migration 0005c) and a supervisor's read-only planner (`roster.view_team`, granted to `manager` and `admin` by the migration; the supervisor grant is one line in the tenant seed once Arno and Kira say yes, see the line above), each its own pull request. An agent's own teams and skills on Welcome or My day (the demo's third line) is now a link to My account if wanted.
- My account and ids in other systems: the page leaves `app_user_external_id` out, because the sign-in id is stored once and never shown again (the People screen's rule). Since the night of 13 October 2026 the CRM and telephony ids are set on People → Linked accounts by whoever may edit the person (Decision log, 13 October 2026); still open: whether My account shows a person's own linked ids read-only, so an agent can see why an alert did or did not reach them, and the owner and user picker that replaces typing (Decision log, 13 October 2026, names what it needs).
- Urgent messages: read and acknowledged are two facts and the urgent pop-up writes acknowledged (built 11 October 2026); the alert rules have their configuration screen since the night of 13 October 2026 (B14). Still open: quiet hours and Spain's right to disconnect (delivery outside working time). Design and question in `docs/night-2026-10-13/NIGHT_NOTES.md` (Part 4): the proposal is that a person who is not clocked in gets no sound and no pop-up, the message waits in the inbox, and an urgent pop-up shows at their next clock-in; people whose time is not kept (managers, admins) are not quieted; a per-person quiet-hours preference and the work-mobile channel come later. Question for Martin, with Yordi on the Spanish rule: is "not clocked in means quiet" the rule, and does a must-acknowledge broadcast wait for the clock-in too?
- Total Calls Today and other productivity figures: whether agents see colleagues' numbers (gamification), with Arno.
- One-click dial through `POST /v1/users/:id/calls`: verify against a live Aircall account that it works with the embedded Workspace rather than only the desktop Phone app, and the plan coverage; otherwise `dial_number` stays two clicks.
- Setting an agent's Aircall availability per user through the API (the status-to-availability mapping of Features 1): endpoint, plan coverage and the custom statuses Aircall allows (Out for lunch, On a break, In training, Back office, Other); if not possible, mismatch display only.
- Cohort definitions for Pulse4all (entry, country, source, proposition, converting agent or team) and the payment-behaviour measures (days to pay, dunning stages, collections) with Kira and Peter; which system holds the Asset Management Database and how it is reached; the Customer App's event feed (webhooks or API) and how its interactions are attributed to an agent when one is involved.
- Benchmarking agents across employers and countries: what agents see of each other, what the partner's HR sees, retention of per-agent performance history (employee-monitoring rules in NL and Spain, with Yordi).
- HubSpot API rate limits for the desk's write-back and live reads at Newco's volume; static versus OAuth auth of the project app per tenant; search-by-phone limits make the mirror's phone index necessary.
- Result-code seeds for Subs (semantics, priorities, retry intervals, do-not-call flags) with Kira; do-not-call scope across business lines (one boolean on the contact today) with Peter and Kira.
- Callback appointments written back to the CRM as tasks: tenant setting, default on or off.
- Dev identities as system `mock` with a subject switch per request for two-user tests: decided for dev; to revisit if Cloud Run's direct IAP integration lets dev run with real Google identities without a load balancer (check when the first real agent is in dev).
- Parallel run against Steam Connect before Steam is switched off for hours: with the import (0008d) both sides can be compared for the same weeks.
- Legacy time import, staged rows of `steam-2026`: `import_session` holds Steam's account names (`external_person_name`, no reader view) for traceability; clear the staged names after Arno's review of the 108 flagged days (the days and events do not depend on the names).
- Legacy time import and the 1-year rule: the identifiable retention of agent data (Decision log, 12 October 2026) reaches the first imported days (2 January 2026) in January 2027, not October 2027; the aggregation must cover imported days like any other, so it is needed before January 2027.
- Legacy time import, the 108 days flagged for review in batch `steam-2026` (closed by the 17:00 rule or clipped to the window): Arno reviews them on Team hours (needs correction).
- The route for the next legacy import instead of Cloud Shell: a Cloud Storage bucket in europe-west4, or the Cloud SQL Auth Proxy from a trusted machine.
- Context-Aware Access and session length on IAP, and 2-Step Verification for the Newco unit (both depend on the Google Workspace edition). 2SV enforcement for the agents' unit to be checked in the Admin console now, the gate is open to the domain since 12 October (fifth audit FIND-06).
- Container vulnerability scanning in Artifact Registry (fifth audit FIND-08): billed per scanned image, one per merge; automatic scanning or an on-demand scan before a release.
- Cloud SQL Data Access logs and pgaudit: scope and cost, decided before more direct readers are added.
- The final name instead of the working title Contactcenter-Management-App, with Mark.
- Build versus buy check: compare the CMA with workforce-management tools that integrate with Aircall (Assembled, Playvox, injixo, Surfboard) and with agent desks on CRM plus telephony, so the choice to build stays checked; HubSpot and Aircall themselves don't cover this layer (their focus is CRM and telephony).
- Business case: confirm the ten targets with Arno, Kira and Peter; the hourly rate per employer and the margin per order for the ROI line come from finance and stay out of the README; who takes and records the baselines, and when the first quarterly review is.
- Assignment tie-breaker and no matching agent: decided 9 October 2026 (Decision log). Still open: a fallback skill or a manager notice when a record waits too long, and the queue engine's "campaign has queued work and no eligible agent" check at campaign creation.
- Routing's language source (S2): the record's market through a market-to-language mapping (Decision log, 9 October 2026) or the contact's own language, now that it is on the contact record; a per-tenant mapping either way.
- Heads-up to Yordi for the intake track: sent 10 October 2026 (`docs/night-2026-10-10/YORDI_HEADS_UP.md`), including Claude querying the reporting views through BigQuery; prod for that waits for his answer. The thin contact record (contact id, country, language, Shopify id) is pseudonymous personal data, and the HubSpot app's contacts scopes can read every contact field even though the ingest requests only those properties.
- Shopify's Email and Phone fields: closed on 14 October 2026 (Decision log): a custom-distribution app gets every customer field automatically, so nothing is selected and no fallback for Email is needed. Shopify withholds the order's own phone, so an order's hash is its customer's phone's (Indirect sales, below).
- Moving the remaining Make scenarios (contact creation from Shopify, quotes) into the CMA, one at a time.
- Aircall's token-in-body authentication (Aircall signs nothing: each webhook request carries the webhook's own token in the JSON body, no HMAC): accepted, with the mitigations built in B9 (13 October 2026; `docs/night-2026-10-10/NIGHT_NOTES.md` §4, DESIGN §6.1 and §6.2): the token compared in constant time (`crypto.timingSafeEqual` on equal-length buffers, another length a failure) with the connection's signing secret before anything but the key lookup touches the database; the opaque connection key in the path as the second factor (an identifier in the URL, never a secret: Decision log, 12 October 2026); HTTPS only (Cloud Run and the load balancer terminate TLS, and `aircall-webhook.mjs` refuses an address that is not `https://…/aircall/<key>`); one webhook per connection, so its token pins a request to one connection (the script refuses a second webhook for the same address); the nightly reconcile watching the webhook (`webhook_id_not_set`, `webhook_missing`, `webhook_token_mismatch`, `webhook_inactive`, `webhook_reactivated`); and the read-back from Aircall's API, so a forged event can only make the CMA read a real call of its own company by id. Still open: asking Aircall for signed webhooks; rotating a token means deleting and creating the webhook (a new token), never by a job.
- Shopify organisations and store plans: the client credentials grant covers only stores in the app's organisation, and some plans withhold protected customer data; checked per store.
- Aircall and Shopify in prod (`docs/ingest/PROD_RUNBOOK.md` phases 8 and 9, 13 October 2026): Aircall's phase needs B9 (`Pulse4all-com/cma#111`) merged and deployed, Shopify's B10 and B10.1 merged, 0008g and 0008h run and the service and jobs deployed; until then only the vendor-side steps run. B9, B10 and B10.1 were merged on 13 October 2026 (`Pulse4all-com/cma#111`, `Pulse4all-com/cma#114`, `Pulse4all-com/cma#115`), and 0008g and 0008h ran in dev and prod the same day (`records/0008g-prod-2026-10-13`, `records/0008h-prod-2026-10-13`). B9 answers the three points that were open for it (Decision log, 13 October 2026): the account id is the company's name (`Pulse4all`), `aircall-webhook.mjs` refuses a second webhook for the same address, and the backfill lists a day at a time at `aircall_max_per_minute`. B10 answered the one open for it (`ingest-outbox` every minute); since B10.1 the job has no schedule, as nothing enqueues a write-back. Not walked through in Pulse4all's accounts: Aircall's API Keys page and the user id in a user's address. Shopify's phase 9 was walked through for the NL store on 14 October 2026 and corrected where it differed (`records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`); the other 13 stores followed the same day, all at once (`PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.6, Several stores at once; same record).
- Indirect sales: an order (Shopify first order, later repeat if Kira decides) is indirect when the person or company was in contact with an agent before it: rules in order (1) the same CRM contact had a lead deal or a call, (2) the same CRM company, (3) the order's phone hash equals an earlier call's; window, counted touches and credit rule as tenant settings (Kira); domain and name matching later. The Self Serve deals are web orders closed as won on creation; once Shopify is connected, orders come from Shopify and the intake tiles count lead pipelines only (Kira). Touches are levelled, in an order Kira sets: conversation (a connected outcome, or answered with at least n seconds of talk, n a tenant setting), quote email, product email (backlog CMA-002), attempt, lead deal only. The credit rule is a tenant setting. First orders are attributed, renewals never, repeat orders if Kira decides. Lifetime value per CRM contact or company sums the linked Shopify customers' amount spent; to decide: currency and Shopify versus NetSuite as the source of value. Sales cycle (contact created to first order) uses a HubSpot property for the original created date of contacts from the old CRM. Retention from renewals is a later KPI. Data quality and lead to order still join through the Shopify id properties and move to the 0008h link with the attribution brief.
- Indirect sales after the NL store's first read (14 October 2026, `records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`): (a) about a third of the NL orders carry no phone hash (339 of 501 orders with one; 911 of 1,874 customers), because the phone sits only in the shipping address, which the adapter never reads; whether to read the shipping address's phone as a fallback when the customer has none, hashed like the others and the address itself dropped, is to decide with Yordi (it widens what the adapter reads, not what it stores). (b) Whether the adapter should stop asking for the fields Shopify withholds (the order's own `phone`), so that Shopify's Dev Dashboard no longer counts those answers as errors (about 51%); the adapter itself already reads a withheld field as empty and fails nothing (Decision log, 14 October 2026).
- Retention of the keyed phone hashes: the commerce hashes of 0008g follow the tenant's retention for call data (1 year), but no retention job exists for call data either (`crm_call` and `telephony_call.counterpart_hash`, `commerce_customer` and `commerce_order.phone_hash`, and the audit log's images of those rows keep them indefinitely today). To build with the aggregation of agent data older than a year (above): clear the hashes older than a year, the audit images included, with Yordi.
- Shopify's `read_all_orders`: not offered when the app was created on 14 October 2026, and the Dev Dashboard has no API access requests page to ask for it (`PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.2); how to get it is open. Without it the API serves the last 60 days of orders, enough for the backfill from 1 October 2026 until about 30 November 2026, after which an older backfill needs the scope. The prior order count follows Shopify's own `numberOfOrders` (whether that counts test orders is not documented).
- Shopify backfill window: customers all (lifetime totals live on the customer); orders the last 13 months; the phone hash only on orders within the 1-year retention; `read_all_orders` if Shopify offers it; Bulk Operations if a store's volume needs it (Kira, Martin).
- Shopify subscriptions are checked once a night: a subscription Shopify deleted after failed deliveries is recreated at the next reconcile, and the 26-hour look-back reads what was missed; a shorter check (the subscription check on its own schedule) if the alerts or intake need the gap closed sooner.
- Lines by hand on the Sources page: a telephony line appears when the adapter reads the line catalog; `set_telephony_number` can add one by its id, the screen does not (no line to type before the catalog exists). Revisit if a line must be configured before its first call.
- Invest's Aircall connection, a later step: its own connection in the Invest tenant on the same Aircall company, with its own webhook (`aircall-webhook.mjs`, its own key and token) and its own lines counted; Aircall sends every call of the company to each webhook, so the Invest connection marks the Subscriptions lines not counted, as Subscriptions does the Invest lines (Decision log, 13 October 2026). A line added later in Aircall arrives counted in every connection until someone switches it off (which then removes what it stored); whether a connection should take new lines as not counted is open.
- A line switched back on gets its earlier calls back only through a read that takes them as new (addition 0008f): the reconcile and the backfill skip a call they listed before with an unchanged fingerprint, also one skipped while its line was off, so the next backfill does not restore them today. A re-read option for the backfill (a run whose events are keyed apart) would; it is an ingest change, left out of 0008f, which changes no adapter.
- The audit images of removed calls (addition 0008f): the deletes of an uncounted line keep ids only, but the earlier insert and update rows of those calls in `cma.audit_log` keep their full images (times, line, user id, keyed hash; the raw payload is filtered); removing those too means rewriting the append-only audit log, so it is left for the audit-retention decision with Yordi. FIND-05 of the fifth audit asks the same; decide with Yordi together with Invest calls in the Subscriptions HubSpot portal, including that stores and business lines are separate legal entities (e.g. Defibswap UK LTD for the UK store).
- Invest calls in the Subscriptions HubSpot portal (addition 0008f): the Aircall–HubSpot integration logs calls on every line of the Aircall company into the Subscriptions portal, so calls on lines that do not count still arrive as CRM calls (`crm_call`) in the Subscriptions tenant; 0008f removes their telephony calls and links but keeps the CRM calls, as HubSpot owns them. In prod on 13 October 2026, all 12 calls on the five Invest lines were linked to such CRM calls. Metrics on CRM calls (attempts, connected rate) still count them, and the call link rate drops by those calls. To decide (Martin, Kira): leave CRM calls as they are and exclude them in reporting by the line of their linked or matched telephony call, or stop the Aircall–HubSpot integration from logging the Invest lines into the Subscriptions portal (an Aircall integration setting per number).
- Aircall tags as a refinement of "connected": a HubSpot call linked to an Aircall call already counts as connected when Aircall reports it answered, but an answered outbound call can still be a voicemail box or a gatekeeper. Option: a per-connection mapping of Aircall tags to meanings (the same shape as `connection_call_outcome`), tenant configuration, never hardcoded. Data prerequisite: agents apply the tags consistently. B9 records tags as tags (no meanings): still to decide, before tags feed "connected".
- An index on `cma.telephony_call (tenant_id, connection_id, number_ref)` with the next telephony migration (fifth audit FIND-01).
- Cloud Armor's rate limits (600 per minute per IP, and 2,400 on `/shopify/` since 13 October 2026) reviewed after the first month of real webhook volume in the prod records and the `cma-ingest 429` alerts.
- HubSpot's call timestamp for calls logged by Aircall: assumed to be the start of the call, verified on the first prod call; the call link supplies the start otherwise.
- Office hours and holidays per market, with Kira (provisional default Mon to Fri 09:00 to 17:00 local).
- Belgium's language (`nl` by default).
- Which forms count as intake, and their markets.
- Renewal source names for Juo: per store at `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.6, read with `shopify-subscribe.mjs --source-names` before the store's backfill. Every store uses Juo; since 14 October 2026 `commerce.renewal_source_names` = `9548333057,subscription_contract_checkout_one`, `commerce.renewal_app_ids` empty (Decision log; the NL pilot's `9548333057` alone corrected the same day). IE, NO, ES, FI, CH and IT had no orders in the lookup's 60 days (no sales yet): their first renewals are checked with 9.8's source-name query once they sell.
- The commerce match outcome: since 0008h (B10.1) the matched contact is stored per customer (the link on `commerce_customer`); no contact and several contacts clear it and are counted in the adapter's log lines (`match_none`, `match_several`, no ids), not stored. The bridge check of `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 9.8 reads the link. Whether to store the reason a customer has no link stays open.
- Data quality (0007d: commerce customers whose id no contact holds, or several hold) and lead to order still join commerce customers to contacts through the contact refs (`crm_contact_ref`, the HubSpot–Shopify app's Contact-Shopify ID properties as the HubSpot connection's `shopify` refs), not through the link of 0008h. Because the CMA holds a contact only while a record points to it, customers without a deal count as unmatched there. Moving those views to the link (or to either) is a later migration; B10.1 changed nothing in 0007b, 0007c or 0007d. In prod on 14 October 2026 (NL store) Data quality counted all 1,874 customers without a contact and lead to order found 0 orders for 189 deals, while the link held 1,830 (`records/intake-prod-2026-10-14`); both stay there until the HubSpot–Shopify app fills Contact-Shopify ID-1/2 or the views move to the link.
- The reporting view `cma_read.commerce_customer` leaves the link of 0008h out (B10.1 changed no 0007b view); adding `crm_connection_id`, `crm_contact_source_id` and `matched_at` to it, for reporting and indirect sales, is a later migration.
- Data prerequisite: the match by id (for a customer without an email, since Shopify no longer withholds Email) and the refs above assume the HubSpot–Shopify app writes the Shopify customer id into the properties the HubSpot connection names as its `shopify` contact refs (Contact-Shopify ID-1 and ID-2 today, `PROD_RUNBOOK.md` 7.3). Which properties the app fills is to be checked on a real contact; if they differ, the refs move to them. Not in place on 14 October 2026: Data quality and lead to order found no NL customer through these properties (the line above).
- The outbox job `ingest-outbox` is deployed without a schedule since B10.1 (`deploy.sh` pauses a schedule an earlier deploy made): nothing enqueues a write-back. Scheduling it again is a `deploy.sh` change that comes with the next action that needs it (0009's owner write-back). Until then the sweep's outbox guard (13 October 2026) warns, and Data quality shows the count, when an action waits ten minutes past its due time.
- The HubSpot app's contacts write scope (`crm.objects.contacts.write`, Decision log, 10 and 11 October 2026) is unused since 0008h; remove it (reinstall in both portals). Dropping it (least privilege) is a change to the `hubspot/` project template, its verifier and a new app version; 0009's owner write-back decides which write scope the app needs next.
- NocoDB visibility of the new intake views (ids only).
- Showing customer data from HubSpot inside the CMA (the offer carries the contact id and opens HubSpot in a new window): belongs to the agent desk (Roadmap step 8) and its heads-up to Yordi, not to the speed-to-lead track.
- Whether an agent who lets a lead expire is automatically set to an "away" status.
- Can one person work for two business lines on the same day, and if so where does the time clock live (today: one user and one clock per tenant).
- Default role ladder and permissions: confirm the seed with Arno and Kira.
- Owner of customer contact details: Shopify or customer portal.
- Shared mailbox: agreement that customer mail goes through HubSpot, which depends on making attachments as easy in HubSpot as in Gmail. Also how to attribute replies to individual agents.
- Choice of realtime push service: managed (Pusher Channels, Ably; check EU data residency and pricing) vs self-hosted (Centrifugo on Cloud Run) vs custom WebSocket service. Decide in Roadmap step 6; polling is the fallback.
- HubSpot subscription check for UI extensions and OAuth.
- NetSuite connectivity (Make module vs. direct API).
- Plan B for agents if the CMA is down (today: HubSpot and Aircall as they work now).
- Source of review data per country and how it links to deals.
- Office hours per country, used for speed-to-lead and SLA calculations.
- Handling of GB vulnerability data in Postgres, in CMA alerts and on the desk's GB records (with Yordi).
- Retention of staff data, the audit log and the customer-field mirror; employee monitoring rules in NL and Spain; quiet hours for notifications (with Yordi).
- Cloud SQL's Knowledge Catalog integration (on by default on both instances, shares schema metadata only, not data): keep or switch off (with Yordi).
- Which Aircall AI features (transcripts, sentiment, playbooks) are in the current Aircall plan.
- Tool for the short satisfaction question after a call or ticket.
